# Plan: Phase 6, reports

**Source:** `docs/PLAN.md` §4.4 (reports) and §6 Phase 6; ADR 0002 (tiers and honesty); five research notes taken on 2026-10-22 for this plan and committed with it as `docs/research/phase6-behaviour.md` (the public report model), `phase6-data-sources.md` (sanctions, PEP and adverse-media sources and their terms), `phase6-matching.md` (screening algorithms, libraries, retrieval and evaluation), `phase6-risk-reports.md` (email, phone, address, crypto and business reports) and `phase6-inventory.md` (the code at `f9349ed`, during Workflow 5A Stage 1b). A fact-check pass re-read 114 load-bearing facts at their primary sources (each note ends with its table). Downloads made during research stay out of git.
**Complexity:** Very large. Two Workflows of ten agents each:
- **6A** opens every layer to reports (Stage 1a `contracts-6`, Stage 1b `foundation-6`), builds list ingestion, the matcher, the synthetic fixtures and the risk logic in parallel beside Stage 1b, then the report engine, the watchlist sync and the provider-backed reports in parallel, then integration;
- **6B** adds the console surfaces and the evaluation report, reviews all of Phase 6 and fixes.

Stage 1b carries plumbing only (F01). Phase 5's Stage 1b did not finish at the pause, and this one has more tables and routes. So:
- every Phase 5 amendment with real logic (the streaming list client, TXT lookups, the RDAP mode for email domains) is built by its only consumer behind an interface Stage 1b declares;
- candidate retrieval and the watchlist loaders, the report index rebuild, the crypto exposure step and the report filter resolution are built by their owners behind Stage 1b stubs (V11);
- Stage 1a freezes the normalised record (F12) and adds the `packages/watchlist` dependencies, so Stage 2 starts right after Stage 1a, beside Stage 1b, and only Stage 3 waits for Stage 1b;
- Stage 1b works in three checkpoints, each committed with every suite green, so a pause resumes from the last one.

The alternative, splitting Stage 1b into two agents, would have meant folding `risk-logic` into `api-providers` to stay at ten.

**Why this split.** The report engine (resources, templates, runs, review, monitoring) is shared by nine types, so Stage 1 freezes it before anyone builds a type. Ingestion and matching are pure TypeScript in `packages/watchlist` and run beside the risk logic in `packages/checks`; the API agents then wire them. The console and the evaluation need every type's API documents and a stable matcher, so they open 6B, followed by the review of the whole phase.

**Phase 5 dependency.** Phase 6 starts only once `docs/PROGRESS.md` records Phase 5 done. It builds on Phase 5's matching library (C58), crypto canonicalisation (C57), libphonenumber rules (C55.8), MX and the disposable list (C56), the RDAP client (C61.4), the outbound HTTP client (C54.6), the provider binding pattern (C63), the repeatable-job registry (C54.1, 5B) and the trust-store sync pattern (C62.7). Stage 1a re-verifies every fact under "Starting point" and records drift as a "Workflow 6A Stage 1a amendment".

**Review of this plan.** Three adversarial reviews read the first draft: privacy and security (P01–P18), honesty and product coverage (H01–H23), feasibility and fit with the code (F01–F21). Together they found no CRITICAL, 16 HIGH, 36 MEDIUM and 10 LOW, 62 in all; `docs/plans/phase-6-review-resolution.md` disposes of each. Among them:
- redaction left the match link, its qualifiers and two blind indexes in plain columns; the append-only trigger planned for report actions would have made every redaction and KEK rewrap fail; the GDELT cache held screened names, unsealed and across tenants; dismissal reasons reached the developer role through API logs;
- a stale source beside a hit had no rule, and staleness counted from the last applied change, so a quiet publisher errored every default report each weekend; an errored completion report left the inquiry `completed`;
- the completion trigger screened the typed name before the verified one; a cross-script miss had nowhere to be reported; identifiers were never retrieved; an NXDOMAIN email could score `high`, and every ordinary US number came back as `phone-type: OTHER`;
- run claims had no fencing, a crashed run stayed `running`, an outage replayed every missed interval, and the completion hook locked the inquiry before the account, the reverse of consolidation;
- Stage 1b was larger than Phase 5's, which did not finish; Stage 1a's event names broke three existing suites; several files had no owner, or two.

The main changes:
- **privacy** (C73, C70.3, C72, C81): match rows keep the entry key as an HMAC and the evidence in the sealed snapshot, and redaction nulls every index; the action trigger allows only the rewrap and the redaction; no article cache; report action bodies are masked in API logs; linking an inquiry or an account needs its scope; per-environment limits, a recurring cap and a fair share of the GDELT budget;
- **honesty** (C69.4, C76.3, C78, C79, C82, C84): a run screens what it can, keeps its matches and errors for what it could not; freshness counts from the last successful check; a `coverage` attribute and `result_truncated`; identifier retrieval; presets as method sets; the research's email and phone rules with confidences; `null` warning lists; a match state machine; completion reports screen the verified data first, and `on-report-match` also covers an errored report;
- **concurrency** (C74, C84.2, C76.2): fenced claims with crash recovery, one catch-up run per report, completion reports that link their account in their first run, and sync guards re-checked under the lock;
- **feasibility** (Workflows, C85, C70.4): Stage 1b keeps plumbing only, Stage 1a freezes the record and keeps every existing suite green, and every file named here has one owner.

No finding was rejected and none deferred. Eight fixes take a different route from the reviewer's proposal, each recorded with its reason in the resolution.

**Verification round.** A fourth pass checked the revision. It confirmed 56 of the 62 dispositions, found six only partly resolved (P09, P11, H09, H17, F01, F05), and raised 14 new findings (V01–V14): 1 CRITICAL, 3 HIGH, 7 MEDIUM and 3 LOW. All twenty are now resolved; none is rejected or deferred. The main fixes:
- the source rows are reconciled from the manifest at startup and an empty source list is refused, so a default production PEP template errors `list_unavailable` instead of answering "no match" (V01; C76.4, C71.2);
- report jobs are removed when they finish and a final failure is fenced, so budget waits, crash recovery and re-screens run again (V02, V05; C74);
- sandbox lists are never stale (V03), and stored keys carry a version, so a matcher upgrade errors until a re-key (V04);
- notify-on-change also covers programmes and PEP positions (V06), and the match state machine gains the three rows it lacked (V07);
- a failed inquiry that is approved still runs its reports (V08);
- the GDELT budget is shared among the environments that wait for it, with a cap on scheduled media runs (V09), and the GDELT query is built from letters and digits only (V10);
- candidate retrieval has one owner, and Stage 2 starts beside Stage 1b (V11, F01).

## Summary

OpenVerify has no reports today. Phase 6 adds the `report/<type>` resource and nine report types, each at its tier (ADR 0002):
- **R:** real logic: list parsing and versioning, normalisation, exact identifier and crypto-address matching, the email, phone and address facts we can compute;
- **H:** a heuristic with a confidence, shipped as `beta`: the fuzzy name score, PEP from Wikidata, adverse media from GDELT, the email and phone risk levels;
- **A:** an adapter with a deterministic sandbox provider: breach exposure, carrier and SIM-swap data, postal verification, crypto exposure analytics, adverse media itself (opt-in).

What lands:
- **The report engine** (C69–C75): resources with `rep_` ids, versioned report templates, statuses, history (runs and actions), tags, dismissal and review of matches with an ignore list that lapses when the evidence changes, continuous monitoring (scheduled runs, pause, resume, run now, re-screen after a list update), redaction, events and scopes.
- **Watchlist ingestion** (C76, C77): OFAC (SDN and Consolidated), the EU Financial Sanctions Files and the FCDO UK Sanctions List enabled by default; the UN list as an opt-in source; an operator-run sync that is never committed, with change signals, monotonicity, a mass-removal guard and staleness; synthetic lists for tests and sandbox environments.
- **The matcher** (C78): name analysis on top of C58, our own ICAO 9303 Latin folding and Cyrillic and Arabic transliteration, `any-ascii` for lossy keys, cross-script variant groups, rare-key, trigram and exact-identifier retrieval in Postgres, a scored alignment with date, country and gender qualifiers, presets defined as method sets, a coverage statement on every result, and provisional thresholds fixed here.
- **Nine types** (C79–C82): `watchlist`, `business-watchlist`, `crypto-address-watchlist`, `politically-exposed-person`, `adverse-media`, `business-adverse-media`, `email-address`, `phone-number`, `address-lookup`.
- **Integration** (C83, C84): five new provider kinds, a report list pinned on inquiry template versions that runs when an inquiry completes, an opt-in "a match sends the inquiry to review", account linkage, consolidation and redaction.
- **Console** (C86): report index and detail, review, monitoring actions, history, the template editor, the watchlist sources page, and the reports sections on inquiries and accounts.

Report honesty, a new ADR (0005), extends ADR 0002 to reports: a report never says "no match" for a list it did not screen or a search it cut short, and it says how completely it compared the subject's names.

## Starting point (inventory at `f9349ed`, during Workflow 5A Stage 1b)

Phase 5 is still landing; facts that Phase 5 changes are marked *(5A)* or *(5B)*. **Stage 1a re-verifies each fact before any change.** Details and line numbers: `docs/research/phase6-inventory.md`.
- **Identifiers.** `<prefix>_<ULID>`; two prefix tables (`packages/contracts/src/ids.ts`, `packages/db/src/ids.ts`); `rep`, `rptp`, `rptpv`, `rpr`, `ract`, `rpm` are free (`rpm` to be confirmed). `anyIdSchema` accepts a new prefix automatically.
- **Events.** Every current name is `<subject>.<action>` without a type (`verification.passed` for all types). The DB format check already accepts one slash (`report/watchlist.matched`, no digits). `eventSubject()` throws for a subject outside its five-entry map; `payloadFitsName` accepts `report/<type>.<action>` for a `report/<type>` payload without a special rule. The publisher validates every name and payload before staging, and there is no in-process subscriber. Webhook filters fall back to the payload's `inquiry` relationship.
- **Scopes and roles.** `API_SCOPES` (20) and `OPERATOR_PERMISSIONS` in `packages/core/src/permissions.ts`, `<resource>:<action>`. The viewer role gets every `:read` scope except `NOT_FOR_VIEWERS`; developer and reviewer are explicit lists; built-in roles are re-synced at API startup; the dashboard's `PERMISSION_INFO` is a typed record that forces a label and an area.
- **Tags.** `add-tag`, `remove-tag`, `set-tags` on inquiries (one event, `inquiry.updated-tags`) and accounts (three events); `applyTagOperation`; the tags column and its GIN index.
- **Templates.** Inquiry templates and versions (draft, published, superseded; one published per template; draft-only updates; publish under the template lock; audit, no event). `templateSettingsSchema` is a strict four-key object, so a report list needs a new version member.
- **Accounts.** `resolveForInquiry`; consolidation moves inquiries and relations; `add-objects` accepts only inquiries; account writes lock accounts in id order, then inquiries; account redaction redacts its inquiries first.
- **Worker.** BullMQ: `process-verification`, `webhook-delivery`, the `inquiry-expiry` repeatable (60 s) and the daily `retention` job; `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` batches; no advisory lock anywhere; enqueue after commit; `PermanentJobError`. `jobs/repeatable/` is planned by 5B.
- **Completion.** `settleInquiry` runs only in the worker and has no queue; `transitionInquiryInTx` (`services/inquiries.ts`) is the one choke point for every inquiry status change and only stages events.
- **Data at rest.** Sealed columns with row AAD, per-environment blind-index subkeys, `REWRAP_TABLES`/`ID_COLUMNS`, the redaction header table, `SUBJECT_PII_TABLES`, `REDACTION_SUBJECT_ID_PREFIXES` (`inq`, `ver`, `iqse`, `acc`, `self`), and `aboutInquiries` for the event timeline. Every data table has `environment_id`; there is no install-wide data table yet (5B plans the trust tables). The newest migration is `0017` *(5A)*; 5B plans `0018`.
- **Postgres.** `pg_trgm` is used nowhere. The pinned `pgvector/pgvector:pg17` image ships `pg_trgm` 1.3, `fuzzystrmatch` and `unaccent` (checked 2026-10-22). PGlite 0.4.6 ships `contrib/pg_trgm`, but `packages/db/src/testing.ts` loads only `vector`.
- **Outbound HTTP** *(5A)*. `OutboundHttp` buffers whole bodies (1 MiB default), returns `{ status, contentType, body }` without headers, follows no redirect, does not decompress or stream. There is no runtime XML, CSV or ZIP parser; `saxes@6.0.0` is in the lockfile through `jsdom` (test only).
- **DNS** *(5A)*. `DnsResolver` has `resolveMx`, `resolve4`, `resolve6`; no `resolveTxt`.
- **Providers** *(5A, 5B)*. Kinds and names in `providers/index.ts`; `PROVIDER_NAMES = ['sandbox','twilio','json-rpc']` (5B adds `none` and four kinds); the worker reads every kind except `sms`.
- **Data files.** `scripts/fetch_data.py` takes only SHA-256-pinned snapshots; `PERMISSIVE_LICENSES` has no identifier for public-domain government works, OGL or the EU reuse notice.
- **Checks.** At `f9349ed` the C58 matching library, crypto canonicalisation, the disposable loader and libphonenumber use are stubs *(5A builds them)*; the RDAP client is *(5B)*. The Phase 1 `normalizeName`, `jaroWinkler` and `tokenSetSimilarity` exist.
- **Dashboard.** App-router folders per resource; server components through `renderPage`; dashboard endpoints for masked documents and `@openverify/node` for the rest; `NAV_GROUPS`; no tab component (inquiry and account pages are stacked sections); a JSON template editor; event groups and words for selfie events only.
- **Settings and seed.** Environment settings through `dashboardSettingsSchema` and `services/settings.ts`; `seed:dev` creates an organization, both environments, the four roles, operators, a sandbox key with every scope and the default template.
- **Records to correct** (research): PLAN §3 names the closed OFSI list and treats UN as public; `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` §4.2 rows for OFAC, UN, EU, UK, Wikidata and GDELT; `persona-surface.md` writes report events with an underscore (`report_watchlist.matched`, the webhook file names) where the event names are `report/watchlist.matched`, and misses 76 report events and two types; PLAN §3 and §6 name `packages/watchlist` (built now) and `packages/signals` (Phase 7); PLAN §6 Phase 0 names `scripts/fetch-watchlists.ts`, which never existed.

**Facts the review relied on** (re-checked at `d797da0`, during Workflow 5A Stage 1b; Stage 1a re-verifies them with the rest):
- **Append-only trigger.** `audit_logs`' trigger refuses every UPDATE except a KEK rewrap that changes only `ip` and `user_agent` (`packages/db/drizzle/0003_schema_v2.sql:484-492`); copied unchanged, it would refuse a report redaction and a rewrap of any other column.
- **Masking.** Stored API logs null `note`, `ip-address`, `user-agent`, `phone-number` and `email-address` at any depth, and the masked attribute names only as direct members of an `attributes` object (`apps/api/src/services/api-logs.ts:202-236`). `api-log.query` is a non-PII attribute (`packages/contracts/src/api-logs.ts:39-40`). Today's collision test walks the verification schemas only. The developer role holds `api-logs:read` and no `pii:view` (`packages/core/src/permissions.ts:103-122`).
- **Webhook filters.** C14 lets a subject without the filtered attribute through (`packages/db/src/repositories/webhooks.ts:385-390`), and `eventFilterSubjects` resolves only an `inquiry` relationship (`apps/api/src/events/subject.ts`).
- **Locks.** `transitionInquiryInTx` updates the inquiry before anything else, and `LifecycleDeps` is `{ events, clock }` (`services/inquiries.ts:105-108, 572-583`). Consolidation locks accounts `FOR UPDATE`, then updates inquiries (`packages/db/src/repositories/accounts.ts:694-747`). Account redaction catches up on inquiries linked while it ran (`services/redaction.ts:283-321`). `resolveForInquiry` share-locks the account but does not check `redacted_at`. Settlement can move an inquiry `completed` then `needs_review` in one transaction (`services/inquiry-settlement.ts`).
- **Samples and clients.** `RESOURCE_FIXTURES` and `STATUS_BY_EVENT` are keyed by `EventSubject`, and every `.redacted` sample sets `fields` (`webhooks/samples.ts:40-98`); `samples.test.ts` builds a sample for every event name. The `eventNameGroups` test fixes five labels (`apps/dashboard/src/lib/api/events.test.ts:106-118`). Android's `ContractFixturesTest` fails on an `api/` fixture with neither a decoder nor a `serverOnly` entry.
- **Jobs.** `RepeatableJob.run()` takes no argument, and each repeatable job has one fixed scheduler (`worker.ts:157-162, 366-388`). Failed job ids are kept 7 days (`jobs/index.ts:22-27`), so re-adding one is a no-op. The verification processor fails its verification before a job fails for good. Default worker concurrency is 4.
- **Outbound HTTP.** The 10 s default deadline covers the whole response, body included (`apps/api/src/http/index.ts:34, 155-186`).
- **Other.** `.dockerignore` re-includes three data files only; `migration-v2.test.ts` opens its own PGlite with `vector` only; the inquiry route's `INCLUDE_PERMISSIONS` is a `Record` over `INQUIRY_INCLUDES`, and the account route has no `include`; `assertPublishable` and `PublishContext` know verification types only; `packages/checks/src/extraction/agreement.ts` keeps the ICAO 9303 §6.A table private, and `normalizeName` folds Ä to A; the API rate limit is 300 per minute per key or session; C16's generic `redacted` problem is a 410.
- **CI and e2e.** One Playwright worker with a 40-minute global timeout; the CI e2e job allows 45 minutes.

**Facts the verification round relied on** (re-checked at `e3a75ee`, read-only):
- **Job ids.** `process-verification` keeps completed jobs a day (at most 10,000) and failed ones 7 days (`apps/api/src/jobs/index.ts:17-27`). BullMQ ignores an `add` under an id it still holds, a completed or failed job included (`jobs/queue.ts:11`). The webhook sweep removes a left-over job before it adds the attempt again (`webhooks/queue.ts:22-29`, `replace`). BullMQ ids may not contain `:` (`webhooks/queue.ts:41`).
- **Inquiry transitions.** `failed` may move to `approved`, `declined` or `needs_review` (`packages/core/src/inquiry-state-machine.ts:36-38`). The operator actions reach `transitionInquiryInTx` through `applyInquiryAction` (`services/inquiries.ts:621`).
- **Startup sync.** The API re-syncs every organization's built-in roles when it starts (`container.ts:285`, `routes/dashboard/admin.ts:36`).
- **Index rebuilds.** Each table's rebuild lives in its owner's repository (`rebuildCryptoAddressIndexes`, `repositories/crypto-challenges.ts:196-201`), and `apps/api/scripts/blind-index-rebuild.ts` calls each one.
- **Worker failure.** `processVerificationProcessor` fails its verification on the last attempt or a permanent failure, without any claim to fence (`worker.ts:173-200`).

**How the gaps are resolved:**

| Gap (inventory §16) | Resolution |
|---|---|
| Three event-name conventions | `report/<type>.<action>`, the public event names, which the DB already accepts; one `report` subject (C70.4) |
| No install-wide data table | Install-wide watchlist tables and a `watchlist_syncs` audit table; sandbox lists filtered in SQL by environment kind (C73.3, C76.6) |
| `fetch_data.py` cannot pin daily lists | An operator-run sync with verification instead of pinning; ADR 0005 interprets rule 4 for live public-sector data (C76, C87) |
| The outbound client cannot carry list files | A streaming variant with response headers, per-client caps and one allowlisted redirect (C85.1) |
| `pg_trgm` breaks PGlite tests | The migration creates it; `testing.ts` loads the PGlite contrib (C73.1) |
| Migration number depends on merge order | The next free number after 5B's (expected `0019`) |
| No completion hook, no queue in settlement | Reports created in the completion transaction and run by a sweep: an outbox (C84.2) |
| PII lists are verification-scoped | `PII_REPORT_ATTRIBUTES`, report redaction, `rep` in every redaction list (C70.3, C73.5) |
| Accounts know only inquiries | Report links in a join table, a consolidation move rule and the lock order (C84.4) |
| Viewer inherits every `:read` | `reports:read` stays viewer-visible with PII masked; `watchlists:read` too; review, write and redact are not (C72.2) |
| No tab component | Reports sections on the inquiry and account pages (C86) |
| The audit trigger refuses every update | A report-action trigger with two stated exceptions: the rewrap and the redaction (C73.2) |
| C14 filters pass a subject without the attribute | Strict filters for report subjects only (C70.4) |
| Completion runs while the inquiry row is locked | Completion reports link only the inquiry; their first run links the account in lock order (C84.2) |
| Stage 1a's names break the samples, the event groups and Android | Those three land in the same commit (C70.4) |

## Patterns to mirror

| Category | Source | Pattern |
|---|---|---|
| State machine | `packages/core/src/verification-state-machine.ts` | Statuses `as const`, a transition table, an event per status |
| Registry with tiers | `packages/core/src/check-registry.ts`, `check-config/<area>.ts` | One entry per type; defaults as `ConfigDefault { value, basis, source }` |
| Resource contracts | `packages/contracts/src/verifications.ts`, `json-api.ts`, `resource-types.ts`, `identifiers.ts` | Zod attributes with `.meta({ pii: true })`, builders, golden fixtures |
| Versioned templates | `packages/db/src/tables/inquiry-templates.ts`, `repositories/inquiry-templates.ts`, `routes/server/inquiry-template(-version)s.ts` | Draft, published, superseded; one published; draft-only update; publish under the template lock |
| Tags | `routes/server/inquiries.ts:271-332`, `repositories/tags.ts` | Three routes, `applyTagOperation`, the tags column and index |
| Events | `packages/contracts/src/event-names.ts`, `events.ts`, `apps/api/src/events/index.ts`, `webhooks/samples.ts` | Stage in the transaction, dispatch after commit; a golden resource per subject |
| Repeatable jobs | `worker.ts` `RepeatableJob`, `jobs/inquiry-expiry.ts`, `repositories/retention.ts` | A sweep with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` batches; a CLI for one pass |
| Operator-run sync | `docs/plans/phase-5.plan.md` C62.7 (trust store, 5B) | A committed source manifest, verification before swap, monotonicity, withdrawal, an audit table, a refresh job behind a flag, never in CI |
| PII at rest | `packages/db/src/sealed.ts`, `blind-index.ts`, `repositories/key-rotation.ts`, `repositories/redaction.ts` | Sealed with row AAD; HMAC under the environment subkey; rewrap and redaction lists |
| Masking | `apps/api/src/auth/pii.ts`, `services/api-logs.ts` | Lists built from the contracts' PII marks; a test per exit |
| Append-only rows | `audit_logs`' trigger (`packages/db/drizzle/0003_schema_v2.sql:484-492`) | Refuse updates except stated cases, each checking every other column unchanged |
| Job failure and redaction catch-up | `worker.ts` `processVerificationProcessor`; `services/redaction.ts` (`redactAccount`) | Fail the row before a job fails for good; after a redaction, catch up on rows linked while it ran |
| A-tier | `apps/api/src/providers/index.ts`, `providers/sandbox.ts`, `docs/checks/sandbox-providers.md` | A port, a real adapter through the outbound client, a deterministic sandbox, refused in production |
| Tests | Vitest colocated; Testcontainers; Playwright | RED commit first; ≥ 80% per package; `docs/tdd/phase6-<agent>.tdd.md` |

## Locked contracts

C1–C68 stay locked except where a section below amends them. Stated amendments of earlier contracts: C54.6 (streaming, headers, a per-source deadline and one allowlisted redirect for list clients, C85.1), the C56 `MxResolver`/`DnsResolver` port (`resolveTxt`, C85.2), C61.4 (an RDAP mode for email domains, C85.3), C63 (five provider kinds, C83), C16 (one setting, `report-retention-days`, C73.6), C12 (a `reports` policy keyed by environment, C72.5), C14 (strict filters for report subjects, C70.4), C15 and C53.7 (the report and report-action PII attributes, and the collision test over every resource, C70.3), the inquiry template version (a `report-config` member, C84.1), C40.6 (the lock order gains reports, C73.7), the account resource (report links and an `include`, C84.4, C70.5).

### C69. Report types, statuses and honesty

1. **Types** (`REPORT_TYPES` in `packages/core/src/report-registry.ts`; one entry each with tier, stage, recurrable, review model, query schema and the sources or provider kinds it needs; a test asserts every entry has a query schema, a result schema, a golden fixture, a dashboard label and a docs page):

   | Type | Resource type | Tier | Stage | Recurrable | Review | Needs |
   |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
   | `watchlist` | `report/watchlist` | R lists, H score | `beta` | yes | matches | sanctions sources |
   | `business-watchlist` | `report/business-watchlist` | R lists and identifiers, H score | `beta` | yes | matches | sanctions sources |
   | `crypto-address-watchlist` | `report/crypto-address-watchlist` | R | `ga`-able (exact) | yes | matches | sanctions sources with crypto addresses |
   | `politically-exposed-person` | `report/politically-exposed-person` | H | `beta` | yes | matches | a `pep` source (`wikidata-pep`; the sandbox PEP list in sandbox) |
   | `adverse-media` | `report/adverse-media` | H, A | `beta` | yes | matches | `adverseMedia` provider |
   | `business-adverse-media` | `report/business-adverse-media` | H, A | `beta` | yes | matches | `adverseMedia` provider |
   | `email-address` | `report/email-address` | R subset, H level, A extras | `beta` | no | none | DNS; `emailIntelligence` optional |
   | `phone-number` | `report/phone-number` | R subset, H level, A extras | `beta` | no | none | `phoneIntelligence` optional |
   | `address-lookup` | `report/address-lookup` | R subset, A verification | `beta` | no | none | `addressVerification` optional |

   `crypto-address-watchlist` is `ga` once the test vectors of every row of the C79.3 format table pass; every other type stays `beta` until a calibration report exists (ADR 0002 §3f).
2. **Statuses** (`REPORT_STATUSES`; `packages/core/src/report-state-machine.ts`): `pending`, `submitted` (reserved for parity; no Phase 6 path uses it, decision 3), `ready`, `errored`. Transitions: `pending → ready | errored`; a monitored report moves `ready → ready | errored` and `errored → ready | errored` on each run, and never back to `pending`. A report's status is its latest completed run's. Status is about processing, never about the result; `has-match` carries the result, and reads as "no match" only beside `ready` (4). Event per status: `ready` → `report/<type>.ready`, `errored` → `report/<type>.errored`.
3. **Errors.** An errored report or run carries `errors: [{ code, source? }]` with codes `insufficient_input`, `list_unavailable`, `list_stale`, `result_truncated` (C78.5, C81.2), `matcher_data_unavailable` (C78.3), `provider_not_configured`, `provider_error`, `execution_error`. No PII, no provider body, no URL.
4. **Report honesty** (ADR 0005, a new ADR written in Stage 1a):
   - **partial runs** (H01): a run screens every selected source it can, persists the matches it finds and fires `.matched` for them. When any selected source was not screened (never synced, unconfigured, disabled, withdrawn, keyed by another matcher version (C78.3), or stale by C76.3), the run ends `errored` with `list_unavailable`, `list_stale` or `matcher_data_unavailable` naming each such source, whatever it found. A matching run whose selection is empty ends `errored` with `list_unavailable` and no source: a backstop, since C71.2 refuses an empty list (V01). A report is never `ready` while a source its latest run selected went unscreened, and dismissing or reviewing its matches leaves it `errored`. `filter[has-match]=false` returns only `ready` reports, and the console shows "not screened" for an errored one;
   - a type whose whole answer needs a provider (`adverse-media`, `business-adverse-media`) is `errored` with `provider_not_configured` when none is bound;
   - a partial type (`email-address`, `phone-number`, `address-lookup`) is `ready` with every field it could compute; a field that needs an unbound provider, or that the rule cannot decide, is `null` and listed in `unavailable-fields` with its reason, never `false`;
   - **coverage** (H05, H12): every matching run states how completely it compared the subject's names and whether the search was cut short (C78.5); a cut-short search that found no match ends `errored` with `result_truncated`;
   - every result carries provenance: each source used with its sync id, publication time, last check time, SHA-256 and `origin` (`publisher` or `import`, C76.2), the source of each query field (C84.2), the matcher version, the preset and thresholds, the provider identity (`sandbox: true` in sandbox);
   - a sandbox provider or a sandbox-only list is never used for a production environment (C71.2, C76.6, C83);
   - a match never changes an inquiry or account on its own, except through the opt-in of C84.3.

   **Tests** (H20; unit or Testcontainers, one per rule): a never-synced, a disabled and a stale source each give `errored` with its code and the source id; a production PEP template created before any sync, with `OPENVERIFY_WIKIDATA_USER_AGENT` unset, gives `errored` with `list_unavailable` naming `wikidata-pep` (V01); a run with an empty selection gives `list_unavailable`; a stale EU source beside an OFAC hit gives `errored` with the match kept and `.matched` fired, and dismissing that match keeps the report `errored`; `filter[has-match]=false` leaves errored reports out; each partial type without its provider lists its `unavailable-fields`; media without a provider gives `provider_not_configured`; binding `gdelt-doc` without the acknowledgement stops the worker naming the variable; a Han-only subject with no hit is `ready` with a limited coverage; a search at its cap with no match gives `result_truncated`; a production template cannot select a sandbox list.
5. **Parity choices** (H22; each item of the research gap table, `docs/research/phase6-behaviour.md`, recorded):

   | Public behaviour (gap-table items) | Phase 6 choice |
   |---|---|
   | G1, G2, G8–G10, G13: `report/<type>` resources, `rep_` ids, statuses, relationships, tags, history, event names | Matched; `transaction` served as null until Phase 7; `submitted` reserved (decision 3) |
   | G3: no error codes documented | Ours: `errors[]` with codes (3) and the honesty rules (4) |
   | G4: templates without versions or an API | Ours: versioned templates and their API (decision 26) |
   | G5: create body with `account-id`, `meta.auto-create-account*` (default true), `processing-mode` | Matched (C72.1); `reference-id` refused; `inquiry-id` ours |
   | G6, G7, G12: workflow, CSV bulk and print | Phase 8 and Phase 9; the inquiry-template list is ours (C84) |
   | G11: redaction; dismissal refused once redacted | Matched (410); our `.redacted` event |
   | M1–M3, M5: recurrence of 1–365 days, run, pause and resume, the 30-minute delay | Matched; pause and resume use `reports:write` |
   | M4: legacy types stop on a hit | Ours by default (continue); `pause-on-match` for parity (decision 5) |
   | M6: sources checked as often as every 10 minutes | An operator-run sync or the 15-minute refresh tick (C76.2), with freshness and staleness (C76.3); the re-screen after a list update is ours (C74.4) |
   | M7: `is-continuous` and `is-recurring` defined inconsistently | Defined by us (C70.2) |
   | V1: dismissal body under `data.attributes` | Matched; `entity-id` on every type with review is ours |
   | V2: the per-report `ignore-list` | Matched; the suppression key is ours (C75.3) |
   | V3, V4: screening review and `.reviewed`; a notify-on-change record returns to `pending` when its data changes | Matched in content for every type with review; the details in `payload.meta` (C70.4). "Its data" is ours, defined: the matched identity (the evidence hash) or its risk data (programmes, listing date, PEP positions and relations; C77.3, C75.8; V06) |
   | V5: `report.read`, `report.write` | Split: `reports:read`, `:write`, `:review`, `:redact` and the template scopes (C72.2) |
   | V6: no organisation-wide ignore list | None (decision 6) |
   | T1: watchlist query and configuration | Matched (`address-country-code`, presets as method sets, C79.1) |
   | T2: `matched-lists[]`, no score | Matched with `flag-code`, `list-types[]` and list-level `match-types`; `score`, levels and `coverage` ours |
   | T3: PEP classes, toggles and result shape | Classes matched, with heads of international organisations in class 2 (ours, C80.2); `result[]`, `positions[]`, `associates[]` and `sources` matched; `topics`, `images` and `related-urls` not served; candidates and portrait comparison not in Phase 6; "sources without URLs" moot (every record has its Wikidata URL) |
   | T4: media from a 300-million-article archive with entity resolution | GDELT titles over 90 days in four lexicon languages, with a coverage notice (C81) |
   | T5: crypto from OFAC only, no dismissal | OFAC and the UK list; dismissal ours |
   | T6: email signals; deprecated fields null since 2023; `age-estimated-*` | R subset and adapters (C82.1); `email-is-free-provider` filled (informational, a divergence); `age-estimated-*` null (no documented method) |
   | T7: phone type, carrier, risk and SIM swap | Numbering plan, the research rule and adapters (C82.2); `phone-type` null when the plan cannot tell |
   | T8: address lookup | R subset and the Smarty adapter (C82.3) |
   | T9: business watchlist, default Strict | Matched (`strict`, `address-country-code`); the warning and fitness-and-probity lists `null` (decision 18) |
   | T10: the screening composite | Later (C88) |
   | X1–X5: workflows, cases, dashboard, sandbox inputs, Connect | Dashboard core matched (C86); synthetic sandbox values (decision 14); the rest later (C88) |

### C70. The report resource

1. **Ids** (both prefix tables): `report: 'rep'`, `reportTemplate: 'rptp'`, `reportTemplateVersion: 'rptpv'`, `reportRun: 'rpr'`, `reportAction: 'ract'`, `reportMatch: 'rpm'` (ours: a match's `record-token`).
2. **Shared attributes** (every type; parity names from the public schema plus ours, marked): `status`, `created-at`, `completed-at`, `redacted-at`, `report-template-version-name` (`v<N>`), `has-match` (at least one match whose status is `open`, C75.8; always `false` for types without review; "no match" only beside `ready`, C69.4), `is-continuous` (ours, defined: more than one completed run), `is-recurring` (ours, defined: a next run is scheduled and not paused), `tags`, and ours: `query` (PII, the inputs as sent, masked member-wise), `origin` (`inquiry_completion`, `api`, `dashboard`; P05), `errors`, `unavailable-fields`, `coverage` (C78.5: `complete` and `limits[]` from a closed set; no names or scripts), `next-run-at`, `paused-at`, `paused-reason` (`operator` | `match`), `sources` (provenance per source: `source-id`, `sync-id`, `published-at`, `checked-at`, `sha256`, `origin`), `query-sources` (the source of each query field, C84.2), `matcher-version`. Relationships: `inquiry`, `account` (the home account), `accounts` (every linked account; expanding needs `accounts:read`), `report-template`, `report-template-version` (ours), and `transaction` (always null until Phase 7, as the public schema serves it).
3. **Masking** (C53.7 extended). Each report attribute schema marks PII with `.meta({ pii: true })`: `query` (masked member-wise, as `fields`), `matched-lists`, `result` (PEP), `ignore-list`, `matches`, `articles`, `positions`, and every type-specific value derived from the subject, `email-domain` and `email-domain-typo-suggestion` included (a vanity domain names its owner, P15; the console reads the non-PII `email-domain-kind` instead). The dismiss and review bodies' attributes `reason`, `comment` and `entity-id`, and the history entries' `reason` and `comment`, are marked too (P04). `PII_REPORT_ATTRIBUTES` (contracts) is built from these marks and consumed by `MASKED_ATTRIBUTES` (`auth/pii.ts`) and the API-log masked attribute set (`services/api-logs.ts`). Because the create, dismiss and review bodies carry their members under `data.attributes` (C72.1), the stored request bodies of those routes are masked by the same set, so the developer role (`api-logs:read`, no `pii:view`) never reads a reviewer's free text or the entry a subject resembled. The C53.7 collision test walks every resource schema in contracts, not only verifications, and records one exemption with its reason: `api-log.query` is never passed to `mask.attributes()`, and `maskPersonalData` masks its members by name, so the report keeps the public name `query` (P17). Tests per exit: resource, list, `included` on inquiries and accounts, event payload, the stored API-log request and response bodies of create, dismiss and review, dashboard document.
4. **Events.**
   - Names: `report/<type>.ready`, `.errored`, `.matched`, `.dismissed`, `.reviewed` (ours for every type with review; public for `screening` only), `.redacted` (ours). `.submitted` is reserved and not registered.
   - Definitions: `.matched` fires on a run that creates a new open match, re-opens one, or returns a reviewed one to `pending` under notify-on-change (C75.8), whether or not `has-match` was already true; `.dismissed` once per dismiss action, with the dismissed match ids in `meta`; `.reviewed` once per review action, with `review-type`, `review-status`, `risk-level`, `record-token` and `reviewer` (`usr_` or `key_` id) in `meta`; `.redacted` once, on the first redaction.
   - `EventSubject` gains `report`; `eventSubject` maps every `report/<type>` to it; `PREFIX_BY_SUBJECT_TYPE`, the redacted stub enum and the payload `data` union follow. Tags emit no report event (parity).
   - **Samples and clients** (F09). `RESOURCE_FIXTURES` and `STATUS_BY_EVENT` are keyed by the payload's resource type for report events (`report/<type>` → `report-<type>.json`), and a report's `.redacted` sample nulls the report's PII attributes instead of setting `fields`. Stage 1a lands this, the dashboard "Report" event group with its `eventNameGroups` test, and Android `serverOnly` entries for every new `contracts/fixtures/api/report-*` fixture in the same commit as the names and fixtures, so the API, dashboard and Android suites stay green for every later agent. 6B adds only the event words.
   - **Webhook filters** (F10). C14 lets a subject without the filtered attribute through; for reports that would send a standalone report to a webhook filtered to one inquiry template. So report subjects are strict: `eventFilterSubjects` resolves a report's `inquiry` relationship to its template and its `account` relationship to the account's `account_type_id` (both scoped to the environment), and a report subject without the attribute fails a present filter. Every other subject keeps C14's rule. Stage 1b builds the strict switch and the call in `apps/api/src/events/subject.ts`, `packages/core/src/webhook-payload.ts` and `packages/db/src/repositories/webhooks.ts`. `api-reports` implements the resolution behind it, in `services/reports/filter-subjects.ts` (`resolveReportFilterSubjects`; F01). Tests: a report linked to an inquiry, to an account, to both and to neither, against each filter; a verification event without an inquiry still passes a template filter.
5. **Includes** (F08). `GET /inquiries/:id?include=reports` (needs `reports:read`: the `INCLUDE_PERMISSIONS` entry lands with the include name in Stage 1a) and `GET /accounts/:id?include=reports` (a new `include` query on the account route, frozen in Stage 1b) list the reports linked to the inquiry, or to the account as home or through `report_accounts`, newest first, at most 50; the dashboard inquiry and account documents list them too. All of them call `includedReportsFor(tx, environmentId, { inquiryId } | { accountId }, mask)` in `services/reports/` (a Stage 1b stub, implemented by `api-reports`), with a masking test per exit.

### C71. Report templates and versions

1. **Tables** mirror inquiry templates: `report_templates` (`rptp_`, `environment_id`, `name`, `report_type`, `status` `active` | `archived`) and `report_template_versions` (`rptpv_`, `version` ≥ 1, `config` jsonb, `status` `draft` | `published` | `superseded`, `published_at`, `created_at`; one published version per template; draft-only updates). Creating a template publishes version 1; publishing a draft supersedes the published version under the template row lock; both write an audit entry (`report-template-version.published`) and no event. A report pins the version it was created from; a template change applies to new reports only, and a monitored report keeps its version. Archiving a template refuses new API reports from it (422 `report_template_not_published`); completion entries that cite it still run from its published version (C84.1).
2. **Configuration.** `reportTemplateConfigSchema(type)` (contracts; strict, kebab-case) with the per-type keys of C79–C82 and these shared keys: `recurrence-interval-days` (1–365 or null; default null), `pause-on-match` (false), `resume-delay-minutes` (0–1,440; 30), `max-list-age-hours` (1–720; per type: 48 for the sanctions types, 192 for `politically-exposed-person`, its source's weekly refresh plus a day; H02, F02). Recurrence is refused for non-recurrable types. Defaults live in `REPORT_CONFIG_DEFAULTS` as `ConfigDefault { value, basis, source }`; every value is provisional; the resolved configuration is served.
   - **Source lists are pinned** (H09, P13). An omitted `search-lists` is resolved into explicit source ids when the template or the version is created, and the resolved list is stored and served: in a sandbox environment, every enabled source of the type's kind plus the sandbox list of that kind; in production, every enabled source of that kind that is not `sandbox_only`. "Enabled" is what `watchlist_sources.enabled` records then. C76.4 derives it from the manifest and `OPENVERIFY_WATCHLIST_SOURCES_DISABLED` alone, at every startup, so the rows exist before any template does. A source that still lacks its token or User-Agent is enabled but unconfigured: it is selected, and its reports error `list_unavailable` until it syncs (V01). A selected source disabled later errors `list_unavailable` (C69.4); it never silently leaves the list.
   - **Publish-time rules** (422 with JSON Pointers): a source id the manifest does not know (`watchlist_source_unknown`); a `sandbox_only` source in a production environment (`watchlist_source_not_allowed`); an empty `search-lists`, resolved or explicit (`watchlist_sources_empty`, V01: an empty list would screen nothing and answer "no match"); a recurrence on a non-recurrable type; `max-list-age-hours` below a selected source's refresh interval.
3. **Defaults shipped** (F11). `seed:dev` creates one published template per type in each environment (`Default watchlist`, …) through `apps/api/src/seed/report-templates.ts` (owned by `api-reports`, called from `dev-seed.ts`), with every default and explicit source lists:
   - the sandbox templates select only the sandbox lists (`openverify-sandbox`, `openverify-sandbox-pep`), which the seed imports, so a fresh stack answers `ready` without any download;
   - the production templates select the enabled real sources and answer `list_unavailable` until the operator syncs, which is the honest answer;
   - the adverse-media templates answer `provider_not_configured` until a provider is bound.

   `seed:dev` reconciles the source rows first (C76.4). A template whose resolved list would be empty (every source of its kind disabled by the operator) is skipped and counted in the seed's output, never created. A test publishes every seeded template in both environment kinds with the default configuration; another shows the production PEP template pins `wikidata-pep` before any sync.

### C72. API routes, scopes and problems

1. **Server routes** (plugin-registered, zod schemas, `problemResponses`; bodies follow the public shapes in `docs/research/phase6-behaviour.md` §1):
   - `POST /api/v1/reports` (`{ data: { type: 'report/<type>', attributes: { 'report-template-id', query, 'account-id'?, 'inquiry-id'? (ours) } }, meta?: { 'auto-create-account'? (default true, as the public body), 'auto-create-account-type-id'?, 'auto-create-account-reference-id'?, 'processing-mode'?: 'async' | 'sync' } }`; the deprecated `reference-id` is refused; H22): validates the query against the type, pins the published version, links the account (the given one, else the one with `auto-create-account-reference-id`, else a new one unless `auto-create-account` is false), creates the report `pending` with `origin: api` (`dashboard` from the console) and its first run, and enqueues it after commit. Creation follows the lock order of C73.7 and answers 410 `redacted` (C16's problem) when the linked inquiry or account is redacted (P06). `sync` waits up to 10 s (polling the row, holding no lock) and answers with the current state; at most 20 requests per environment wait at once, and a request beyond that answers at once, as `async` does (P09). `async` (default) answers at once. Idempotency keys apply as everywhere.
   - `GET /api/v1/reports` (filters `filter[report-template-id]`, `[status]`, `[has-match]` (`false` returns only `ready` reports, C69.4), `[account-id]`, `[inquiry-id]`, `[reference-id]` (the account's), `[tag]`, `[type]`; cursor pagination), `GET /api/v1/reports/:id`, `DELETE /api/v1/reports/:id` (redact; C73.5).
   - `POST /api/v1/reports/:id/add-tag`, `/remove-tag`, `/set-tags` (the shared tag bodies).
   - `POST /api/v1/reports/:id/dismiss`, `/review` (C75; bodies under `data.attributes`, as the public endpoints).
   - `POST /api/v1/reports/:id/pause`, `/resume`, `/run` (C74).
   - `GET /api/v1/reports/:id/history` (runs and actions, newest first, paginated).
   - `POST|GET /api/v1/report-templates`, `GET|PATCH /api/v1/report-templates/:id` (name, status); `POST|GET /api/v1/report-template-versions`, `GET|PATCH /api/v1/report-template-versions/:id` (draft config), `POST /api/v1/report-template-versions/:id/publish`.
   - `print` is not registered until Phase 9.
2. **Scopes** (core `API_SCOPES`, additive): `reports:read`, `reports:write` (create, tags, pause, resume, run), `reports:review` (dismiss, review), `reports:redact`, `report-templates:read`, `report-templates:write`, `report-templates:publish`, `watchlists:read` (the sources status). Roles: viewer gets `reports:read`, `report-templates:read` and `watchlists:read` automatically (PII masked: viewers hold no `pii:view`); developer gets all but `reports:review` and `reports:redact`; reviewer gets `reports:read`, `reports:review`, `reports:write`, `reports:redact`, `report-templates:read`, `watchlists:read`; admin everything. The dashboard gains a "Reports" permission area. **Linking needs the linked resource's scope as well** (P05, V14):
   - `inquiry-id` needs `inquiries:write`, because a linked report joins the inquiry's document, its includes and its redaction (C70.5, C73.5). It cannot move the inquiry: only completion reports do (C84.3);
   - `account-id` needs `accounts:read`;
   - `auto-create-account-reference-id` needs `accounts:read` whenever it is present, whether or not an account with that reference id exists. A check made only when it resolves would tell a key without `accounts:read`, by 403 against 201, whether such an account exists;
   - a new account without a reference id needs nothing more, as it holds nothing.

   A test per refusal, and one that a key without `accounts:read` gets the same 403 for a known and an unknown reference id.
3. **Dashboard routes:** `GET /api/v1/dashboard/reports`, `GET /api/v1/dashboard/reports/:id` (masked documents with runs, matches and actions included), `GET /api/v1/dashboard/watchlist-sources` (`watchlists:read`; sources, enabled, configured (from `last_error_code`), whether the source's `keys_version` is the worker's, a pending re-screen, the scheduled media runs per day of the install against its GDELT budget (C81.2), last check, last applied sync, publication time, SHA-256, `origin`, entry and name counts, staleness, last error code, the sandbox lists labelled, and the templates whose pinned lists hold a disabled source or leave out an enabled one, C76.4).
4. **Problem codes** (frozen in Stage 1a with golden fixtures under `contracts/fixtures/problems/reports/`): `report_query_invalid` (422, pointers), `report_template_not_published` (422: unknown, archived or never published; also the inquiry template's `report-config` publish rule, at `/data/attributes/report-config/on-completion/<i>`, F07), `report_type_mismatch` (422: the template is another type), `report_not_reviewable` (409: pending, or errored with no match row), `report_not_recurrable` (409), `report_no_scheduled_run` (409: pause needs an upcoming run; resume needs a paused report), `report_match_not_found` (404 within the report), `report_match_superseded` (409: a `record` review of a dismissed match whose entry already has an `open` or `delisted` row, C75.8; V07), `report_redacted` (410: every route that changes a redacted report, H23), `report_recurring_limit` (422: the environment holds its cap of recurring reports, or of scheduled media runs per day, 5), `watchlist_source_unknown` (422, publish), `watchlist_source_not_allowed` (422, publish: a sandbox-only source in a production environment), `watchlist_sources_empty` (422, create and publish: an empty `search-lists`, C71.2; V01).
5. **Rate limits and caps** (P09; provisional, ours; a C12 amendment):
   - a `reports` policy keyed by environment (`RATE_LIMIT_POLICIES` gains it in Stage 1a): 600 creations and `run` calls per minute per environment (`OPENVERIFY_RATE_LIMIT_REPORTS_PER_MINUTE`), beside the per-key `api` policy, which a developer can multiply by minting keys; 429 with `Retry-After` beyond it;
   - at most 20 waiting `sync` requests per environment (1);
   - at most 250,000 recurring reports per environment (`OPENVERIFY_REPORTS_MAX_RECURRING_PER_ENVIRONMENT`): creating a recurring report beyond it answers 422 `report_recurring_limit`; a completion report beyond it runs once without a schedule, and its run records `recurring_limit` so the console shows that monitoring did not start;
   - the GDELT budget is shared among the environments waiting for it, and one environment that waits alone gets all of it (C81.2; V09);
   - at most `OPENVERIFY_REPORTS_MAX_MEDIA_RUNS_PER_DAY` scheduled media runs per day per environment (default 8,640, half the install budget of 17,280 requests a day; provisional; V09). They are counted as the sum of 1 / `recurrence_interval_days` over the environment's recurring `adverse-media` and `business-adverse-media` reports. A recurring media report beyond the cap answers 422 `report_recurring_limit`; a completion one runs once without a schedule and records `recurring_limit`, as for the recurring cap.

   Tests: the limit per environment, both caps, one environment alone reaching the install budget, and one environment over its media share not erroring another environment's media run.

### C73. Storage, encryption, redaction and locks

1. **Migration** (the next free number after 5B's, expected `0019`; hand-written statements where drizzle-kit would recreate a type): `CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS pg_trgm`; the C71 template tables; the tables below; the `report_actions` trigger (2); enums `report_status`, `report_run_type`, `report_action_type`, `report_match_status`, `report_review_status`, `report_risk_level`, `report_origin`. `packages/db/src/testing.ts` exports `PGLITE_EXTENSIONS` (`{ vector, pg_trgm }`, the latter from `@electric-sql/pglite/contrib/pg_trgm`), used by both its PGlite instances and by `migration-v2.test.ts`, which opens its own and runs every migration (F16). A migration test asserts the environment keys and composite FKs of every environment-scoped Phase 6 table (as C53.2) and lists the install-wide ones.
2. **Environment-scoped tables** (composite FKs to `(environment_id, id)` of their parents, like `selfie_captures`):
   - `reports` (`rep_`): `report_type`, `report_template_version_id`, `origin` (`inquiry_completion`, `api`, `dashboard`), `status`, `query` (sealed JSON), `query_fields` (text[], names only), `query_sources` (jsonb: query field name → source kind, C84.2), `result` (sealed JSON: the non-match outputs of email, phone and address types, and the per-name coverage detail of C78.5), `coverage` (jsonb: the C78.5 summary, no names or scripts), `has_match`, `errors` (jsonb, codes only), `unavailable_fields` (text[]), `inquiry_id` (nullable), `account_id` (home, nullable), `recurrence_interval_days` (copied from the pinned version at creation, null when not recurring; the version never changes, and the media cap of C72.5 sums it; V09), `next_run_at`, `paused_at`, `paused_reason`, `tags`, `crypto_address_index` (blind index, label `openverify/report-crypto-address/v1`, crypto type only, written by the run), `created_at`, `completed_at`, `redacted_at`; indexes `(environment_id, created_at desc, id desc)`, `(next_run_at) where next_run_at is not null and paused_at is null and redacted_at is null`, `(environment_id, report_type) where next_run_at is not null` (the recurring counts of C72.5), `(environment_id, inquiry_id)`, `(environment_id, account_id)`, the tags GIN index;
   - `report_accounts`: `(environment_id, report_id, account_id)` primary key, `created_at`;
   - `report_runs` (`rpr_`): `report_id`, `run_type` (`initial`, `scheduled`, `manual`, `list_update` (ours)), `status` (`pending`, `running`, `ready`, `errored`), `scheduled_for`, `not_before` (a budget or retry wait), `enqueued_at`, `attempt` (claims made, C74.1), `claimed_by` (the claim token), `claimed_until`, `trigger_sync_id` (the sync a `list_update` run screens; unique `(report_id, trigger_sync_id)` where not null, so a repeated re-screen inserts each run once; V05), `started_at`, `completed_at`, `match_count`, `new_match_count`, `sources` (jsonb provenance, non-PII), `coverage` (jsonb), `errors`, `notes` (codes only: `inquiry_already_decided`, `report_match`, `report_errored`, `recurring_limit`, `evidence_changed`, `risk_data_changed`, `match_not_found_in_run`);
   - `report_actions` (`ract_`): `report_id`, `action_type` (`dismiss`, `review`, `pause`, `resume`, `run`, `redact`), `actor_type` (`user`, `api_key`, `system`), `actor_id`, `details` (sealed JSON: reason, comment, the affected match ids), `created_at`. A trigger (`openverify_report_actions_append_only`) refuses UPDATE except in two cases, each with every other column unchanged (P02, F03): with `openverify.kek_rewrap = 'on'`, a new ciphertext of `details` (the rewrap); with a transaction-local `openverify.report_redaction = 'on'`, set only by the redaction repository, `details` from non-null to null. It refuses DELETE unless a transaction-local `openverify.environment_deletion = 'on'` is set: reports are never deleted (C73.6), and no production path deletes an environment (only `packages/db/src/constraints.test.ts` does, in a cascade test). Tests: each allowed case; a refused edit of `action_type` and of `actor_id`; a refused DELETE; the redaction of a report with one action of each type; an inquiry redaction that cascades through such a report; `report_actions` in the `kek:rewrap` test;
   - `report_matches` (`rpm_`): `report_id`, `source_id`, `entry_index` (32 bytes: HMAC under the environment's blind-index subkey, label `openverify/report-match-entry/v1`, over `<source>:<publisher id>` or the article key; P01), `evidence_hash` (32 bytes, HMAC, C75.3), `score`, `snapshot` (sealed JSON: the readable entry key, the entry's content hash and risk-data hash at match time (C77.3), the matched list names, dates, countries, identifiers, positions or article fields, and the components: the matched name pair's levels and methods, the qualifiers, the preset and thresholds), `status` (`open`, `dismissed`, `delisted`), `review_status` (`pending`, `indeterminate`, `true_positive`, `false_positive`), `risk_level` (`material`, `immaterial`, null), `notify_on_change`, `present_in_last_run`, `first_run_id`, `last_run_id`, `lapsed_from` (the match it re-opened), `delisted_at`, `created_at`, `updated_at`; an index on `(report_id, entry_index, evidence_hash)`, not unique: an entry that returns to content dismissed before gives two rows with one hash, and two dismissals of one evidence are two history rows (V07); and at most one `open` or `delisted` row per `(report_id, entry_index)` (a partial unique index, which C75.8 keeps). A readable entry key, plain qualifiers or a plain content hash would join to the public list and name the person the subject resembles (with its date of birth and passport number), so only `source_id`, `score` and the statuses stay plain.
3. **Install-wide tables** (no `environment_id`; list data is public and not sealed; personal data all the same, so C76.8 limits its retention):
   - `watchlist_sources` (`id` text from the manifest, `enabled` and `sandbox_only` (both written only by the reconciliation of C76.4), `keys_version` (C78.3), `last_sync_id`, `last_published_at`, `last_sha256`, `last_applied_at`, `last_checked_at` (C76.3), `last_origin` (`publisher`, `import`), `last_error_code` (`source_not_configured` among them, C76.2));
   - `watchlist_syncs` (audit: `id`, `source_id`, `started_at`, `finished_at`, `outcome` (`applied`, `unchanged`, `rekeyed`, `busy`, `refused_rollback`, `refused_future`, `refused_mass_removal`, `refused_schema`, `refused_size`, `refused_matcher_data`, `error`), `published_at`, `sha256`, `entry_count`, `added`, `changed`, `removed`, `actor` (`cli`, `job`, `seed`), `origin` (`publisher`, `import`), `override` (`allow-mass-removal`, `accept-older-publication`, `allow-normalised`, or null), `keys_version`, `rescreen_state` (`none`, `pending`, `done`; C74.4) and `rescreen_enqueued_at`);
   - `watchlist_entries` (`source_id`, `entry_id` (the publisher's), primary key both; `kind` (`individual`, `entity`, `vessel`, `aircraft`), `content_hash` (32 bytes, C77.3), `data` (jsonb, the normalised record of C77.1), `programs` (text[]), `listed_at`, `first_seen_sync_id`, `last_seen_sync_id`, `changed_sync_id` (the sync that added the entry or last changed its content or risk-data hash; indexed with `source_id`; V05), `removed_at`);
   - `watchlist_names` (`source_id`, `entry_id`, `ordinal`, `name`, `normalised`, `script`, `quality` (`strong`, `weak`), `primary`, `keys` text[] (C78.3) with a GIN index, `normalised` with a `gin_trgm_ops` GIN index);
   - `watchlist_identifiers` (`source_id`, `entry_id`, `kind` (C77.1), `value_normalised`, index on `(kind, value_normalised)`);
   - `watchlist_crypto_addresses` (`source_id`, `entry_id`, `chain` (CAIP-2 or `unknown`), `canonical`, `raw`, `ticker`; index on `canonical`);
   - `watchlist_key_df` (`source_id`, `key`, `count`), rebuilt with each applied sync.
4. **Sealing and keys.** Sealed columns use `sealValue`/`sealJson` with the row AAD. Stage 1b adds `reports.query`, `reports.result`, `report_actions.details` and `report_matches.snapshot` to `ENCRYPTED_COLUMNS`, `SEALED`, the schema-v2 list and `REWRAP_TABLES` (a `kek:rewrap` test per table). The match entry index (`openverify/report-match-entry/v1`) and the evidence hashes (`openverify/report-match-evidence/v1`) are new blind-index labels. `blind-index:rebuild` recomputes `crypto_address_index`, `entry_index` and `evidence_hash` through `rebuildReportIndexes`. As Phase 5's `rebuildCryptoAddressIndexes` lives in its owner's repository, `api-reports` implements it in its repositories; Stage 1b adds only the stub and the script's call (F01). It works from stored values only: the sealed query and the sealed snapshot, which holds the readable entry key and the entry's content hash at match time, so a rebuild never reads today's list (P10, H18, F13). Redacted rows are skipped: their indexes are null. Test: dismiss a match, rotate the blind-index key and rebuild, change the entry in a new list version, re-run: the dismissed match lapses and `.matched` fires.
5. **Redaction.** `DELETE /reports/:id` (`reports:redact`) nulls `query`, `result` and `crypto_address_index`, every match's `snapshot`, `entry_index` and `evidence_hash`, and every action's `details` (the trigger's redaction case); sets `redacted_at`; cancels monitoring (`next_run_at` null; a claimed run re-checks `redacted_at` under the lock and discards its result); keeps `source_id`, scores, statuses and counts; stubs the report's earlier events; writes an audit entry with counts; and stages `report/<type>.redacted` once with the redacted resource. Every route that changes the report answers 410 `report_redacted` afterwards. A test scans every column of every report table after redaction for the fixture entry id, its date of birth, the subject's name and the HMACs (P01). Inquiry redaction redacts every report whose `inquiry_id` is that inquiry; account redaction redacts every report linked to the account (home or `report_accounts`), and, as for inquiries, catches up after its transaction on reports linked while it ran (F04). `rep` joins `REDACTION_SUBJECT_ID_PREFIXES`; the inquiry's and account's stub lists include their reports; `aboutInquiries` includes the inquiry's reports; `SUBJECT_PII_TABLES` and the coverage test seed one row of each report table; the `redaction.ts` header table gains its rows.
6. **Retention** (P14). A new environment setting `report-retention-days` (1–3,650 or null; default null, keeping reports as long as inquiries are kept by default), served and updated through the C16 settings route and enforced by redaction, never deletion: Stage 1b adds a report step to `services/retention.ts` and `listReportsDueForRetention` to `repositories/retention.ts` (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` batches; implemented as a cross-agent read), which calls the report redaction hook `redactReportInTx` (implemented by `api-reports`). Reports created by an inquiry also follow the inquiry's redaction. Testcontainers: a report past `report-retention-days` is redacted; with the setting null it is kept. `docs/reports/README.md` tells operators to set it.
7. **Locks** (C40.6 extended): accounts in id order (share-locked through `lockAbsorbingAccount` when only linked, so a consolidation in flight is waited for), then the inquiry (re-checking `redacted_at`), then the report (re-checking `redacted_at`), then its runs and matches. Writers: API creation (C72.1, P06), the completion hook (inquiry only, C84.2), the initial run's account link (C84.2), run completion, review, dismiss, pause, resume, run, redaction, retention, consolidation, the sweep's claim. The completion hook takes no account lock: it runs inside an inquiry transition that already holds the inquiry row, and consolidation locks accounts before inquiries (F04). A Postgres race test per pair named in C74.6.
8. **Database logs** (P18). Retrieval binds the subject's name tokens and normalised name as query parameters (C78.3), and Postgres logs bind parameters in full by default when statement or duration logging is on. Compose and the shared Testcontainers helper start Postgres with `log_parameter_max_length=0` and `log_parameter_max_length_on_error=0`; an `infra/tests` check reads the compose command; `docs/reports/README.md` says that statement logging with parameters would write screened names to the server log, outside redaction and retention.

### C74. Runs, history and monitoring

1. **Runs** (P11, F05, V02). Every execution is a `report_runs` row: `initial` at creation, `scheduled` from the sweep, `manual` from `POST /run`, `list_update` from a re-screen (4).
   - **Jobs.** The `report-run` job id is the run id. Its jobs are removed when they complete or fail (`removeOnComplete: true`, `removeOnFail: true`), unlike `process-verification`'s. The run row is the record. BullMQ ignores an `add` under an id it still holds (`jobs/queue.ts:11`), so a kept job would turn every later re-add of a budget wait or a crashed run into a no-op for a day. A waiting, delayed or active job still dedupes a second add.
   - **Claim.** A worker claims a run with one conditional update that matches `status = 'pending'` with `not_before` null or past, or `status = 'running'` with `claimed_until` past (a crashed worker's run), and sets `status = 'running'`, `claimed_by` to a fresh token (worker id and attempt), `claimed_until` to now + 10 min and `attempt + 1`. A run aborts itself after 5 minutes, so a live claim does not expire under it in normal operation.
   - **Nothing to claim.** A job whose run is finished, redacted, held by another unexpired token, or waiting for a future `not_before` returns successfully and writes nothing. It never throws for this, so it never reaches the final-failure path. The sweep enqueues the run again once it is claimable.
   - **Fenced completion.** The run completes with `UPDATE … WHERE id = $run AND claimed_by = $token AND status = 'running'` under the report lock (C73.7). A lost claim discards the whole result: no match, event, transition or schedule change is written.
   - **Retryable errors.** A job that throws after its claim first releases it (`status = 'pending'`, claim cleared, `WHERE claimed_by = $token`) and rethrows, so BullMQ's next attempt can claim. A release that fails leaves the claim to expire.
   - **Final failure.** On the last BullMQ attempt, or a `PermanentJobError`, the processor marks the run `errored` with `execution_error`, fenced as completion is (`WHERE claimed_by = $token AND status = 'running'`, under the report lock). A report on its initial run becomes `errored` and emits `report/<type>.errored`. A job that never claimed writes nothing, and the sweep enqueues the run again. A claim that would be the run's eleventh ends the run `errored` with `execution_error` instead of running it, so a run that kills its worker cannot loop. This mirrors the verification processor (`worker.ts:173-200`), with the fence added because two jobs can hold one run over time.
   - **Waits.** A run that cannot take a provider budget token goes back to `pending` with `not_before` set, releases its claim without counting it (`attempt − 1`, so waits never reach the eleventh-claim cap) and clears `enqueued_at`. The sweep then enqueues it on its first tick after `not_before` (C81.2).
   - **Tests** (Testcontainers):
     - a claim expires while the first worker still runs, and exactly one completion lands;
     - a worker killed after its claim is recovered once `claimed_until` passes, although its BullMQ job was re-delivered and completed;
     - a budget-waiting run runs again within 15 s of `not_before`;
     - an exhausted job for a run that a live worker holds leaves that run `running`, and that worker's completion lands;
     - an exhausted job ends its own run and the report `errored`;
     - an eleventh claim ends the run `errored`, and twenty budget waits do not.
2. **History** (`GET /reports/:id/history`): runs (`rpr_`, `run-type`, `status`, `scheduled-for`, `completed-at`, `match-count`, `new-match-count`, `notes`) and actions (`ract_`, `action-type`, `actor` as a `usr_` or `key_` id or `system`, `created-at`; `reason` and `comment` are PII attributes, shown only with `pii:view`), merged newest first.
3. **The sweep** (`report-sweep`, a repeatable job every 15 s in the worker, registered in `jobs/repeatable/index.ts`), in batches with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`:
   - (a) enqueues every claimable run (as 1) whose `enqueued_at` is null or older than 5 minutes, oldest `scheduled_for` first, and sets `enqueued_at`. This is the outbox of C84.2 and the recovery path for a lost enqueue, without re-adding tens of thousands of jobs every 15 s after a large list update (F05);
   - (b) for every recurring report with `next_run_at ≤ now`, not paused, not redacted and without a `pending` or `running` scheduled run, inserts **one** `scheduled` run, and moves `next_run_at` to the first interval boundary after now, counted from the previous `next_run_at`. So an outage of several intervals gives one catch-up run per report, and the schedule does not drift (F06);
   - (c) calls `recoverPendingRescreens` (`jobs/reports/rescreen.ts`; a Stage 1b stub implemented by `api-watchlists`). It enqueues `report-rescreen` again for every applied sync whose `rescreen_state` is still `pending` and whose `rescreen_enqueued_at` is null or older than 15 minutes (V05).

   Tests with a frozen clock: the three paths; a restart in the middle; a gap of several intervals (one run, and `next_run_at` in the future); a re-screen whose enqueue was lost when its sync committed is enqueued by the next sweep.
4. **Re-screen after a list update** (`report-rescreen`: a queue with its worker in `worker.ts`, not a repeatable job; F17, V05).
   - **Record.** The apply transaction of a sync that added or changed entries sets the sync's `rescreen_state` to `pending` (C76.2); a sync that only removed entries sets `none`.
   - **Enqueue.** After commit, the sync CLI, the refresh job and `seed:dev` each enqueue one job per sync, with the job id `rescreen-<sync id>` (BullMQ ids may not contain `:`), through a producer over their own Valkey connection. Jobs are removed when they complete or fail, as in 1. While the state stays `pending`, the sweep enqueues the job again (3 (c)), so a re-screen lost to a Valkey outage or a crash after the commit still runs.
   - **Work.** The job takes in turn each environment that holds recurring reports of a type selecting the source. It builds the reverse index of those reports' decrypted subjects (C78.6, through `listRecurringReportSubjects`, a Stage 1b cross-agent read) in chunks of at most 50,000 subjects (P09). It screens the names, identifiers and crypto addresses of the entries whose `changed_sync_id` is the sync against each chunk; an entry changed again by a later sync is screened by that sync's job. It inserts a `list_update` run with `trigger_sync_id` for each report with a candidate above its threshold, through `insertListUpdateRuns` (a Stage 1b cross-agent write; F11). The insert does nothing on a conflict over `(report_id, trigger_sync_id)`, so a job that runs twice inserts each run once. After the last environment, it sets `rescreen_state = 'done'`.
   - Removed entries mark the reports' open matches `delisted` on their next run. Paused reports are not re-screened; resume catches them up (5). Nothing about the subjects is persisted. Budget: ≤ 60 s per environment per sync at 200,000 recurring reports (measured by `evaluation-6`).
   - **Tests** (Testcontainers): with Valkey down when a sync commits, the next sweep after Valkey returns re-screens it and a `list_update` run appears; a job killed half-way and run again inserts each run once; a sync that only removes entries enqueues nothing.
5. **Pause, resume, run.** `pause` (needs `is-recurring`; sets `paused_at`, `paused_reason: operator`, keeps `next_run_at` for display), `resume` (needs `paused_at`; when any selected source applied a change since `paused_at`, a `scheduled` run is inserted at once, so designations added during the pause are screened now, not at a boundary up to a year away; otherwise `next_run_at` becomes the next interval boundary after now; H19), `run` (recurrable types only; inserts a `manual` run; allowed while paused). Each writes an action. With `pause-on-match: true`, a run that brings a new open match pauses the report with `paused_reason: match`; once no match is `open` or every open one is reviewed, the report resumes after `resume-delay-minutes` (the next run is then due). The default keeps monitoring through a hit (decision 5). Test: a designation imported during a pause is screened at resume.
6. **Race tests** (Postgres, Testcontainers): run completion against dismissal, against redaction and against pause; the sweep's claim against a manual run; two sweeps against one due report; re-screen against redaction; consolidation against run completion; API creation against inquiry redaction and against account redaction (P06); completion-hook creation against consolidation and against account redaction, and the initial run's account link against consolidation (F04); a claim that expires during a live run (P11).

### C75. Match review

1. **One engine for every type with review.** A match row holds the screening review fields of the public schema; a legacy dismissal is a review with `review-status: false_positive` and a required reason.
2. **Dismiss** (`POST /reports/:id/dismiss`, `reports:review`): `{ data: { attributes: { reason (1–500), 'dismiss-type'?: 'entity' | 'media', 'entity-id'? } } }` (the public body; H22). `entity-id` is the readable entry key of a match, as the report serves it. Without `entity-id`, every open match is dismissed; with it, the open matches of that entry (for media, `dismiss-type: media` targets one article, `entity` every article of the report). Dismissed matches leave `has-match`, appear in `ignore-list` (`ignored-at`, the run that found them, the snapshot, the actor, `ignore-reason`), and are suppressed on later runs while their evidence hash is unchanged.
3. **Evidence key.** `evidence_hash = HMAC-SHA-256(blind-index subkey of the environment, label openverify/report-match-evidence/v1, canonical JSON of [entry key, the entry's content hash (C77.3), the matched list name, the report's screened query fields in a fixed order])`. A JSON array frames each part, so no two splits of name and query give one input (P10). The subject part is keyed because subject names have low entropy (C73.4). The content hash at match time is kept only in the sealed snapshot (C73.2). So is the entry's risk-data hash (C77.3), which notify-on-change compares (8). It never enters the evidence hash, so a new programme or position never lapses a dismissal of the identity (V06).
4. **Lapse and re-open.** A report's query never changes (no update route; every run reuses the inputs it was created with), so a changed evidence hash always means the list entry changed (H20, F20). The transitions are in 8: a dismissed match lapses into a new `open` one with `lapsed_from` and `.matched`; a reviewed match with `notify-on-change: true` returns to `pending` and fires `.matched` when its evidence or its risk data changes (a new programme, a new PEP position; V06), and with `false` the change is recorded in the run only; a match whose entry left its source becomes `delisted`, leaves `has-match`, stays visible with `delisted_at`, and is never auto-dismissed.
5. **Review** (`POST /reports/:id/review`, `reports:review`): `{ data: { attributes: { 'review-type': 'all_records' | 'record', 'record-token'? (an `rpm_` id), 'review-status': 'pending' | 'indeterminate' | 'true_positive' | 'false_positive', 'risk-level'?: 'material' | 'immaterial', comment? (≤ 1,000), 'notify-on-change'? } } }`. `false_positive` needs a comment (it is a dismissal). `all_records` applies to `open` matches only. Each review writes an action and fires `.reviewed`; a `false_positive` review also fires `.dismissed`.
6. **Refusals.** Both routes answer 409 `report_not_reviewable` on a pending report or an errored report with no match row (an errored report that kept matches, C69.4, can be reviewed), 410 `report_redacted` on a redacted one, 404 `report_match_not_found` for an unknown token or entity id, 409 `report_match_superseded` for a review of a dismissed match that a later row of its entry replaced (8); neither touches a type without review (409).
7. **No organisation-wide ignore list** in Phase 6 (decision 6).
8. **Match state machine** (H17, V07; `packages/core/src/report-match-state-machine.ts`, one unit test per row). For each entry a run finds, the entry's `open` or `delisted` row decides when there is one; dismissed rows are consulted only when there is none. A match is closed only by a person or by its entry leaving the source, never by a run that stops finding it.

   | Match now | Event | Result |
   |---|---|---|
   | none open or delisted for the entry | a run finds it above the preset's minimum | a new `open`, `pending` match; `.matched` |
   | `open`, any review | the same evidence and risk data | unchanged; `present_in_last_run` true |
   | `open`, any review | the entry still listed, but this run does not find it above the minimum (an alias removed, a birthdate corrected) | stays `open` with `present_in_last_run` false; the run records `match_not_found_in_run`; still counts in `has-match` until a person dismisses it; never auto-closed |
   | `open`, `pending` | a changed evidence hash | the row's `evidence_hash` and `snapshot` are updated in place (no second row); the run records `evidence_changed`; no event, as it already alerted |
   | `open`, `indeterminate` or `true_positive` | a changed evidence hash | updated in place; with `notify-on-change` true, back to `pending` and `.matched`; with false, recorded in the run only |
   | `open`, any review | a changed evidence hash equal to a dismissed row's of the same entry (the entry reverted to content dismissed before) | updated in place, as any change (the hash index is not unique, C73.2); stays `open`: only a person dismisses |
   | `open`, `pending` | the same evidence, a changed risk-data hash | the snapshot updated in place; the run records `risk_data_changed`; no event |
   | `open`, `indeterminate` or `true_positive` | the same evidence, a changed risk-data hash (V06) | the snapshot updated in place; with `notify-on-change` true, back to `pending` and `.matched`; with false, `risk_data_changed` in the run only |
   | `open` | a dismissal, or a `false_positive` review | `dismissed`, `false_positive`; into `ignore-list`; `.dismissed` (and `.reviewed` for a review) |
   | `open` | any other review | the review fields set; stays `open`; `.reviewed` |
   | `dismissed` | the same evidence, whatever the risk data | stays suppressed; recorded in the run; a programme or position change never lapses a dismissal |
   | `dismissed` | a changed evidence hash | lapses: a new `open`, `pending` match with `lapsed_from`; `.matched` |
   | `dismissed` | a `record` review other than `false_positive` (a mistaken dismissal corrected), while the entry has no `open` or `delisted` row | re-opened: `open` with that review; leaves `ignore-list`; counts in `has-match`; `.reviewed` |
   | `dismissed` | the same review, while the entry already has an `open` or `delisted` row (a lapse or a delisting since) | 409 `report_match_superseded`; nothing changes; the reviewer reviews that row |
