   | `open` | its entry removed from the source | `delisted`, `delisted_at` set; leaves `has-match`; never auto-dismissed |
   | `dismissed` | its entry removed | stays `dismissed`; `delisted_at` set |
   | `delisted` | the entry listed again | `open` with its earlier review; `.matched` |
   | `delisted` | a `record` review | the review recorded; stays `delisted` |

   `has-match` is "some match is `open`". The console marks an open match that the last run did not find.

### C76. Watchlist sources and sync

1. **Manifest** (`watchlists/sources.json`, committed, validated by a schema test): per source `id`, `publisher`, `kind` (`sanctions`, `pep`), `urls` (one per file), `redirect_host` (exact host or null), `content_types`, `max_bytes` (compressed and inflated), `timeout_seconds` (the whole request's deadline, C85.1; F18), `parser`, `change_signal`, `refresh_interval_hours`, `stale_after_hours`, `enabled_by_default`, `sandbox_only`, `terms` (a note and its URL), `attribution` (the line shown with results). OFSI's closed list is absent, and a test refuses any OFSI URL. Sources (intervals provisional, from the research's cadence notes; H02, F02):

   | Source | Format | Default | Refresh | Stale after | Timeout |
   |---|---|---|---|---|---|
   | `ofac-sdn`, `ofac-consolidated` | enhanced XML | enabled | 6 h | 48 h | 300 s |
   | `eu-fsf` | XML 1.1 | enabled; syncs once `OPENVERIFY_EU_FSF_TOKEN` is set (decision 11; C76.4) | 6 h | 48 h | 300 s |
   | `uk-sanctions-list` | XML | enabled | 6 h | 48 h | 300 s |
   | `un-sc` | XML | disabled (decision 1) | 24 h | 72 h | 300 s |
   | `wikidata-pep` | SPARQL | enabled; syncs once `OPENVERIFY_WIKIDATA_USER_AGENT` is set (C80; C76.4) | 168 h (a full pass) | 192 h | 75 s per query |
   | `openverify-sandbox` (`sanctions`), `openverify-sandbox-pep` (`pep`) | normalised JSON | `sandbox_only: true` | none (imported by `seed:dev`) | never (C76.3): static synthetic data | — |
2. **Sync** (`pnpm --filter @openverify/api watchlists:sync [--source <id>] [--import <file> --source <id>] [--allow-mass-removal] [--accept-older-publication] [--allow-normalised] [--json]`, an operator-run CLI). The `watchlists-refresh` repeatable job runs the same code only with `OPENVERIFY_WATCHLIST_REFRESH=true`: a tick every 15 minutes syncs each enabled source whose `refresh_interval_hours` has passed since its last check (F17). Never in CI. Each run first reconciles the source rows (C76.4). A source that is not configured (no EU token, no Wikidata User-Agent) is not synced: the CLI ends with `error`, the refresh tick skips it without writing a sync row, and both set `last_error_code: source_not_configured`, without any request. A sync or import that cannot find the filtered variant-group file, or finds another SHA-256, is refused before any change (`refused_matcher_data`, C78.3). A source whose `keys_version` differs from the process's is first re-keyed from its stored records, as `watchlists:rekey` does (C78.3), so an unchanged publication still leaves current keys. Per source:
   1. take a session-level `pg_try_advisory_lock` for the source on a dedicated connection, which runs every later transaction of the sync and holds the lock until it ends (advisory locks are re-entrant within a session); when another sync holds it, stop with `busy` (P07);
   2. check the change signal (C77.4) and stop with `unchanged` when nothing moved;
   3. download through the list client (C85.1), within the source's `timeout_seconds`, into a new private directory (`mkdtemp`, mode 0700, the file 0600; stale directories removed at start): HTTPS, the vetted lookup, at most one redirect to the exact `redirect_host`, the content-type allowlist and the byte caps; the `Location` of a redirect is never logged or stored;
   4. hash and parse the same open file descriptor; record the SHA-256; stop with `unchanged` when equal to the last applied;
   5. parse under the guards of C77.2 and validate every record against the normalised schema; any failure refuses the sync (`refused_schema`) and keeps the previous data; a publication time later than now plus 24 h is refused (`refused_future`; P08);
   6. apply in one transaction under a per-source `pg_advisory_xact_lock` and `FOR UPDATE` of the `watchlist_sources` row, re-checking there, against the locked row, both guards (P07): refuse a publication older than the last applied one (`refused_rollback`) unless `--accept-older-publication` (the CLI only; recorded as an `override`), and refuse removal of more than 10% of the source's entries (or all, for a source with fewer than 100) unless `--allow-mass-removal` (`refused_mass_removal`). Then upsert entries by `(source_id, entry_id)` with their content and risk-data hashes. Replace their names, identifiers and crypto addresses when the content hash changed, with the keys of the process's `keys_version`. Set `changed_sync_id` on added entries and on entries whose content or risk-data hash changed. Set `removed_at` on entries absent from the publication. Rebuild the source's key frequencies, update `watchlist_sources` (with `keys_version`), and write `watchlist_syncs` with `rescreen_state` `pending` when anything was added or changed, else `none` (C74.4);
   7. after commit, enqueue `report-rescreen` (C74.4) for a sync left `pending`; the sweep recovers a lost enqueue (C74.3 (c)).

   **Checks.** Every sync whose change-signal check or download succeeded, `applied` or `unchanged`, sets `last_checked_at` (C76.3) in a short transaction under the same locks; `last_applied_at` and `last_published_at` stay provenance.
   **Imports** (P08). `--import` reads a local file with the same checks and records `origin: import` in `watchlist_syncs`, on the source row and in every report's `sources` provenance, which the sources page shows, so a report never cites a publisher for data the publisher did not publish. A file in the source's own format is accepted for any source; the normalised JSON of C77.5 only for the sandbox sources, or with `--allow-normalised` (recorded as an `override`). An import makes no request, so it needs no token or User-Agent. Imports are how tests, the e2e and air-gapped operators load lists.
   **Tests** (Testcontainers): two apply transactions with the older publication committing last end in `refused_rollback`; two partial removals that together pass 10% end in `refused_mass_removal`; a second sync while one runs ends `busy`; a future publication is refused; `--accept-older-publication` recovers from a bad import and is audited; a sync without the group file is refused with `refused_matcher_data`; an unconfigured source records `source_not_configured` and sends nothing; an unchanged publication after a keys-version change re-keys every entry.
3. **Staleness** (H02, F02). A source is stale when now minus its `last_checked_at` exceeds its `stale_after_hours` or the template's `max-list-age-hours`, whichever is smaller; a source never checked is unavailable. Freshness therefore follows the last successful check, not the publisher's last change: a quiet publisher (OFAC over a weekend, an EU file a week old) stays fresh while checks succeed, and failed checks turn it stale. A report selecting a stale source errors with `list_stale` (C69.4); the sources page shows it. The docs tell operators to enable the refresh job, or to schedule the CLI more often than the stale limit. **A `sandbox_only` source is never stale** (V03): its data is static and synthetic and is never refreshed, so neither its `stale_after_hours` nor a template's `max-list-age-hours` applies to it. One never imported is unavailable. Tests: an OFAC Friday-to-Monday gap with daily `unchanged` checks stays `ready`; checks failing for longer than the limit give `list_stale`; with a frozen clock, a seeded sandbox watchlist report run 72 h after `seed:dev` is `ready`, and so is a sandbox PEP report 9 days after.
4. **Enablement and withdrawal** (V01, H09).
   - **Reconciliation.** `reconcileWatchlistSources` (`apps/api/src/watchlists/reconcile.ts`, owned by `api-watchlists`) runs at API startup, at worker startup, and first in each CLI (`watchlists:sync`, `watchlists:rekey`, `pep:sync`, `seed:dev`). Stage 1b calls it from `container.ts` and `worker.ts`, as the API re-syncs the built-in roles at startup (`container.ts:285`). It upserts one `watchlist_sources` row per manifest source, with `sandbox_only` from the manifest and `enabled` = `enabled_by_default` and not listed in `OPENVERIFY_WATCHLIST_SOURCES_DISABLED` (comma-separated ids). A source the manifest no longer lists keeps its row, disabled, for history. So the rows exist before any template, and nothing else writes `enabled`. Every process that reads the variable parses it the same way (a config test), and compose gives it to every service that runs the API, the worker or the CLIs.
   - **Configured is a separate state.** A source without its token or User-Agent stays enabled and selected, never syncs, and reports selecting it error `list_unavailable` until it does. The sources page shows "not configured" from `last_error_code`.
   - **Withdrawal.** Disabling a source in the environment's configuration is not offered in Phase 6. After an operator disables a source install-wide, reports selecting it error with `list_unavailable`. The sources page names the templates whose pinned lists hold a disabled source or leave out an enabled one.
   - **Tests:** after API startup, a fresh database holds a row per manifest source; a production PEP template created before any sync pins `wikidata-pep`, and with the User-Agent unset its report errors `list_unavailable`; a source added to the disabled variable is disabled at the next startup, and its reports error.
5. **Audit and output.** The CLI prints counts and outcomes only, never names; `watchlist_syncs` is served on the sources page.
6. **Sandbox lists.** `openverify-sandbox` and `openverify-sandbox-pep` hold the synthetic fixture lists of C77.5 in the normalised format. `seed:dev` imports them (and refuses when the resolved mode is `production`). Without the filtered variant-group file it skips the import with a warning naming `python3 scripts/fetch_data.py` and seeds everything else, so the sandbox reports answer `list_unavailable` instead of the seed failing (C78.3). The repository functions that load candidates (`retrieveCandidates` and `loadEntries`, implemented by `api-watchlists`; C78.3) take a mandatory `environmentKind` and add `sandbox_only = false` in SQL for production environments; a repository test shows a production query returns no sandbox entry after `seed:dev`, and C71.2 refuses a sandbox list in a production template, so a production report never names one in its provenance (P13; a test). Sandbox environments may also select enabled real sources; tests and docs never name a real listed person.
7. **Terms and attribution.** Each source's attribution line appears in report provenance, the sources page and `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md`: OFAC (US government work), EU (Decision 2011/833/EU, credit the source), UK (Open Government Licence v3.0), Wikidata (CC0, cited as a courtesy), GDELT (the required citation and link). The UN source, when an operator enables it, shows its terms note.
8. **Retention of list data.** Removed entries are purged `OPENVERIFY_WATCHLIST_REMOVED_RETENTION_DAYS` (default 90) after removal; match snapshots keep what a report needs. PEP entries follow C80.4.

### C77. The normalised record, parsers and diff (`packages/watchlist`)

Stage 1a freezes the record (1), the content and risk-data hashes (3) and the import format (5) in `packages/watchlist/src/record/`: the zod schemas, their TypeScript types and caps, the canonical JSON rule, `contentHash(record)` and `riskDataHash(record)` with golden vectors, and `isHeldOutGroup` (C78.3) with golden vectors. `watchlist-matcher`, `fixtures-6` and the Stage 3 agents build against them from the start; `watchlist-ingest` builds the parsers, the ZIP reader and the diff, and a change to the record is a recorded amendment (F12).

1. **Record** (inspired by FollowTheMoney's model, MIT, read as a reference; our own code): `kind`; `names[]` (`full`, `parts` with roles `given`, `family`, `middle`, `patronymic`, `title`, `suffix`, `legal_form`, `script`, `quality` (`strong` | `weak`, from OFAC `isLowQuality`, UN `QUALITY`, EU `strong`, UK `AliasStrength`), `primary`); `birth_dates[]` (`{ value, precision: day | month | year, range? }`, from OFAC date ranges, UN `TYPE_OF_DATE`, EU parts, UK `dd/mm/yyyy` placeholders); `birth_places[]`; `countries[]` (`{ code: ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 | null, role: nationality | citizenship | residence | birth | registration | address }`); `gender?`; `identifiers[]` (`{ kind: passport | national_id | tax_id | registration_number | lei | swift_bic | imo | mmsi | aircraft_serial | aircraft_tail | website | email | other, value, country? }`); `addresses[]` (country, city, postcode, lines; not matched in Phase 6); `crypto_addresses[]` (`{ raw, ticker }`); `programs[]`; `listed_at?`; `positions[]` (PEP only, C80.2); `relations[]` (PEP family links only). Every string capped (names 300, addresses 500, identifiers 100).
2. **Parsers** (one module per format, streaming): OFAC enhanced XML (SDN, inside a single-member ZIP, and Consolidated), EU FSF XML 1.1, UK Sanctions List XML (XSD 4.33.x), UN consolidated XML, and the Wikidata SPARQL JSON of C80. Guards: XML through `saxes` with any `<!DOCTYPE` refused, no entity beyond the predefined five, element depth ≤ 64, attribute and text node length caps, a total byte cap from the manifest; ZIP through our own single-member reader over `node:zlib` `inflateRaw` (one entry with the expected name, its declared sizes checked against the caps, bytes counted while inflating, no data descriptor surprises, no encryption); never the publishers' CSVs (OFAC truncates its remarks column at 1,000 characters, losing identifiers and addresses). A hostile-input test set (entity expansion, an external entity, deep nesting, a 1 GB declared ZIP member, a ZIP with two members, a truncated file) fails each parser closed.
3. **Content hash.** SHA-256 of a canonical JSON (sorted keys, normalised Unicode) of the fields matching reads: names with role, script and quality, birth dates, countries, gender, identifiers, crypto addresses and kind. Programme or remark edits do not change it, so they never lapse a dismissal.
   **Risk-data hash** (V06). SHA-256 of the same canonical JSON over the programmes, `listed_at`, the PEP positions with `pep-class` and `in-office`, and the relations. It changes when a sanctions party joins a programme or a PEP takes a position, which the content hash leaves out on purpose. Notify-on-change compares it (C75.8). Remarks change neither hash.
4. **Change signals.** OFAC: the publication id and `datePublished` (or the `/changes/latest` answer), compared before downloading; UN: `dateGenerated`; EU: the RSS `pubDate`, then the file's `generationDate`; UK: the GOV.UK content API's `public_updated_at`, then `DateGenerated`; Wikidata: none (a full pass each interval). Each parser returns the publication time it read; monotonicity uses it.
5. **Fixture formats.** `@openverify/watchlist/testing` writes synthetic lists in each publisher's format and in the normalised JSON import format (`{ source, published_at, entries: [record] }`), with fictional persons, companies, vessels and addresses, and a crypto address derived from a published test key; the two sandbox lists (`openverify-sandbox`, `openverify-sandbox-pep`) are written in the normalised format.
6. **Diff.** By `(source_id, entry_id)` and content hash: added, changed, removed, unchanged. OFAC delta files are not used (OFAC advises full refreshes).

### C78. Matching (`packages/watchlist`, on `packages/checks` C58)

1. **Name analysis** (R): the C58 normaliser (NFKD, marks stripped, casefold) per token with script detection, plus our ICAO 9303 §6.A fold (Ä → AE, Ö → OE, Ü → UE, Å → AA, Ø → OE). C58 promises only "fold" and the existing `normalizeName` folds Ä to A, so `watchlist-matcher` builds the §6.A fold in `packages/watchlist/src/names/` from the table `packages/checks/src/extraction/agreement.ts` keeps privately (exported unchanged in Stage 1a), and emits both folds as retrieval keys and token forms: "Müller" meets "MUELLER" and "MULLER" (a test; F21). Titles, honorifics and generational suffixes tagged; particles joined and split; legal forms canonicalised (C58.6 plus a table generated once from rigour `org_types.yml` @ `607fa82`, MIT, committed with its source commit and SHA-256); `FAMILY, Given` commas and structured fields give roles.
2. **Transliteration.** Our own code from the ICAO 9303 Part 3 §6.B (Cyrillic) and §6.C (Arabic) tables, deterministic (R). Cyrillic output is a reliable form. Arabic output carries no vowels (ICAO's Appendix B counts at least 32 Latin spellings of one name), so it is a **lossy** form, like `any-ascii@0.3.3`'s (ISC) output for every other non-Latin script: lossy forms feed retrieval keys and capped scores only (H; H05). When both sides carry the same original script, they are compared in that script, so an Arabic subject is compared reliably with an entry that carries its Arabic original. Node's `Intl` cannot transliterate (checked), and no other transliteration package is used (`transliteration`, `unidecode`, `unidecode-plus` and `text-unidecode` carry Perl or GPL data terms and are banned).
3. **Variant groups and keys.** Cross-script and spelling groups from rigour `person_names.txt` @ `b0ba4ad` (MIT repository; Wikidata CC0 groups; 8,610,609 bytes, SHA-256 `be47a435969da3fa7311d8c682e462d6d20e6cf27cf90ad1473ce213c9a91c79`), fetched through `data/manifest.json` and `scripts/fetch_data.py`, filtered at build time (groups above 60 forms and scripts we do not handle dropped), baked into the worker image, never committed; Phase 5's nickname table (C58.3). The build script takes `--hold-out <seed>`, which also drops the groups that `isHeldOutGroup(groupId, seed)` selects: about a tenth, those whose SHA-256 over the seed and the group id starts with a byte below 26. The function is frozen in Stage 1a with golden vectors, so the generator of 8 and the build agree by construction. Nobody knows the held-out groups before `evaluation-6` picks its seed (V12). Only the evaluation of 8 uses the flag, and the shipped build keeps every group.
   - **Missing data** (F14). When the filtered file is absent or its SHA-256 differs, runs of `watchlist`, `business-watchlist` and `politically-exposed-person` end `errored` with `matcher_data_unavailable`, never screening without the groups; the worker logs it once at start. `integration-6a` puts the file and its hash in the apps image (`.dockerignore`, `apps-image-context.test.sh`). Test.
   - **Keys version** (V04). Every derived column depends on the code and data that made it: the names' `normalised` and `keys`, `watchlist_key_df`, the identifiers' `value_normalised` and the crypto `canonical`.
     - `keysVersion()` (`packages/watchlist`, owned by `watchlist-matcher`) joins `KEYS_VERSION` (the fold, key and name-normaliser rules), the crypto and identifier normaliser versions and the filtered group file's SHA-256. A golden test of the keys, canonical forms and normalised identifiers of fixed inputs fails when their output changes and the version does not.
     - Each applied sync records the version on the source row. A run selecting a source whose `keys_version` differs from the worker's ends `errored` with `matcher_data_unavailable`, naming the source; the worker logs it once at start. So an upgrade never screens against keys that the query no longer produces, which would answer "no match" silently.
     - `watchlists:rekey [--source <id>]` (owned by `api-watchlists`) recomputes every derived column and the key frequencies from `watchlist_entries.data`, in batches under the source's advisory lock (C76.2). It then sets the version and writes a `rekeyed` row in `watchlist_syncs`. Every sync and the refresh tick re-key first when the version differs (C76.2). A re-key without the filtered file, or with another hash, is refused (`refused_matcher_data`). The docs' upgrade note says to run it.
     - Tests: change the version and see `errored`; re-key and see `ready` with the match found; a sync without the group file is refused.
   - **Name retrieval** (V11: one owner). Retrieval is one repository function, `retrieveCandidates(tx, { environmentKind, sourceIds, keys, identifiers, cryptoAddresses, normalisedName, cap }) → { candidates, truncated }`. Its signature is frozen in Stage 1b. `api-watchlists` implements it, with `loadEntries`, in `packages/db/src/repositories/watchlists.ts`, beside the sync that writes the keys and frequencies it reads; `watchlist-matcher` computes the query keys and normalised identifiers it is given. Keys per list name: `t:<token>` (both folds), `s:<consonant skeleton>`, `g:<group id>`, `j:<joined adjacent pair>`; only keys whose document frequency is ≤ 2% of the source's names are used at query time (`watchlist_key_df`); `pg_trgm` on the normalised name is the second path for single-token queries and empty key sets; at most 2,000 candidates ranked by shared keys, and a query that reaches the cap records `truncated` (5). Weak aliases are looked up by exact normalised equality only, and only with `screen-weak-aliases: true` (4).
   - **Identifier retrieval** (H06). A third path of its own: an exact lookup on `watchlist_identifiers (kind, value_normalised)`, within the country when C79.2 says so, for every identifier in the query (passport, national id and tax id for persons; registration number, LEI, website, SWIFT/BIC, IMO, MMSI and aircraft serial and tail for businesses), whatever the names say.
4. **Score** (H, `beta`; every constant provisional, fixed here, ours unless stated, and not tuned on the evaluation set; a change needs a calibration report). Each preset enables a set of the rules below (C79.1):
   - token pairs: 1.0 equal (or equal in the original script, or equal after ICAO §6.B transliteration), 0.9 same variant group, 0.8 nickname, 0.9 × weight 0.5 an initial against a token it begins, Jaro-Winkler (C58's, with Winkler's 0.7 gate) when ≥ 0.88 and within the preset's typo budget, else 0; a lossy pair at most 0.8; **joined**: two adjacent tokens on either side compared as one token (H11);
   - alignment: one to one, any order; family-name pairs weigh 1.3, unmatched subject tokens 0.8, unmatched list tokens 0.2, particles and generic words 0.5; a role swap × 0.95; **subset**: when every token of the shorter name (at least two tokens) is matched, the unmatched tokens of the longer name weigh 0.2 on either side, which covers the public "subset" and "first and last words" rules (H11); the name score is the weighted mean, the best over every (subject name, strong list name) pair; a single-token subject needs an exact or group match;
   - qualifiers, applied when the name score is ≥ 0.70: `dob_day_disjoint` −0.25, `dob_transposed` −0.10, `dob_year_disjoint` −0.15 (ranges and circa ± 1 year allowed), `dob_exact` +0.05, `country_mismatch` −0.10, `gender_mismatch` −0.10; dates follow C58.5 over every pair, the most favourable taken;
   - **identifier match** (H06): an exact identifier of the same kind (and the same country when both sides state one) creates a match whatever the name score, at the higher of the name score and 0.95, with the name level recorded and `identifier_exact` in `match-types`;
   - **corroborated weak alias** (H10), a rule of its own, not arithmetic: with `screen-weak-aliases: true`, a weak alias equal to a subject name after normalisation creates a match only when `dob_exact` holds or an identifier agrees, under every preset and whatever `min-score`, at a fixed 0.90 (ours) with `weak_alias` in `match-types`; with false (the default) weak aliases are not screened at all; they are never fuzzy-matched;
   - entity kind is a hard filter (persons against individuals, businesses against entities), except that a query's IMO, MMSI or aircraft identifier searches vessel or aircraft entries whatever `entity-kinds` says (H06);
   - thresholds by preset (C79.1): below 0.70 nothing is shown; a match is created at or above the preset's minimum.
5. **Results and coverage.** Each match records its components (the matched name pair's levels and methods, qualifiers, the score, the preset and thresholds, the matcher version) and the list values in the sealed snapshot (C73.2). `match-types` (public) is `name_exact` when the normalised names are equal, else `name_fuzzy`, plus `birthdate_exact`, `identifier_exact`, `crypto_address_exact` and `weak_alias` where they apply. **Coverage** (H05, H12; ours): every run records, per subject name, how it was compared (`same_script`, `transliterated` for ICAO Cyrillic, `lossy`, or `not_compared` for a script with no key at all), per source whether any entry carried the subject's script, and `truncated` when retrieval reached its cap or a provider filled its record cap. That detail sits in the sealed `result`. The report's `coverage` attribute serves `complete` (false when any name was `lossy` or `not_compared`, or the search was truncated) and `limits[]` from a closed set (`lossy_script`, `not_compared`, `truncated`, `unclassified_format`, and for media `title_only`, `window_90_days`, `lexicon_languages`), with no names or scripts; the console shows it beside `has-match`. A truncated search that found no match ends `errored` with `result_truncated` (C69.4). Tests: a Han-only subject with no hit is `ready` with `limits: [lossy_script]`; an Arabic subject against a Latin-only entry is flagged, and against an entry with its Arabic original is not; a query at the candidate cap with no match gives `result_truncated`.
6. **Reverse index.** For re-screens (C74.4), the same keys built over the decrypted subjects of the recurring reports in one environment, in chunks of at most 50,000 subjects, in worker memory, dropped after each chunk.
7. **Performance budgets** (measured by `evaluation-6` on synthetic tables): one watchlist screening p95 ≤ 100 ms at 100,000 list names and ≤ 250 ms at 2,000,000 (PEP scale); a re-screen ≤ 60 s per environment per sync at 200,000 subjects. `api-watchlists` gates the 100,000-name p95 in a Testcontainers test beside `retrieveCandidates` (V11). The 2,000,000-name case is a benchmark script (reported, not gated), which also reports how often the 2,000-candidate cap binds (H12). `evaluation-6` reports both scales; a number over budget is a finding for `api-watchlists`, never a constant change.
8. **Evaluation** (H21). `fixtures-6` builds, independently of the matcher's author, a held-out synthetic set: listed fictional persons and companies, variants (typos, transliterations, order swaps, dropped middle names, initials, joined and split tokens, legal-form changes) and a synthetic customer population. Its cross-script variants come only from sources the evaluated matcher does not use: the tenth of the variant groups that the `--hold-out` build drops (3), measured against that build, and transliteration rules `fixtures-6` writes itself, not ICAO's tables. The generator and the build both select the held-out groups through `isHeldOutGroup` with `evaluation-6`'s seed, and `evaluation-6` asserts that no held-out case's group survives in the build it measures (V12). The generator lives in `packages/watchlist/src/testing/held-out/`, which `watchlist-matcher` never reads; the cases are generated at evaluation time from a seed `evaluation-6` chooses, and never committed. `evaluation-6` publishes recall per variant source, precision and alerts per 1,000 customers at each preset in `docs/calibration/watchlist-phase6.md`, with the statement that synthetic variation is not real-world variation.

### C79. Sanctions reports

1. **`watchlist`** (persons). Query (the public request; H22): `name-first`, `name-middle`?, `name-last` or `term` (1–300), `birthdate`? (`YYYY`, `YYYY-MM` or `YYYY-MM-DD`), `address-country-code`?, `gender`?, `identification-number`? with `identification-type`? (`passport`, `national_id`, `tax_id`) and `identification-country-code`?, and ours: `additional-names`? (0–3 names of the same shape, all screened; C84.2). Config:
   - `search-lists` (source ids, resolved and pinned as C71.2);
   - `name-matching` (H11), each preset a set of the C78.4 rules plus a minimum:

     | Preset | Rules | Minimum |
     |---|---|---|
     | `exact` | a strong list name equal to a subject name after normalisation, in any token order; no pair rules | none |
     | `strict` | equal tokens, original-script and ICAO-transliterated equality, cross-script variant groups (the same name in another script), initials, joined tokens, the subset rule; no nicknames, no same-script spelling variants, no typos | 0.85 |
     | `tolerant` (default; the sanctions alert level of the research) | everything in `strict`, plus nicknames, same-script variant groups and Jaro-Winkler typos within a budget of one per token of at least 5 characters (the public "medium" budget) | 0.80 |
     | `custom` | `min-score` 0.70–1.0, with `allow-nicknames`, `allow-variants` and `typo-budget` (`none`; `low`, `medium`, `high`: one typo per token of at least 18, 5 or 3 characters) | `min-score` |

     The public preset names cover these rule sets, which follow the public descriptions (`docs/research/phase6-behaviour.md` §4.1); the scores and minimums are ours;
   - `birthdate-matching` (`exact_date`, `exact_year`, `year_plus_minus_one`: default; what counts as agreement) and `birthdate-filter` (`off`, `penalty`: default, `strict`: drop candidates that disagree), `country-fields` (⊆ `birthplace`, `citizenship`, `nationality`, `residency`; default all) and `country-filter` (`off`, `penalty`: default, `strict`), `screen-weak-aliases` (false; C78.4).

   Results: `matched-lists[]` (`source-id`, `name`, `url`, `list-type`, `list-types[]`, `slug`, `flag-code` (the publisher's country or union code: `US`, `EU`, `GB`, `UN`), `match-types` (the union of its entities'), `entities[]` with `entity-id`, `name`, `alias-type`, `birthdates[]` with `granularity`, `countries`, `programs`, `match-types`, `score`), `ignore-list[]`, `matches` (the review view, C75), `coverage`, and the shared attributes. Events: ready, errored, matched, dismissed, reviewed, redacted.
2. **`business-watchlist`.** Query: `term` (1–300), `address-country-code`? (the public request; H22), `registration-number`?, `lei`?, `website`?, `swift-bic`?, `imo-number`?, `mmsi`?, `aircraft-serial`?, `aircraft-tail`?. Config: as `watchlist` without birthdate and gender, with `name-matching` defaulting to `strict` (the public business default; H11), plus `entity-kinds` (⊆ `entity`, `vessel`, `aircraft`; default `entity`; an identifier query searches vessels and aircraft whatever it says, C78.4). Matching: business names through C58.6 and the legal-form table; exact identifiers (LEI with its ISO 17442 check, registration numbers within a country, website domains, SWIFT/BIC, IMO with its check digit, MMSI, aircraft serial and tail) are retrieved and matched by C78.3 and C78.4 whatever the name says. Results: `matched-lists[]`, `sanction-list[]` (parity), and `fitness-probity-list` and `warning-list` as `null`, listed in `unavailable-fields` with `list_not_configured`, never empty arrays, which integrators read as "nothing found" (H15, decision 18); `lists-searched` names what was searched. **Not 50%-rule screening**: every result and `docs/reports/business-watchlist.md` say so (decision 17).
3. **`crypto-address-watchlist`** (H16). Query: `crypto-address` (≤ 256, as OFAC allows), `chain-id`? (a C57.1 id; omitted for other chains). Surrounding whitespace and a URI scheme and query (`bitcoin:` BIP-21, `ethereum:` EIP-681) are stripped first; the format is then derived from the address, and list addresses go through the same table at ingestion, raw kept, ticker as metadata (OFAC tickers do not name chains: USDT appears in TRON, Ethereum and Omni formats). An address that fails its checksum is kept raw, never dropped. An exact match on the canonical form is the only match (R).

   | Format | Canonical form |
   |---|---|
   | The C57 chains: EVM, Bitcoin and its testnet, Litecoin, Dogecoin, Solana, TRON, XRPL | C57's (EVM lower-cased hex, bech32 lower-cased, base58 exact); an EVM address matches on every EVM chain |
   | Bitcoin Cash CashAddr | lower-cased, `bitcoincash:` stripped |
   | Monero, Dash, Zcash t-addresses, Bitcoin Gold, Bitcoin SV, Verge, BNB Beacon `bnb1` | the raw string after trimming |
   | anything else | the raw string after trimming, with `unclassified_format` in `coverage.limits` |

   Each row has test vectors, and they are the `ga` condition of C69.1. Results: the public list shape with each entity's `crypto-addresses[]` (`type` = ticker, `address`). Monitoring re-screens by the blind index on each sync. Events: ready, errored, matched, dismissed (ours: the public type has no dismissal), reviewed, redacted.

### C80. Politically exposed persons (Wikidata)

1. **Source.** `wikidata-pep`, built by `pnpm --filter @openverify/api pep:sync` (and the refresh job when enabled) from the Wikidata Query Service: POST queries paged per position class, one in flight, back-off on 429 with `Retry-After`, the service's 60 s query timeout respected (our client allows 75 s per query, C76.1, not the outbound client's default 10 s), a required `OPENVERIFY_WIKIDATA_USER_AGENT` carrying the operator's own contact (unset: the source stays enabled, selected and unsynced, and PEP reports error with `list_unavailable`, C76.4; an `--import` needs none, as it makes no request), a configurable endpoint (`OPENVERIFY_WIKIDATA_SPARQL_URL`) for the planned v2 service or a mirror. Never from CI, and never with an end user's name: the sync builds a local mirror, and screening runs against it.
2. **Positions** (H14). `packages/watchlist/data/pep-positions.json` (authored, committed, reviewed): position classes by QID with a PEP class whose numbers follow the public definitions, so a migrating operator's `pep-classes` filter screens the same people:
   - **1:** heads of state and government, national and regional ministers and legislators, heads of the armed forces, courts and police, central-bank board members, party leadership;
   - **2:** senior officials below them (military, judiciary, police, agencies, senior civil servants), senior religious figures, ambassadors and diplomats, and, ours, the heads and deputy heads of international organisations (FATF's third category; publicly class 4, so screened by the default `pep-classes` here);
   - **3:** leadership and boards of state-owned enterprises;
   - **4:** mayors and local assemblies, other senior staff of international bodies.

   Each row carries its source (FATF 2013 guidance categories, the EU Commission's national function lists where published, or `authored`). Records carry `positions[]` (`position`, `pep-class`, `country`, `start`, `end` (a date or `unknown`), `in-office` (`yes`, `no`, `unknown`), `source-key`: the QID).
   **Open-ended tenures** (P12, H14). A P39 statement without an end date may mean "in office" or "end unknown". It counts as current (`in-office: yes`) only when it has no successor statement (P1366) and no later holder of a single-holder position, and started within `OPENVERIFY_PEP_OPEN_TENURE_MAX_YEARS` (default 20; ours, provisional; the reference practice treats no tenure started over 40 years ago as current). Otherwise it ended at the successor's start, or at an unknown date (`end: unknown`, `in-office: no`), and an unknown end counts as older than the retention of 4. Tests on synthetic SPARQL answers for each case.
3. **Relatives** (P12, H14). Spouse (P26), children (P40), parents (P22, P25) and siblings (P3373) of class 1–2 holders, when Wikidata states them, are synced only when the install sets `OPENVERIFY_PEP_INCLUDE_RELATIVES=true` (default false), whatever the templates say, so the install-wide tables hold no relatives otherwise. A relative born less than 18 years ago, or with no birth date, is never synced. A relative carries the linked PEP's class and the relation (`spouse`, `child`, `parent`, `sibling`). Close associates are not derivable and are not built. A template also needs `include-relatives: true` (default false). Tests for both rules.
4. **Retention.** A holder whose every position ended more than 12 months ago is excluded at sync time; 5 years for heads of state or government (`OPENVERIFY_PEP_RETENTION_MONTHS`, `OPENVERIFY_PEP_HEAD_RETENTION_MONTHS`; decision 9). Deceased persons are excluded. **Relatives** (V13) hold no positions, so the rule never reads theirs: a relative is kept exactly while a PEP it is linked to is kept, under that PEP's clock, and is excluded with the last of them. Tests on synthetic SPARQL answers: a relative of a holder within retention is kept; a relative of a holder past it is excluded; a relative of two holders stays while either does.
5. **Report.** Query (the public PEP request): name parts or `term`, `birthdate`?, `country-code`?, and ours `additional-names`?. Config: `search-lists` (sources of kind `pep`, resolved and pinned as C71.2), `pep-classes` (default 1, 2), `include-relatives` (false), `include-unknown-class` (false), `default-country`?, `name-matching` (default `strict`, 0.85, the research PEP alert level), the birthdate settings with `keep-without-birthdate: true` (a PEP with no recorded birthdate is not filtered out). Results, in the public shape (H14): `result[]` entities with `entity-id`, `name`, `birthdates[]`, `country`, `sex`, `match-types[]`, `pep-type` (`pep` or `relative`), `positions[]`, `associates[]` (`name`, `relationship`) and `sources` (QID → its Wikidata URL); `death-dates` is always empty (deceased persons are excluded); `topics`, `images` and `related-urls` are not served; plus `matches` (C75), `coverage` and the shared attributes. `result` and `positions` are PII. Coverage notice in every result: Wikidata's coverage is uneven by country and level, and dates are often missing, so a miss is not evidence of non-PEP status. H-tier, `beta`.

### C81. Adverse media

1. **Provider kind `adverseMedia`**: `gdelt-doc` or `sandbox`; unset means unbound (`provider_not_configured`). Binding `gdelt-doc` also requires `OPENVERIFY_GDELT_ACKNOWLEDGE_DISCLOSURE=true`, because every query sends the screened name to a third party; without it the worker refuses to start, naming the variable (decision 7).
2. **The `gdelt-doc` adapter** (the outbound client, its own content-type allowlist, 1 MiB cap): the DOC 2.0 `artlist` mode in JSON, `sort=hybridrel`, the query below, the rolling three-month window, at most 250 records (the API's maximum; H12). A plain-text error answered with status 200 and a 429 are both `provider_error` (retryable).
   - **Query** (V10). The screened name comes from the API caller or from an inquiry's typed fields, so it may hold GDELT operators (a quote, `-`, `sourcecountry:`, `domain:`, `OR`, parentheses) that close the phrase and add a filter that returns nothing, which would read as "no match". So the query is built only by `gdeltQuery` (`apps/api/src/providers/adverse-media/query.ts`):
     - the C58-normalised (casefolded) name tokens, each reduced to Unicode letters and digits (`\p{L}\p{N}`), joined by single spaces inside one double-quoted phrase that the adapter writes;
     - the template's category terms from our authored lexicon (`packages/checks/src/reports/media/`, en, es, fr, de), as an `(a OR b)` group that the adapter writes;
     - a country only as `sourcecountry:` from a fixed table keyed by the validated ISO code.

     Nothing from the subject reaches the query outside the phrase. A name that is empty after this answers 422 `report_query_invalid` on creation; a completion report's run ends `errored` with `insufficient_input`. Unit tests with names holding `"`, `-`, `sourcecountry:`, `OR` and parentheses assert the exact query string the stub receives.
   - **Budget** (P09, V09). An install-wide GCRA of one request per 5 s in Valkey, as GDELT asks. It is shared among the environments that asked for a token in the last 60 s: each takes tokens through a per-environment GCRA whose period is 5 s times the number of those environments. So one environment waiting alone gets the whole budget, and none can take another's share. Valkey holds environment ids and times only.
   - A run that cannot take a token goes back to `pending` with `not_before` at the GCRA's retry time plus jitter (C74.1), and the sweep enqueues waiting runs oldest `scheduled_for` first. A run that has waited 24 h ends `errored` with `provider_error`. With the media cap of C72.5, that happens only when the environments together schedule more than the install budget. The sources page shows the scheduled media runs per day against the 17,280 the budget allows and warns above 80%.
   - Tests: one environment alone reaches the install budget; two waiting environments get half each; one environment over its share does not error another's media run.
   - **No cache** (P03). A cached title would hold the screened name beside a crime term, unsealed, outside redaction and retention, and a cache hit would tell one organisation that another screened the same name. So nothing about an article is cached; every run asks again. Test: run the `sandbox-media` path and scan every Valkey key and value for the subject's name and the article titles.
   - **Truncation** (H12). An answer that fills the 250 records is asked again once with `sourcecountry:` when the query has a country. When it still fills the cap, the run records `truncated`, and ends `errored` with `result_truncated` if no article matched (C69.4).
3. **Relevance** (H): an article is a match when its title contains the normalised name (or every token of it) and at least one category term; otherwise it is dropped. **Business names** (H13): the legal form is stripped (articles rarely carry it), and an article then matches only with the full distinctive phrase plus the query's country (or its name) or a second distinctive token; a `business-adverse-media` term that is a single generic word once stripped is refused (422 `report_query_invalid`). The score is ours (title match, category weight, recency), with a confidence capped at 0.6. No entity resolution beyond the name and the optional country.
4. **Stored per article** (the sealed snapshot): URL, domain, `seen-date` (when GDELT saw the article: GDELT returns no publication date, so the field is named for what it is; H13), language, source country, and the title capped at 200 characters, shown only as link text with `rel="noopener noreferrer nofollow"`; never the body, an image or a snippet (decision 8); the GDELT citation and link in the result.
5. **Reports.** `adverse-media` (persons; the public query: name parts or `term`, `birthdate`?, `country-code`?, and ours `additional-names`?) and `business-adverse-media` (`term`, `country-code`?). Config: `categories` (default all), `max-article-age-days` (1–90; 90; measured on `seen-date`), `allowed-countries`?; `dated-articles-only` is not offered, as every GDELT record has a `seen-date`. **Coverage notice** in every result (H13): the 90-day window, the lexicon languages (en, es, fr, de), relevance from titles only, and the 250-record cap, so "no match" means no title mention in that window, not no coverage anywhere; `coverage.limits` carries the same (C78.5). Review per article (`dismiss-type: media`) or for the whole report (`entity`). Results: `articles` (PII). Sandbox: names containing the token `sandbox-media` return two synthetic articles under `*.example`.

### C82. Email, phone and address reports

1. **`email-address`.** Query: `email-address`. R fields: `email-domain` (PII: a vanity domain names its owner; P15), `email-domain-kind` (ours, not PII: `free_mail`, `relay`, `disposable`, `custom`), `email-domain-exists`, `email-is-valid-mx` (null MX aware, C56), `email-is-disposable` (C56's list), `email-domain-age-days` (RDAP, C85.3), `email-is-free-provider`, `email-is-relay`, `email-is-role-account`, `email-mx-provider` (ours), `email-spf-policy` and `email-dmarc-policy` (ours; RFC 7208 record parse without following `include:`, the RFC 9989 DMARC tree walk with at most 8 queries; informational only: they say nothing about a person), `email-is-deliverable` (true only for a report created by an inquiry's completion when that inquiry holds a confirmed OTP for exactly this address (C55, C84.2); otherwise null, in `unavailable-fields` with `not_confirmed`, or `not_linked` for any other origin, so no API caller can test guessed addresses against an inquiry's confirmed one; P05), `email-domain-typo-suggestion` (ours; PII, P15). H fields (`beta`; the research rule, H07): `email-reputation` is the worst band reached.

   | Band | Any of these reasons | Confidence |
   |---|---|---|
   | `low` | `syntax_invalid`, `domain_not_found`, `domain_rejects_mail` (null MX), `no_mail_server`, `disposable_domain`, `domain_recently_registered` (a custom domain younger than `min-domain-age-days`), `mx_of_disposable_service` | 0.95 for DNS and list facts; 0.7 for age; 0.5 for the MX heuristic |
   | `medium` | `domain_typo_of_major_provider`, `role_account`, `young_domain` (a custom domain younger than `young-domain-days`), a breach in the last 12 months from a bound `emailIntelligence` provider | 0.4–0.6 |
   | `high` | none of the above, and the domain accepts mail | 0.5: an absence of bad signals is weak evidence |
   | `null` | `privacy_relay` (decision 19), or a DNS or RDAP `execution_error` that blocks a hard reason: never `high` on a timeout | — |

   `email-is-suspicious` is true exactly when the band is `low`, and null with a null band. Each result carries the band's confidence, the reasons that applied, the thresholds and the provenance (list commits, the RDAP server, the DNS time). Free provider, MX provider, SPF and DMARC never change the band. Config: `min-domain-age-days` (1–3,650; 30) and `young-domain-days` (1–3,650; 365). Tests: NXDOMAIN, a null MX and a disposable domain give `low`; a DNS timeout gives `null`; a relay gives `null`; a bound breach gives `medium`. A fields (`emailIntelligence`: `hibp`, `sandbox`): `email-credentials-leaked-recent` and the breach count; `email-is-spam`, `email-malicious-activity-detected-recent` and `email-estimated-age-days` stay null (no adapter in Phase 6). Authored tables, committed and reviewed: `free-mail-domains.json` (about 200 consumer mailbox domains, each with the provider's own page as source), `relay-domains.json`, `role-local-parts.json` (RFC 2142 plus authored), `mx-providers.json`; a build test asserts no free-mail domain is on the disposable list. No SMTP probing.
2. **`phone-number`** (H08). Query: `phone-number` (E.164). R fields (libphonenumber-js `/max`, C55.8): `phone-country-code`, `phone-is-possible`, `phone-is-valid`, `phone-type` mapped from the numbering plan (`FIXED_LINE`, `MOBILE`, `TOLL_FREE`, `VOIP` for numbering-plan VoIP only, `PAGER`, `PERSONAL`, `VOICEMAIL`, `RESTRICTED_PREMIUM` for premium, shared-cost and UAN, `INVALID`). `FIXED_LINE_OR_MOBILE`, the plan's answer for every ordinary US and Canadian number, gives `phone-type: null` with `phone-type-numbering-plan: FIXED_LINE_OR_MOBILE` (ours) and `line_type_undetermined` in `unavailable-fields`: in the public vocabulary `OTHER` is a definite line type from a carrier, so we never report it from the plan; `PREPAID`, `PAYPHONE` and `OTHER` come only from a provider. With `phoneIntelligence` bound, the network line type replaces the plan type (`nonFixedVoip` → `VOIP`, `fixedVoip` → `FIXED_LINE`, `landline` → `FIXED_LINE`, `mobile` → `MOBILE`). `phone-reachable-at-otp` (ours; as email: completion reports only). H fields (`beta`; the research rule, in the public level vocabulary):

   | Type | `phone-risk-level` / `phone-risk-recommendation` | Confidence |
   |---|---|---|
   | `INVALID` | `high` / `block` | 0.95 |
   | `RESTRICTED_PREMIUM`, `TOLL_FREE`, `PAGER`, `VOICEMAIL` | `high` / `block` (the set Phase 5 refuses for OTP) | 0.9 |
   | `VOIP` | `medium-high` / `block` | 0.7 |
   | `PERSONAL` | `medium-low` / `allow` | 0.5 |
   | `MOBILE`, `FIXED_LINE` | `low` / `allow` | 0.5 |
   | undetermined (`FIXED_LINE_OR_MOBILE`, no provider) | `low` / `allow`, with the reason `line_type_undetermined` stated (decision 51) | 0.3 |

   With a provider, a SIM swap within 24 h gives `high` and within 7 days `medium-high`; a port within 30 days gives `medium` (the windows are ours). Each result carries the confidence, the reasons and the provider identity. A fields (`phoneIntelligence`: `twilio-lookup` (Lookup v2 line type intelligence and SIM swap), `sandbox`): `phone-carrier`, the network line type, `phone-risk-sim-swap`, porting. `phone-risk-score` stays null: we never synthesise a 0–1,000 score (decision 20).
3. **`address-lookup`.** Query as the public shape (`addressee`?, `address-street-1`, `address-street-2`?, `address-city`, `address-subdivision`, `address-postal-code`, `address-country-code`). R fields: `resolved-*` from the C58 normaliser, labelled `normalised` in provenance; postcode validity (libaddressinput patterns); US ZIP-to-state consistency; PO box, APO/FPO/DPO and PMB designators. H: a CMRA keyword hint in an ours-only field, `cmra-hint`, never in a USPS-derived field such as `analysis-dpv-cmra` (H08). A fields (`addressVerification`: `smarty` (US Street API), `sandbox`): the DPV, RDI, CMRA, vacancy, LACSLink, SuiteLink, carrier-route, county and precision fields of the public shape. The adapter receives the normalised components only (P16): never `addressee` unless the template sets `send-addressee: true` (default false), and only for `address-country-code: US`; for any other country the A fields are null, listed in `unavailable-fields` with `unsupported_country`, and nothing is sent. Test: the stub never receives an addressee or a non-US address. No USPS adapter (USPS limits its APIs to shipping and mailing, decision 21); no OpenStreetMap geocoder (ODbL, decision 22).
4. **Sandbox magic values** (sandbox environments only, listed in `docs/checks/sandbox-providers.md`): email local-part tokens `sandbox-breached`, `sandbox-spam` and the reserved domains of C56 and C61.6 (`disposable.example`, `relay.example`, `new.example`, `old.example`); phone numbers in the NANP fictional range `+1 NPA 555 01xx` whose last two digits select an answer (`00` mobile, `01` non-fixed VoIP, `02` prepaid, `03` SIM swap within 24 h, `04` ported within 30 days, `05` unreachable, `06` fixed VoIP); address house numbers `1000`–`1006` (confirmed, commercial, undeliverable, CMRA, vacant, missing secondary, invalid secondary); the business name "Sandbox Sanctioned Holdings Ltd", the LEI `5299000SANDBOXLEI047` and the IMO `9999993` in the sandbox list; fictional office holders and one fictional relative in `openverify-sandbox-pep`; EVM addresses ending in `dead` or `beef` for crypto exposure. No magic value is a real person's or company's name.

### C83. Provider kinds (a C63 amendment)

| Kind | Used by | Values | Unset |
|---|---|---|---|
| `adverseMedia` | `adverse-media`, `business-adverse-media` | `gdelt-doc`, `sandbox` | unbound |
| `emailIntelligence` | `email-address` A fields | `hibp`, `sandbox` | unbound |
| `phoneIntelligence` | `phone-number` A fields | `twilio-lookup`, `sandbox` | unbound |
| `addressVerification` | `address-lookup` A fields | `smarty`, `sandbox` | unbound |
| `cryptoExposure` | an `exposure` block on `crypto-address-watchlist` | `sandbox` | unbound |

Bound by `OPENVERIFY_PROVIDER_<KIND>_{SANDBOX,PRODUCTION}`; the worker reads them; a sandbox value on a production variable, an unknown name or a missing credential stops the worker without echoing a value (C63's rules). Credentials: `OPENVERIFY_HIBP_API_KEY`; `OPENVERIFY_TWILIO_LOOKUP_ACCOUNT_SID`, `_API_KEY_SID`, `_API_KEY_SECRET` (separate from the API's SMS credentials, so the worker never holds the SMS sender's key; an operator may use a restricted key); `OPENVERIFY_SMARTY_AUTH_ID`, `OPENVERIFY_SMARTY_AUTH_TOKEN`. They join the secret-scan list and the startup-error redaction test, and compose gives them to the worker only. Each real adapter goes through the outbound client with its own content-type allowlist and cap and is tested against a local stub with our own synthetic answers. A real adapter receives only its query fields, less what its section withholds (C82.3: no addressee unless asked, US addresses only); only sandbox providers see the reference id. `docs/reports/` has a disclosure section per adapter: what is sent, to whom, under which terms (GDELT's citation; HIBP makes submitted addresses "Subscriber Data"; Twilio; Smarty), and that the operator needs a basis for it (P16). The `cryptoExposure` provider is called through its port, which Stage 1b declares, by the crypto job hook's optional exposure step. `api-reports` writes that step with the hook it owns, and `api-providers` delivers the provider only, so each file keeps one editor (F19, F01).

### C84. Inquiry and account integration

1. **Report list on inquiry template versions.** A new version member, `report-config` (contracts `reportConfigSchema`, strict; stored in a new `inquiry_template_versions.report_config` jsonb column, default `{}`): `on-completion` (0–10 of `{ 'report-template-id', 'query-source'?: 'inquiry' (default) }`) and `on-report-match` (`none` default, or `needs_review`; ours). Publish rules (422 `report_template_not_published` at `/data/attributes/report-config/on-completion/<i>`, frozen in Stage 1a; F07): each template id exists in the environment, is active and has a published version; the same template at most once. Stage 1b stores the column through `repositories/inquiry-templates.ts` and `services/inquiry-templates.ts` (create, draft update, publish and the version resource) and passes `report-config` to `assertPublishable`, which calls `services/publish/report-config.ts` (a stub owned by `api-reports`). Pinned per version, so an inquiry keeps the list it started with. At completion each entry runs from its template's published version at that moment; a template archived after the inquiry template was published still runs.
2. **Run on completion (an outbox).** In `transitionInquiryInTx`, a hook (`createInquiryReportsInTx`, a Stage 1b stub) runs when the inquiry moves `pending → completed`, or `failed → approved | needs_review` (V08). The second is an operator overriding a failed inquiry, for example after a flaky selfie, which would otherwise onboard a person whose report list never ran. The hook reads the pinned `report-config` and, for each entry, builds the query (below) and creates the report (`pending`, `origin: inquiry_completion`, linked to the inquiry only) and its `initial` run in the same transaction. An inquiry makes at most one of these moves (C10: `completed` never becomes `failed`, and `failed` is left once), so its reports are created once; a test asserts it. `failed → declined` creates none.
   - **No account lock here** (F04). The transaction already holds the inquiry row, and consolidation locks accounts before it updates inquiries, so a foreign key to the account taken here would deadlock against a consolidation of that account. The initial run links the inquiry's current account (home and `report_accounts`) under the C73.7 order, and computes `crypto_address_index` (so `LifecycleDeps` needs no blind-index key). Race tests in C74.6.
   - **Outbox.** No job is enqueued there: the sweep (C74.3) enqueues pending runs within 15 s, on every completion path (settlement, the sandbox simulate action, and the approve and mark-for-review actions on a failed inquiry).
   - **Missing input.** A query missing a required input still creates the report `pending`; its initial run ends `errored` with `insufficient_input`, visibly, never silently skipped, and C84.3 applies to it in the run's own transaction (never inside the completion transition).
   - **Query sources** (H04). Each field comes from the first source that has it, and `query-sources` records which:

     | Type | Sources, in order |
     |---|---|
     | `watchlist`, `politically-exposed-person`, `adverse-media` | the extraction of the latest government ID verification decided `passed` or `needs_review`, the inquiry's `fields`, the account's `fields`; every name in these sources that differs from the first is screened too, as `additional-names` (at most 3), so a prefilled name never replaces the verified one |
     | `email-address` | the inquiry's confirmed email OTP destination, the inquiry's `fields`, the account's `fields` |
     | `phone-number` | the inquiry's confirmed phone OTP destination, then as above |
     | `crypto-address-watchlist` | the latest passed crypto-wallet verification's address and chain, then as above |
     | `business-watchlist`, `business-adverse-media` | the latest passed `database-business` verification's registry legal name, registration number and LEI, then its entered business name, then the inquiry's `fields` |
     | `address-lookup` | the inquiry's `fields`, the government ID extraction, the account's `fields` |

     The OTP-based fields of C82 are computed only for these reports (`origin: inquiry_completion`; P05).
   - **Tests** on the worker path and the simulate path: a prefilled name that differs from the ID's screens both; an email collected only by an OTP step runs the email report; a wallet collected only by the crypto step runs the crypto report. On the action path: a failed inquiry then approved creates its reports, whose runs record `inquiry_already_decided`; a failed inquiry then marked for review creates them, and they show in its reports section; a failed inquiry then declined creates none.
3. **A match sends the inquiry to review** (opt-in, `on-report-match: needs_review`; H03). It applies only to reports with `origin: inquiry_completion`, so a key without `inquiries:write` cannot move an inquiry through a report it created (P05). When a run of such a report first brings `has-match: true`, **or its initial run ends `errored` with any code** (a report that could not screen is not clear: ADR 0002 §3b), and the inquiry is `completed`, the run's transaction (locks as C73.7) moves the inquiry to `needs_review` through `transitionInquiryInTx` (`inquiry.marked-for-review`), and the run records the cause (`report_match` or `report_errored`). A decided inquiry (`approved`, `declined`, `needs_review`) is not changed; the run records `inquiry_already_decided`, and the console shows it in the inquiry's reports section ("decided before this report finished"). `docs/reports/README.md` states the window: an operator who decides between completion and the runs decides without the reports. The window is the sweep's 15 s, plus the run, plus any wait for the GDELT budget, which can reach 24 h on an overcommitted install (C81.2; V09). Approving a failed inquiry always decides before its reports run, so the docs advise marking it for review first (V08). Default `none`: nothing changes the inquiry (C69.4). Tests: a stale list on completion gives `needs_review`; an insufficient input gives `needs_review`; an approval inside the window is recorded on the run.
4. **Accounts.** A report created with `account-id` links that account (home), else as `meta.auto-create-account*` says (C72.1). Completion reports link the inquiry's account at their initial run (2). `report_accounts` holds every link; consolidation moves the absorbed account's report links to the absorbing account under the account lock order; account redaction redacts the linked reports, with the catch-up of C73.5. `GET /accounts/:id?include=reports` (C70.5). `add-objects` for reports is not in Phase 6.

### C85. Amendments to Phase 5 contracts

Each amendment with real logic is built by its only consumer behind an interface Stage 1b declares in the shared file, so Stage 1b carries types and stubs only (F01).
1. **Outbound client** (C54.6). A second request form for list sources, `stream(request, sink)`, created only by the watchlist clients: the body is written to a bounded sink (the per-run private file of C76.2, removed afterwards) while bytes are counted against `max_bytes`; the answer returns the response headers named in the client's `exposeHeaders` (`etag`, `last-modified`, `content-length`, and OFAC's `x-amz-meta-publication-id`); `redirect: { maxHops: 1, host }` follows one HTTPS redirect only to that exact host, re-running the vetted lookup on it, with no request headers carried over and the `Location` never logged; every other client keeps refusing redirects. **Deadlines** (F18): the source's `timeout_seconds` bounds the whole request, and a separate 30 s idle timeout bounds each wait for body bytes; every other client keeps the 10 s default, which covers the body too and would cut a 22–26 MB list or a long SPARQL query. Stage 1b declares the form in `http/index.ts`; `api-watchlists` builds it in `apps/api/src/http/stream.ts`. Tests: a 302 to the allowed host followed; to another host, to http, or a second hop refused; a body over the cap aborted; headers exposed only when named; a slow stub under the deadline completes and one over it aborts with `timeout`.
2. **DNS port** (C56). `DnsResolver.resolveTxt(name)` (4 KiB cap per answer, the same error mapping and cache as MX: 1 h positive, 5 min negative). Stage 1b declares it in `dns/index.ts`; `api-providers` builds it in `apps/api/src/dns/txt.ts`.
3. **RDAP** (C61.4). A `registered-domain` mode for email domains that answers for the registered domain and flags a subdomain; known free-mail domains are not queried. `api-providers` builds it in `apps/api/src/providers/domain-age/registered-domain.ts` behind a Stage 1b interface.
4. **Jobs** (C54.1, 5B; F17). `report-sweep` (every 15 s) and `watchlists-refresh` (a 15-minute tick, C76.2) are registered in `jobs/repeatable/index.ts`. `report-rescreen` is an on-demand queue with its worker in `worker.ts` and a producer for the sync CLI and `seed:dev` (over their own Valkey connection), not a repeatable job: `RepeatableJob.run()` takes no argument, and each repeatable job has one fixed scheduler. `report-run` is a queue as well. Both queues remove their jobs when they complete or fail (C74.1, C74.4; V02), because the run rows and the syncs' `rescreen_state` are the record and a kept job id would block every re-add. `report-rescreen` jobs are keyed `rescreen-<sync id>`, since BullMQ ids may not contain `:` (`webhooks/queue.ts:41`). Owned by Stage 1b of 6A.

### C86. Console surfaces (Workflow 6B)

- **Reports index** (filters on type, template, status, has-match, tag, account and inquiry; cursor pagination; an errored report reads "not screened", never "no match") and **report detail**: the query (masked without `pii:view`), status and errors in words, sources used with publication time, last check, `origin` and staleness, the `coverage` beside `has-match` with its limits in words, matches with their evidence (matched names side by side, levels, qualifiers, score, preset), review and dismissal dialogs (reason required), the ignore list, monitoring state and actions (pause, resume, run now), run notes in words (`inquiry_already_decided`, `recurring_limit`, `evidence_changed`, `risk_data_changed`, `match_not_found_in_run`), an open match that the last run did not find marked so (C75.8), tags, redaction, the history timeline; per type: positions and relatives for PEP, articles as links for media (never fetched, never embedded) with the coverage notice, the email, phone and address fields with `unavailable-fields` explained (`email-domain-kind` when the domain is masked), the crypto addresses. Every result shows its tier and `beta` stage and "What a result means".
- **Create report** (choose a template, enter the query) and the **reports sections** on the inquiry and account pages (no tab primitive exists).
- **Report templates**: list, detail, versions, a JSON editor with validation pointers and the resolved configuration beside it, publish; structured forms wait for Phase 9.
- **Watchlist sources** page (read-only): sources, enabled, "not configured" and "needs re-key" (C76.4, C78.3), last check, last applied sync, publication time, SHA-256, `origin`, counts, staleness, the last refusal or error and any override, a re-screen still pending, the sandbox lists labelled (never stale), the templates whose pinned lists hold a disabled source or leave out an enabled one, the GDELT line (scheduled media runs per day against the install budget, warned above 80%; C81.2), the attribution lines.
- **Settings**: `report-retention-days`. **Event words** for every report event (the "Report" event group itself lands in 6A Stage 1a, C70.4). The "Reports" permission area. The README section.

### C88. Deferred

The `screening` composite report and its record API (`scrre_`, `scatr_`), CSV bulk creation and exports, `print` (Phase 9), the workflow "Run report" action and match conditions (Phase 8), cases holding reports (Phase 8), Connect snapshots (Phase 11), custom-list reports (Phase 7 lists), the US Consolidated Screening List, Canada, Australia and Switzerland lists, an OpenSanctions adapter for licensed operators, commercial PEP, media and crypto analytics adapters, ownership flags and 50%-rule data, GeoNames centroids, libphonenumber geocoding and carrier data, email age estimates, the GDELT GKG local index, `add-objects` for reports, an organisation-wide ignore list, re-screening when a linked account's fields change (a report's query never changes in Phase 6; Phase 7 with the account graph), PEP candidates, PEP portrait comparison and PEP `topics` and `images`.
**Note (2026-11-20, Phase 11 Workflow 11E, C282; connect correction 4):** of "Connect snapshots (Phase 11)" above, Phase 11 built none: it defers report sharing (`docs/plans/phase-11.plan.md` C286), because a cloned screening is a "no match" for lists, versions and a matcher the destination never chose, which ADR 0005 allows only beside the search actually made; a destination runs its own reports on the data a share hydrates. The vendor's specification does carry report sources, destinations and snapshot subtypes for five report types (`docs/research/phase11-connect.md`, R8).

### C89. Fixtures and end to end

1. **Rule.** Synthetic only: fictional persons, companies, vessels, addresses and articles; no real listed person, PEP or publisher in any fixture, doc or test; deterministic seeds.
2. **Full-stack e2e** (each spec ≤ 90 s, its budget stated in a header line; lists loaded by `seed:dev` and `watchlists:sync --import`; no publisher, Wikidata or GDELT call; sandbox providers):
   - `report-watchlist.spec.ts`: a watchlist report on a listed fictional person in the sandbox list: ready, `has-match`, the `report/watchlist.matched` webhook verified; a dismissal (`.dismissed`), `has-match` false, a re-run keeps it dismissed;
   - `report-monitoring.spec.ts`: a recurring report; a new list version imported that adds a matching entry: a `list_update` run and `.matched`; that match dismissed, then a version imported that changes the entry's birthdate, and a run: a new open match with `lapsed_from` and `.matched` (the real lapse path; H20, F20); pause, resume, run now;
   - `report-inquiry.spec.ts`: an inquiry template with an email OTP step and `report-config` (watchlist, email); the first inquiry completes through the flow, the second through the sandbox simulate action (F15); both reports run, linked to the inquiry and, after their first run, its account; the email report of the first inquiry screens the confirmed OTP address and is deliverable; with `on-report-match: needs_review` the inquiry is marked for review; inquiry redaction redacts the reports;
   - `report-risk.spec.ts`: email (`sandbox-breached`; an API report's `email-is-deliverable` is null with `not_linked`), phone (`555 0103` SIM swap), address (`1002` undeliverable), crypto (the sandbox listed address), business (the sandbox company by LEI), adverse media (`sandbox-media`), PEP (the synthetic SPARQL import into `wikidata-pep`, recorded as `origin: import`; no User-Agent is set or needed, as an import makes no request, C76.2).

   **Suite time** (F15). `integration-6a` measures the whole suite once the four specs land and raises Playwright's `globalTimeout` and the CI e2e job's `timeout-minutes`, or shards the job, updating `infra/tests/ci-workflow.test.sh`; 6B's `fix-api` measures again after the UI specs land.
3. **Testcontainers, not e2e:** every sync refusal (rollback and future publications, mass removal, schema, size, matcher data, the redirect rules) and the sync races of C76.2, the source reconciliation (C76.4), freshness and staleness with the sandbox exemption, the keys version and re-key (C78.3), the race tests of C74.6, claim fencing, crash recovery and budget waits with jobs removed (C74.1), the sweep's catch-up and re-screen recovery (C74.3), retrieval and scoring at 100,000 synthetic names, the reverse re-screen and its idempotence, consolidation, retention and redaction scans (C73.5, C73.6), the Valkey scan and the GDELT share (C81.2), the report limits (C72.5).

## Workflow 6B (console, evaluation, review, fixes)

- **Stage 1** (parallel): **`dashboard-reports`** (C86's reports index, detail, create, the inquiry and account sections, event words, the permission area, the `report-retention-days` settings field, the README; owns every shared dashboard file, `components/operators/settings-fields.ts` and the settings page included, adding the other agent's navigation entries when asked; F19), **`dashboard-report-config`** (report templates and versions, the watchlist sources page; owns `app/report-templates/`, `app/watchlist-sources/`, its components, fixtures, mock routes `testing/routes/report-config.ts` and one spec `e2e/tests/ui-report-config.spec.ts`), **`evaluation-6`** (C78.7–C78.8 measurements on the held-out set it generates from its own seed, which also drives the `--hold-out` build (V12), and on synthetic tables, `docs/calibration/watchlist-phase6.md`, benchmark scripts in `packages/watchlist/scripts/benchmark-*`; never changes a constant: a bad number is a finding for the owner).
- **Stage 2, review of all of Phase 6** (read-only, parallel): privacy and security; correctness and honesty (matching, review semantics, monitoring, the honesty rules, tiers); ingestion and licences (hostile XML and ZIP, the sync's redirect, caps, monotonicity, mass removal, staleness, the Wikidata and GDELT usage policies, attributions); console (React, accessibility, the RSC payload).
- **Stage 3:** one verifier for every CRITICAL and HIGH finding; it records the 6B Stage 1 amendments.
- **Stage 4:** path-owned fixers **`fix-api`** (apps/api, packages/, e2e except `ui-*` specs and their helpers, docs/plans, docs/api, docs/reports, docs/checks, ci, infra, `.dockerignore`; it measures the e2e suite again with the UI specs and raises the timeouts or shards, F15) and **`fix-console`** (apps/dashboard, `e2e/tests/ui-*.spec.ts` and their helpers, docs/calibration).

## Rules for every agent

- **TDD:** RED first, seen failing for the intended reason; the RED commit compiles and typechecks; GREEN; refactor on green. Evidence in `docs/tdd/phase6-<agent>.tdd.md`.
- **Shared files:** builders never edit the shared-file list above; route schemas, the OpenAPI snapshot, `@openverify/node` and API fixtures change only through the integration agent; a missing hook or schema change is a `contractDeviation`.
- **Shared resources:** commits path-scoped under `flock /tmp/openverify-git.lock` after `git diff --cached --quiet`; full-stack e2e under `flock /tmp/openverify-e2e.lock`; long gates in the foreground with a 600,000 ms timeout; flaky tests made deterministic, never retried.
- **No network in tests:** MockAgent or local stubs with our own synthetic responses; injected resolvers; no publisher, Wikidata, GDELT, HIBP, Twilio or Smarty call in CI.
- **Honesty (ADR 0002, ADR 0005):** no "no match" on an unscreened, stale or truncated search; no `false` for an unknown; a gap on our side ends in `errored` or `null` with a reason, never a pass; H-tier results carry a confidence and their reasons; every constant provisional with a source; no threshold tuned on the evaluation set; no fixture special case beyond the documented sandbox rules.
- **Independence of the evaluation** (H21): `watchlist-matcher` never reads `packages/watchlist/src/testing/held-out/` or any held-out output, and `fixtures-6` never reads `packages/watchlist/src/{score,match}` until they are committed; the held-out cases are generated by `evaluation-6` from its own seed and never committed; the held-out groups are chosen by `isHeldOutGroup` with that seed, so neither the matcher's author nor the generator's knows them in advance (V12).
- **PII:** no query value, matched name, article title or list entry name in logs (except under `pii`), metadata, problems, events or git; synthetic data only.
- **Licences and versions:** rule 4; versions at least 14 days old; no new build script without an `allowBuilds` decision; static data through `scripts/fetch_*` with pinned SHA-256; live lists only through the sync (ADR 0005).
- **Content:** no Persona name, branding or copied text in product UI, docs or messages.

## Validation

```bash
pnpm install --frozen-lockfile && pnpm format:check
pnpm turbo run lint typecheck --force
pnpm test                                     # every package >= 80%, @openverify/watchlist included
pnpm test:integration                         # the migration, pg_trgm retrieval, sync refusals and races, claims, race tests, re-screen, redaction and Valkey scans
python3 scripts/fetch_models.py && python3 scripts/fetch_data.py
(cd services/vision && uv run ruff check && uv run ruff format --check && uv run mypy src tools && uv run pytest)
(cd android && ./gradlew testDebugUnitTest testAndroidHostTest koverVerify assembleDebug :openverify-sdk:assemble lint)
pnpm e2e                                      # mocked flow suite, then Phases 1-6 full-stack specs
docker build -f infra/docker/apps.Dockerfile . && docker build services/vision
for t in infra/tests/*.test.sh; do bash "$t"; done   # includes the image context (variant groups) and Postgres parameter logging
gitleaks git --redact .
```

## Risks

| Risk | Likelihood | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|
| False positives on common names flood reviewers | High | Qualifiers, rare-key retrieval, weak aliases off by default, presets as method sets, alerts measured per 1,000 customers before anyone changes a threshold |
| Cross-script misses (Arabic, Han, Indic names) | High | Same-script comparison, ICAO tables, variant groups, lossy keys; a `coverage` attribute on every result says when a name was compared lossily or not at all (C78.5) |
| Identifier hits missed under another spelling, or a renamed vessel | Med | Identifier retrieval and matching whatever the name says (C78.3, C78.4) |
| A publisher changes its format or URL | Med | Strict schema validation refuses the sync and keeps the last good data; staleness turns reports `errored`; the sources page shows the refusal |
| A quiet publisher looks stale | Med | Freshness counts from the last successful check, not the last change (C76.3) |
| A poisoned, future-dated or truncated list removes designations | Low–Med | HTTPS and the vetted lookup, monotonicity and the mass-removal guard re-checked under the lock, the future-date refusal, `origin` on imports, the audit table |
| A match row or cache re-identifies the subject | Med | The entry key as an HMAC, the evidence sealed, every index nulled on redaction, no article cache, Postgres parameter logging off (C73, C81.2) |
| One tenant exhausts shared budgets | Med | Per-environment limits, a recurring cap, a GDELT budget shared among the environments waiting for it, and a cap on scheduled media runs (C72.5, C81.2) |
| A default source list resolves to nothing and reads as "no match" | Low | Source rows reconciled from the manifest at startup; unconfigured sources stay selected and error; an empty list refused; a run with an empty selection errors (C76.4, C71.2, C69.4) |
| A matcher or data upgrade leaves stored keys stale, so entries are silently missed | Med | A keys version per source; runs error `matcher_data_unavailable` until `watchlists:rekey`; a golden test forces the version bump (C78.3) |
| A lost job or re-screen stalls monitoring | Med | Report jobs removed when they finish; fenced final failures; the sweep re-enqueues runs and re-screens from the database (C74.1, C74.3, C74.4) |
| Stage 1b does not finish, as Phase 5's did not | Med | Plumbing only; logic with its owners and consumers; Stage 2 runs beside it; three green checkpoints; Stage 1a freezes the record and keeps every suite green |
| The redirect allowance becomes an SSRF path | Low | One hop, one exact host per source, HTTPS, the vetted lookup again, only the list clients |
| Hostile XML or ZIP exhausts the worker | Med | Streaming parsers with depth, length and byte caps; DOCTYPE refused; the single-member ZIP reader |
| Wikidata usage policy or availability changes | Med | Operator-run sync with their own contact, a configurable endpoint, a stale source errors honestly |
| GDELT disclosure of screened names | Med | Off by default; an explicit acknowledgement variable; the compliance note |
| Monitoring storms after a large list update or an outage | Med | The reverse index in chunks, the 60 s budget, runs enqueued in batches and not re-added for 5 minutes, one catch-up run per report (C74.3) |
| Screening data is personal data (GDPR Art. 10 for media) | High | Sealed snapshots, redaction, retention settings, the lawful-basis note; the operator decides |
| C58 or the outbound client land differently in Phase 5 | Med | Stage 1a re-verifies and records amendments before any Phase 6 code |

## Acceptance

- [ ] Every C69 type has a query schema, a result schema, a golden fixture, an evaluator at its tier, a docs page and a dashboard view; no report answers "no match" for an unscreened, stale or truncated search, an empty source list or keys from another matcher version, and an errored report keeps the matches it found; every matching result states its coverage; a default production template with an unconfigured source errors `list_unavailable`; a seeded sandbox stack still answers `ready` days after seeding.
- [ ] Reports are usable from the API and the dashboard: create, list, detail, tags, dismiss, review, pause, resume, run, history, redaction, templates and versions, the sources page; the public request shapes are accepted.
- [ ] OFAC, EU and UK parsers pass their synthetic and hostile tests; the sync refuses rollback, a future publication, mass removal, a bad schema, an oversize body and a redirect to another host, under the lock; a quiet publisher stays fresh while checks succeed; nothing from a real list is in git.
- [ ] Matching meets the C78.7 budgets on synthetic tables, or the gap is recorded under owner decision 1 after Workflow 6C; identifiers match whatever the name says; the evaluation report publishes recall per variant source, precision and alert rates at the fixed constants.
- [ ] Every report attribute marked PII is served unmasked with `pii:view`, masked without it and null after redaction, at every exit, the stored API-log bodies of create, dismiss and review included; a redacted report keeps no entry key, qualifier, index or evidence hash that names the matched entry; no screened name or article title is in Valkey; every sealed column is rewrapped and redacted.
