- [ ] A crashed or expired run is recovered once and completes once; a budget wait and a lost re-screen run again; a final failure never errors a run another worker holds; an outage gives one catch-up run per report; a race test per writer pair.
- [ ] Reports run on inquiry completion on every completion path, and when a failed inquiry is approved or sent to review, from the verified data first; `on-report-match` covers a match and an errored report, and is off by default.
- [ ] A reviewed match with `notify-on-change` re-alerts on a new programme or PEP position; every row of the match state machine has its test.
- [ ] Every existing suite stays green at the end of each stage; every validation command passes locally and on GitHub; `docs/tdd/phase6-*.tdd.md` per agent; `docs/PROGRESS.md` records Phase 6.

## Decisions for the owner (made here; confirm or change)

Each item states the default the build uses until the owner decides, so none blocks a Workflow.

**Sources and terms**
1. **UN Security Council list** (site terms: personal, non-commercial use only). Default: shipped as a disabled source; an operator may enable it after reading its terms; the OFAC, EU and UK lists carry most UN designations.
2. **Live list data and rule 4** (no pinned SHA-256 is possible for daily lists). Default: ADR 0005: operator-run sync, never committed or shipped, verified instead of pinned.
3. **`submitted` status and event** unused in Phase 6 (every adapter answers synchronously). Default: reserved, not registered.
4. **One redirect for list downloads** (OFAC and UN serve through pre-signed storage URLs; C54.6 refuses redirects). Default: one hop to one exact host per source, list clients only (C85.1).
5. **Continue monitoring after a hit** (the public legacy types stop until dismissed). Default: continue, with `pause-on-match` for parity.
6. **No organisation-wide ignore list.** Default: per-report suppression only.
7. **GDELT adverse media** sends screened names to a third party. Default: unbound; binding needs `OPENVERIFY_GDELT_ACKNOWLEDGE_DISCLOSURE=true`.
8. **Article storage** (the EU press-publishers' right). Default: URL, domain, date, language, source country and a 200-character title as link text only.
9. **PEP retention after leaving office.** Default: 12 months; 5 years for heads of state or government; deceased excluded.
10. **Wikidata query service** (its robots.txt disallows `/sparql` for crawlers; the usage policy allows API clients). Default: allowed for the operator-run sync with the operator's own contact in the User-Agent; never from CI.
11. **EU FSF token.** Default: the operator sets `OPENVERIFY_EU_FSF_TOKEN` (the EU catalogue publishes a public one); until then the EU source is unconfigured: it stays in the default lists, and their reports error `list_unavailable` naming it (C76.4, decision 56). The value is not committed, so secret scanners stay quiet.
12. **Canada, Australia, Switzerland, the US Consolidated Screening List, World Bank, Interpol.** Default: none built in (unclear or non-commercial terms; CSL deferred).
13. **OpenSanctions** data (CC BY-NC) and code (MIT, but `rigour` needs LGPL `python-stdnum`). Default: neither used; an adapter for licensed operators later.
14. **Sandbox data.** Default: two synthetic lists (sanctions and PEP) and synthetic magic values only; the seeded sandbox templates select only the synthetic lists, so a fresh stack answers without a download (F11); the synthetic lists are never stale, so the stack keeps answering after the 48 h limit (V03); real lists may also sync into sandbox environments, but tests and docs never name a real listed person.

**Matching**
15. **Provisional constants** (C78.4, the presets of C79.1). Default: as stated, changed only through a calibration report.
16. **Weak aliases** (H10) are not screened by default. With `screen-weak-aliases: true`, a weak alias equal to a subject name makes a match only with an exact birthdate or an agreeing identifier, under every preset, at a fixed 0.90 (C78.4); never fuzzy-matched. Default: off.
17. **No 50%-rule or ownership claims** in the business watchlist. Default: stated in every result.
18. **Warning and fitness-and-probity lists** are not built in Phase 6 (H15). Default: both `null`, listed in `unavailable-fields` with `list_not_configured`, with `lists-searched`; never empty arrays.
19. **Privacy relays** in email reports. Default: `email-reputation: null` with `privacy_relay`.
20. **No synthesised phone risk score.** Default: `phone-risk-score` null without a provider score.
21. **No USPS adapter** (USPS limits its APIs to shipping and mailing). Default: Smarty as the address adapter.
22. **No OpenStreetMap geocoding** (ODbL). Default: not in Phase 6.
23. **The subject index for re-screens** is built in memory per job and never persisted (a persisted HMAC table would reveal token equality to a database reader). Default: in memory.
24. **Country and birthdate filters** penalise by default; `strict` per template. Default: penalty.

**Platform**
25. **Event names** `report/<type>.<action>` (the public names), plus our `.reviewed` for every type with review and `.redacted`. Default: adopted.
26. **Report templates versioned** (`rptpv_`, immutable published versions) with a template API, where the public templates have neither. Default: adopted.
27. **Reports run on inquiry completion** through the sweep (up to 15 s later), on every completion path; their first run links the inquiry's account (C84.2). Default: adopted.
28. **`on-report-match: needs_review`** (ours) before Phase 8 workflows exist; it also covers an errored completion report (decision 48). Default: available, off.
29. **Viewers see reports** with PII masked. Default: `reports:read` is viewer-visible.
30. **Vendor adapters built now:** GDELT, HIBP, Twilio Lookup, Smarty, each unbound. Default: as stated; Telesign, Vonage, Google, Loqate, Chainalysis, TRM and Elliptic later.
31. **HIBP terms** (Core forbids third-party use; Pro is undocumented). Default: the operator's own key on a plan that allows screening its users; the docs say so.
32. **Separate Twilio credentials** for the worker's Lookup calls. Default: separate variables.
33. **Report retention** default keeps reports as long as inquiries (null). Default: null, documented.
34. **Lawful basis and DPIA** for screening data. Default: documented as the operator's responsibility; OpenVerify takes no position; not legal advice.
35. **`CLAUDE.md` layout** gains `packages/watchlist`; agents do not edit `CLAUDE.md`. Default: owner edit.

**From the review: privacy and limits**
36. **Match rows name no entry in plain** (P01): the entry key is an HMAC under the environment's blind-index subkey; the readable key, the entry's content hash and the qualifiers sit in the sealed snapshot; redaction nulls every index and hash. Default: as stated.
37. **Report history is append-only** except a KEK rewrap and the redaction of `details`; only an explicit environment deletion removes it (P02, F03). Default: as stated.
38. **No adverse-media cache** (P03): every run asks GDELT again, inside its budget. Alternative: an environment-scoped, sealed, HMAC-keyed cache. Default: no cache.
39. **Linking needs the linked resource's scope** (`inquiries:write` for an inquiry, `accounts:read` for an existing account and for any `auto-create-account-reference-id`, V14), and `on-report-match` and the OTP-based fields apply only to completion reports (P05). Default: as stated.
40. **Report limits** (P09, V09; provisional): 600 creations and runs per minute per environment; at most 250,000 recurring reports per environment; at most 20 waiting `sync` requests per environment; the GDELT budget shared equally among the environments waiting for it (all of it for one), waited for rather than failed; at most 8,640 scheduled media runs per day per environment. Default: as stated.
41. **Email domains are PII** (P15); the console reads `email-domain-kind`. Default: masked without `pii:view`.
42. **The address vendor gets no addressee** unless the template sets `send-addressee`, and only US addresses (P16). Default: as stated.
43. **Postgres logs no bind parameters** (P18): `log_parameter_max_length=0` in compose and tests. Default: as stated.
44. **List imports** (P08): a publication dated more than 24 h ahead is refused; the normalised format is accepted only for the sandbox sources or with `--allow-normalised`; `--accept-older-publication` is the audited way back from a bad import; every import is recorded and shown with `origin: import`. Default: as stated.
45. **Report webhook filters are strict** (F10): a report without the filtered attribute does not pass a present filter; other subjects keep C14's rule. Alternative: C14's rule for reports too. Default: strict for reports.

**From the review: honesty**
46. **Freshness counts from the last successful check** (H02, F02), with the per-source intervals of C76.1 and a PEP default `max-list-age-hours` of 192. Default: as stated.
47. **A partial run errors and keeps its matches** (H01): any unscreened selected source makes the run `errored`, and its matches still alert. Default: as stated.
48. **`on-report-match: needs_review` also routes an errored completion report to review** (H03; ADR 0002 §3b). Alternative: a separate `on-report-error` option. Default: one option.
49. **Completion reports screen the verified data first** (H04): the ID extraction, then the inquiry's and the account's fields, each differing name screened too; OTP and verification values for the contact, crypto and business types. Default: as stated.
50. **Presets are method sets** (H11): `strict` allows no nicknames, same-script spelling variants or typos; `tolerant` adds them with a typo budget by length; the business default is `strict`, as publicly. Default: as stated.
51. **An undetermined US or Canadian line type** (H08) gives `phone-type: null` and the research's `low`/`allow` at confidence 0.3 with `line_type_undetermined` stated, because both possible plan types are low-risk on our rule. Alternative: a null risk level. Default: `low` at 0.3.
52. **PEP classes follow the public definitions**, with heads and deputy heads of international organisations in class 2 (ours); an open-ended tenure is current only within 20 years and without a successor; relatives are synced only with `OPENVERIFY_PEP_INCLUDE_RELATIVES=true`, and never minors (H14, P12). Default: as stated.
53. **A truncated search with no match errors** with `result_truncated` (H12). Default: as stated.
54. **A missing variant-group file errors the person-name runs** with `matcher_data_unavailable` (F14), rather than screening without it or stopping the worker. Default: as stated.
55. **Public request shapes** (H22): `address-country-code` for the watchlist types, `data.attributes` on create, dismiss and review, `auto-create-account` true by default; `reference-id` refused. Default: as stated.

**From the verification round**
56. **Source rows come from the manifest** (V01). The API, the worker and the CLIs reconcile them at startup: `enabled` is the manifest default less `OPENVERIFY_WATCHLIST_SOURCES_DISABLED`. A source without its token or User-Agent stays selected and errors `list_unavailable` until it syncs; an empty source list is refused. Alternative: leave unconfigured sources out of the default lists, so that a template made before the EU token is set never screens the EU list. Default: as stated.
57. **Notify-on-change covers risk data** (V06): a new programme, listing date, PEP position or relation returns a reviewed match with `notify-on-change` to `pending`, beside a change of the matched identity; a dismissal lapses on identity changes only. Alternative: identity changes only. Default: as stated.
58. **Only a person, or the entry leaving its source, closes a match** (V07). An open match that a later run does not find stays open with `present-in-last-run` false. An open match whose entry returns to content dismissed before stays open. Reviewing a dismissed match whose entry already has an open or delisted row answers 409 `report_match_superseded`. Alternative: close or dismiss automatically. Default: as stated.
59. **Reports also run when a failed inquiry is approved or sent to review** (V08); their runs record `inquiry_already_decided` after an approval. Alternative: no reports, with "reports not run: the inquiry did not complete" in the console. Default: run them.
60. **A matcher upgrade errors until re-keyed** (V04): runs selecting a source keyed by another `keys_version` end `errored` with `matcher_data_unavailable` until `watchlists:rekey`, a sync or the refresh tick re-keys it. Alternative: re-key automatically at worker start, which takes long at PEP scale and delays startup. Default: as stated.
61. **Report and re-screen jobs are removed when they finish** (V02, V05): the database rows are the record, and the sweep enqueues again whatever is still due. Alternative: one job id per enqueue generation. Default: as stated.

## Workflow 6A Stage 1a amendments (contracts-6)

Recorded by `contracts-6` on 2026-10-27, at the Phase 6 base `fe4850ca`, before any Phase 6 code; the evidence is in `docs/tdd/phase6-contracts-6.tdd.md`. Each item is binding for the later stages.

**Starting point and Phase 5 assumptions (C85), re-verified against the code:**
1. **Migration number.** The newest migration is `0019_submitted_verifications_index.sql` (Workflow 5C, K1), so Phase 6's first migration is **`0020`**, not the `0019` of C73.1.
2. **C58 landed with both folds.** `foldText()` (`packages/checks/src/matching/text.ts`) returns a `simplified` rendering (ä → a, ø → o) and an `expanded` one (ä → ae, ö → oe, ü → ue, å → aa, ø → oe, ICAO 9303 §6.A and German practice), plus `writingSystemsOf()`, `inDifferentScripts()` and `WRITING_SYSTEMS`. The matcher may take its §6.A fold from `foldText(...).expanded`. The private agreement table is exported unchanged as planned (F21): `ICAO_LATIN_TRANSLITERATIONS` (Æ, Œ, Þ, Ð, Đ, Ł, Ħ) and `ICAO_LATIN_EXPANDED_FORMS` (Ä, Ö, Ü, Å, Ø), both from `@openverify/checks`.
3. **Outbound HTTP.** `OutboundHttpResponse` now carries `headers` (a 3xx answer's `location`; a 2xx answer's `expires` and `cache-control`), and a client may opt into `acceptMissingContentType`. It still buffers whole bodies (1 MiB default, 10 s for the whole request), follows no redirect and does not decompress, so the C85.1 `stream()` form is still needed.
4. **RDAP.** `providers/domain-age/rdap.ts` exists: the IANA bootstrap cached 1 hour to 7 days, labels trimmed from the left (at most four queries), a shorter name answering flagged `subdomain`, each RDAP host paced at one request a second install-wide through `ProviderBindingOptions` (`pacing:<rule>`), and registration dates cached for a day under an unsalted SHA-256 of the domain, install-wide. C85.3's registered-domain mode builds on it. **For `api-providers`:** an email domain is PII (P15), so before it reuses that install-wide cache for email domains it must decide whether a cache hit may tell one tenant that another looked the domain up (the C81.2, P03 reasoning).
5. **Providers.** `PROVIDER_NAMES` is `sandbox`, `twilio`, `json-rpc`, `rdap`, `feed-list` (there is no `none`); the 5B kinds are `businessRegistry`, `domainAge`, `maliciousWebsite`, `backlink`; the API reads `sms`, `database`, `aamva`, the worker every kind but `sms`.
6. **Repeatable jobs.** `phase5RepeatableJobs(deps: RepeatableJobDeps): RepeatableJob[]` in `apps/api/src/jobs/repeatable/index.ts` registers `submitted-verification-sweep`, `malicious-feed-refresh` and `trust-anchor-refresh`; `RepeatableJob.run()` still takes no argument. `report-sweep` and `watchlists-refresh` join that registry (C85.4).
7. **Unchanged as stated:** `DnsResolver` has no `resolveTxt`; `REDACTION_SUBJECT_ID_PREFIXES` is `inq`, `ver`, `iqse`, `acc`, `self`; `packages/db/src/testing.ts` and `migration-v2.test.ts` load only `vector`; `.dockerignore` re-includes the data manifest, the feed list and the disposable list only; the events table's name check allows one slash; `templateSettingsSchema` is a strict four-key object; `MASKED_MEMBERS` also holds `phone-number` and `email-address`; the API rate limit is 300 a minute; `API_SCOPES` had 20 entries (28 now).
8. **e2e.** The full-stack suite has 82 tests in 37 files (about 9.5 minutes); Playwright's `globalTimeout` is 45 minutes and the CI e2e job allows 70, not the 40 and 45 of the Starting point.

**What Stage 1a froze, and how it differs from the plan text:**
9. **Core** (`packages/core`): `report-registry.ts` (`REPORT_TYPES`, `REPORT_REGISTRY` with tiers, stage, recurrence, review model, source kind, provider kinds and events), `report-state-machine.ts` (`submitted` has no transitions in or out: it is reserved, decision 3; a report of a type that does not recur only ever leaves `pending`), `report-match-state-machine.ts` (`decideMatchTransition`, one test per C75.8 row), `report-matching.ts` (the C78.4 constants and the C79.1 presets as method sets) and `report-config.ts` (the option vocabularies, bounds and `REPORT_CONFIG_DEFAULTS`). The query and result schemas live in `@openverify/contracts` (core cannot import the country schema of `flow-spec`), the fixtures in `contracts/fixtures/api/`, the labels in the dashboard; the docs-page half of C69.1's registry test is `integration-6a`'s, which owns `docs/reports/`.
10. **One case the C75.8 table leaves open:** a `record` review with `false_positive` of a match that is already `dismissed` keeps it dismissed, records the review and fires `.reviewed` only (nothing is dismissed again).
11. **`crypto-address-watchlist` ships `beta`.** `integration-6a` moves it to `ga` in `REPORT_REGISTRY` once `watchlist-matcher`'s C79.3 format vectors pass (C69.1).
12. **Template configuration** (`reportTemplateConfigSchema(type)`): `name-matching` is an object discriminated by `preset` (`custom` adds `min-score` 0.70-1.0, `allow-nicknames`, `allow-variants`, `typo-budget`); a type that does not recur takes only `recurrence-interval-days: null` and has no `pause-on-match` or `resume-delay-minutes`; `business-watchlist`'s `country-fields` are `registration` and `address` (ours: the person roles do not apply to a company); `max-list-age-hours` exists for the list types only. An empty `search-lists` passes the schema, so the publish rule can answer `watchlist_sources_empty` (V01); the `search-lists` default (`null`) means "resolved at creation" and is never served. The adverse-media categories are ours, twelve ids in `ADVERSE_MEDIA_CATEGORIES` (`financial_crime`, `fraud`, `corruption`, `money_laundering`, `terrorism`, `organised_crime`, `narcotics`, `violent_crime`, `cybercrime`, `property_crime`, `regulatory`, `financial_difficulty`); `risk-logic` writes the lexicon's terms per id, and another id is an amendment.
13. **Template resources.** A template is a `report-template` with a `report-type` attribute (not the public polymorphic `report-template/<type>`), and embeds its `current-version`, as an inquiry template does. The version create and draft-update bodies take `config` as a free object: its type is the template's, which the body does not name, so the service checks it with `reportTemplateConfigSchema(type)` and answers 422 with pointers.
14. **`report-config` on inquiry template versions** is defined (`reportConfigSchema`, `resolvedReportConfigSchema`, `resolveReportConfig`, `reportConfigEntryPointer(i)`) but **not yet added to the version bodies or resource**: the API cannot store it before the Stage 1b column, and a body member it silently dropped would mislead. `foundation-6` adds `'report-config': reportConfigSchema.optional()` to `createInquiryTemplateVersionBodySchema` and `updateInquiryTemplateVersionBodySchema`, and `'report-config': resolvedReportConfigSchema` to the version attributes (built with `resolveReportConfig`), when it stores the column (checkpoint 2), and regenerates the OpenAPI snapshot in checkpoint 3. A template id listed twice is refused by the schema at `/on-completion/<i>/report-template-id`.
15. **Report attributes beyond C70.2's list, all ours:** `provider` (`{ id, sandbox }`), `thresholds` (the preset and thresholds applied, ADR 0002 §3f) and `notices` (`no_ownership_screening`, `pep_coverage_uneven`, `media_coverage_limited`). `unavailable-fields` is a list of `{ field, reason }` with a closed reason set (`REPORT_UNAVAILABLE_REASONS`), so C73.2's `unavailable_fields text[]` column must keep the reason too (jsonb, or `field:reason` text); `foundation-6` decides. A pending report serves every type attribute, `null` or an empty list, until its first run.
16. **Rules the resource schemas enforce:** a report is `errored` exactly when it carries errors; `has-match` says whether some match is `open` (when the matches are shown); a type without review is never matched (`has-match: false` as a literal); the business watchlist's `fitness-probity-list` and `warning-list` are always `null`; an article's confidence is at most 0.6 and its title at most 200 characters; `phone-risk-score` and the email fields without an adapter or method are always `null`; `phone-type` never takes the plan's `FIXED_LINE_OR_MOBILE`.
17. **The query is echoed under `query` only**, for every type (the public watchlist and media schemas also echo some inputs at the top level; ours do not). **PEP positions are nested** in each `result` entity (C80.5), so they are masked with `result`: there is no top-level `positions` attribute and `PII_REPORT_ATTRIBUTES` does not name one.
18. **Match ids.** A match in `matches` has an `id` (`rpm_`), which is the review body's `record-token`. Entities, articles and ignore-list entries link to it with `match-id`, history actions with `match-ids`, and `.dismissed` carries `meta: { match-ids }`. `.reviewed` carries `meta: { review-type, review-status, risk-level, record-token, reviewer }`, never the comment. Our own members avoid `token` in their names: the secret scan reads a ULID under a `token` key as a credential.
19. **Events.** 45 names, `report/<type>.<action>`, from core's `REPORT_EVENT_NAMES`; `eventSubject()` answers `report` for each, and `eventResourceType(name)` gives the payload type. A `.dismissed` or `.reviewed` payload must carry its meta unless it is a redacted stub. Webhook samples take `report-<type>.json`; `.errored` adds an `execution_error`, `.redacted` nulls `PII_REPORT_ATTRIBUTES`, the next run and recurrence.
20. **PII marks.** `PII_REPORT_ATTRIBUTES` is `query`, `matched-lists`, `ignore-list`, `matches`, `sanction-list`, `fitness-probity-list`, `warning-list`, `result`, `exposure`, `articles`, `email-domain`, `email-domain-typo-suggestion`, the six `resolved-*` members, `metadata-latitude`, `metadata-longitude`, `reason`, `comment` and `entity-id`. The collision test walks every resource and request body the package exports; to hold it, the inquiry's and the account's `fields` are now marked PII too (they were already masked by name; nothing changes at run time). The one exemption is `api-log.query` (P17).
21. **Includes.** `INQUIRY_INCLUDES` gains `reports` (`reports:read`); until `includedReportsFor` lands, the include adds nothing, because no report exists. `ACCOUNT_INCLUDES`, `getAccountQuerySchema` and `accountIncludeDocumentSchema` are defined for the Stage 1b route; `integration-6a` gives `@openverify/node`'s `accounts.retrieve` an `include` once the route takes it.
22. **Rate limit.** `RATE_LIMIT_POLICIES` gains `reports` (600 a minute). The plugin builds its rule but nothing enforces it until `api-reports` does; `config.ts` still reads only `OPENVERIFY_RATE_LIMIT_API_PER_MINUTE`, so `foundation-6` adds `OPENVERIFY_RATE_LIMIT_REPORTS_PER_MINUTE`.
23. **Dashboard documents and the sources page** are frozen in contracts too: `dashboardReportDocumentSchema` (the report with its runs and actions in `included`), `dashboardReportListDocumentSchema`, and `dashboardWatchlistSourcesSchema` with `WATCHLIST_SOURCE_ERROR_CODES` (ours) and `GDELT_INSTALL_BUDGET` (17,280 requests a day, warned above 80%).
24. **`packages/watchlist`.** The record is snake_case (`watchlist_entries.data`), with the publisher's `entry_id`, a `script` from C58's `WRITING_SYSTEMS`, generous list caps (500 names, 1,000 crypto addresses) and `remarks` (read by neither hash). The canonical JSON sorts keys and normalises strings to NFC; the hashes sort set-like lists by their canonical JSON (UTF-16 code-unit order) and hash `["openverify/watchlist-content/v1", …]` and `["openverify/watchlist-risk/v1", …]`; the risk-data hash reads a position's id, class, country, dates and whether in office, not its label. `isHeldOutGroup(groupId, seed)` hashes `["openverify/held-out/v1", seed, groupId]`. The package depends on `@openverify/checks`, `@openverify/core`, `saxes@6.0.0`, `any-ascii@0.3.3` and `zod`, so Stage 2 needs no dependency change; `keysVersion(variantGroupsSha256)` is a signature stub for `watchlist-matcher`.
25. **Lint.** The Phase 5 regexp and log-key rules (`packages/config/eslint/phase5-rules.js`) now also cover `packages/watchlist/src/**` and `packages/checks/src/reports/**`, which read untrusted lists and subjects.
26. **Records.** `persona-surface.md` writes the report events with a slash and adds the two missing types; the 76 missing report events are described by kind, not listed, because the events page was read in summary only.
27. **Existing tests changed with the plan** (C72.2, C70.4, C72.5, C1): the scope and role lists (`permissions.test.ts`, `vocabulary.test.ts`, the dashboard's `permissions.test.ts` and `role-options.test.ts`, the `role.json` fixture), `EVENT_NAMES` and the event-name pattern (`vocabulary.test.ts`), `RESOURCE_TYPES`, the dashboard event groups (`events.test.ts`), the rate-limit policies (`rate-limits.test.ts`, the API's `rate-limit.test.ts`), the ID prefixes (`ids.test.ts`), the node client's groups (`resources.test.ts`) and the checks entry point (`index.test.ts`).

## Workflow 6A Stage 1b amendments (foundation-6)

Recorded by `foundation-6` on 2026-10-27 at the end of Stage 1b. Checkpoint 1 (data) ends at `d98927cc`, checkpoint 2 (masking and hooks) at `e9c2b2b1`, checkpoint 3 (interfaces and wiring) at `462bfad7` and the checkpoint 3 record; the evidence is in `docs/tdd/phase6-foundation-6.tdd.md`. Each item is binding for Stages 3 and 4; a change to a frozen name or signature is a recorded amendment.

**Data (checkpoint 1):**
1. **Migration `0020_phase6_reports_watchlists`.** `CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS pg_trgm` first; the seven enums of C73.1; `inquiry_template_versions.report_config` (jsonb, default `{}`); the C73.2 and C73.3 tables. Beyond C73.2 (ours): `reports.report_template_id`, `sources` (jsonb provenance), `matcher_version`, `provider` (jsonb), `thresholds` (jsonb) and `notices` (text[]); `unavailable_fields` is jsonb, a list of `{ field, reason }` (Stage 1a amendment 15); `report_matches.reviewed_at`; `report_runs.status` is text with a check, not an enum; `report_actions.actor_type` reuses `audit_actor_type` (`user`, `api_key`, `system`, the same set as `REPORT_ACTOR_TYPES`). The list children `watchlist_names`, `watchlist_identifiers` and `watchlist_crypto_addresses` are keyed `(source_id, entry_id, ordinal)`; `watchlist_entries.listed_at` is the record's date text; `watchlist_syncs.id` is a bigint identity (a sync id is a number everywhere). Constraints: one `open` or `delisted` match per `(report_id, entry_index)` (`report_matches_one_live_per_entry_key`), one run per `(report_id, trigger_sync_id)`, one published version per report template; `entry_index` and `evidence_hash` are 64 hex characters or null. Indexes include `reports_due_idx`, `reports_recurring_idx`, `report_runs_claimable_idx`, `watchlist_syncs_rescreen_pending_idx`, `watchlist_entries_source_id_changed_sync_id_idx`, `watchlist_names_keys_idx` (GIN) and `watchlist_names_normalised_trgm_idx` (`gin_trgm_ops`). `PGLITE_EXTENSIONS = { vector, pg_trgm }` is used by every PGlite instance and by `migration-v2.test.ts`.
2. **Sealing and keys.** Seal names `reports.query`, `reports.result`, `report_actions.details`, `report_matches.snapshot` (row AAD); `REWRAP_TABLES` gains `reports`, `report_actions`, `report_matches`. Blind-index labels `openverify/report-crypto-address/v1`, `openverify/report-match-entry/v1`, `openverify/report-match-evidence/v1`, with `reportCryptoAddressIndex`, `reportMatchEntryIndex` and `reportMatchEvidenceHash` (a JSON array frames the parts, P10). `rebuildReportIndexes(db, key, options)` is `api-reports`' to implement; its stub answers zero while no row holds an index and refuses otherwise, so a rebuild never reports as done what it did not do.
3. **Cross-agent reads and writes implemented here:** `listRescreenEnvironments`, `listRecurringReportSubjects` (chunks of at most `RESCREEN_CHUNK_LIMIT` = 50,000), `insertListUpdateRuns` (one pending `list_update` run per report, `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` on `(report_id, trigger_sync_id)`) and `listReportsDueForRetention` (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` batches). The other report repository files carry only their export line and an owner constant.
4. **Redaction and retention.** `rep` joins `REDACTION_SUBJECT_ID_PREFIXES`; inquiry and account redaction stub their reports' events and clear the reports' stored API copies, then call `redactInquiryReportsInTx` and `redactAccountReportsInTx` (with the account catch-up); `RedactionService.redactReport` calls `redactReportInTx`. The environment setting `report-retention-days` (1-3,650 or null, default null) is served and set through the C16 settings route; `services/retention.ts` redacts each report past it through the redaction service and counts `redactedReports` and `failedReportRedactions`.

**Masking and hooks (checkpoint 2):**
5. **Masking.** `MASKED_ATTRIBUTES` (`auth/pii.ts`) and the API-log masked set include `PII_REPORT_ATTRIBUTES`; `query` is masked member-wise inside `attributes`. The log keys `reportQuery`, `matchedName`, `listEntryName`, `articleTitle` and `subjectIndex` are redacted by both loggers and allowed by the lint rule, whose folder list covers every Phase 6 API folder.
6. **Hooks (stubs for their owners; signatures frozen):** `createInquiryReportsInTx(tx, { events, clock }, { inquiry, from, to })` runs in `transitionInquiryInTx` exactly on the moves `runsCompletionReports(from, to)` accepts (`pending → completed`, `failed → approved | needs_review`); `moveReportLinksInTx(tx, { environmentId, targetId, sourceIds })` runs in consolidation; `includedReportsFor(tx, environmentId, { inquiryId } | { accountId }, mask)` (at most `MAX_INCLUDED_REPORTS` = 50) serves `?include=reports` on inquiries and on the account route, whose `include` query is frozen; `resolveReportFilterSubjects(db, environmentId, payloads)` resolves strict report subjects (the `strict` flag of `WebhookFilterSubject`); `reportConfigPublishProblems(ctx)` is the C84.1 publish rule (the stub refuses every entry); `seedReportTemplates(db, { environments, ids, clock })` runs in `seed:dev` after `reconcileWatchlistSources`, which also runs when the API is ready and before the worker's jobs start. `report-config` is stored, served resolved and passed to the publish rule (Stage 1a amendment 14 done).
7. **Report type jobs.** `jobs/report-types/job.ts` freezes `ReportTypeJob { type, run(ctx: ReportRunContext): Promise<RunOutcome> }`, `ReportRunContext { report, run, deps, providers, signal }`, `ReportJobDeps`, `ReportRunResult`, `FoundReportMatch` and `RunOutcome` (`completed` or `wait` with `notBefore`); an unbuilt type fails with `ReportTypeNotBuiltError`.

**Interfaces and wiring (checkpoint 3):**
8. **C85 forms.** `OutboundHttp.streamClient(options)` with `OutboundStreamClientOptions` (`timeoutMs`, `idleTimeoutMs` default 30 s, `exposeHeaders`, `redirect: { maxHops: 1, host }`) and the stub in `http/stream.ts` (`api-watchlists`); `DnsResolver.resolveTxt` through `dns/txt.ts` (`api-providers`); `NodeResolver.resolveTxt` is optional so the DNS test fakes still compile; `providers/domain-age/registered-domain.ts` declares `RegisteredDomainAgeProvider` (`api-providers`); `CryptoExposureProvider` in `ports.ts`.
9. **Watchlist reads.** `retrieveCandidates(db, query)`, `loadEntries(db, { environmentKind, entries })` and `getWatchlistSourceStates(db, sourceIds)` are frozen in `packages/db/src/repositories/watchlists.ts` (stubs for `api-watchlists`), with `DB_WATCHLIST_READS`; `createInMemoryWatchlists(entries, sources)` in `test-support/fakes.ts` implements them (ranked by shared keys, ties by source then entry id; sandbox-only sources left out for production; `truncated` at the cap).
10. **Provider kinds (C83).** `PHASE6_PROVIDER_KINDS` (`adverseMedia`, `emailIntelligence`, `phoneIntelligence`, `addressVerification`, `cryptoExposure`) are read and bound by the worker only, unbound unless named; `PROVIDER_NAMES` gains `gdelt-doc`, `hibp`, `twilio-lookup`, `smarty`. Each factory (`create<Kind>Provider(name, options: ReportProviderOptions)`) gets `{ credentials, outboundHttp, cache, rateLimiter, clock }` from `createProviderBindings`' new `reportProviders` option.
11. **Settings per process.** The API and the worker read the report limits (`OPENVERIFY_RATE_LIMIT_REPORTS_PER_MINUTE` 1-1,000,000, `OPENVERIFY_REPORTS_MAX_RECURRING_PER_ENVIRONMENT` 1-10,000,000, `OPENVERIFY_REPORTS_MAX_MEDIA_RUNS_PER_DAY` 1-17,280, the whole GDELT install budget); the API's `reports` rate-limit rule takes the first. Only the worker (and the CLIs, which read as the worker) reads and checks `OPENVERIFY_WATCHLIST_REFRESH`, `OPENVERIFY_WATCHLIST_REMOVED_RETENTION_DAYS` (1-3,650), `OPENVERIFY_EU_FSF_TOKEN`, `OPENVERIFY_WIKIDATA_USER_AGENT` (8-300 printable characters), `OPENVERIFY_WIKIDATA_SPARQL_URL` (https), the four PEP rules (years 1-100, months 1-600), `OPENVERIFY_GDELT_ACKNOWLEDGE_DISCLOSURE` and each adapter's credentials, these only while their kind is bound to their adapter in either environment kind. Binding `gdelt-doc` without the acknowledgement stops the worker naming the variable. The EU token, the HIBP key, the Lookup key secret and the Smarty token are secrets: a `change-me` value is refused in production. `ApiDependencies.reportLimits` carries the limits to the report routes.
12. **`.env.example` and compose.** `.env.example` declares the Phase 6 settings at their defaults and binds the report sandbox providers for sandbox environments; the EU token is left commented out, never with a value. Compose gives the report limits and `OPENVERIFY_WATCHLIST_SOURCES_DISABLED` to the API, the worker and `migrate` (`x-report-environment`), and the list sync settings and report provider bindings to the worker only (`x-watchlist-sync-environment`). Postgres starts with `log_parameter_max_length=0` and `log_parameter_max_length_on_error=0` (C73.8), and `POSTGRES_TEST_COMMAND` (`@openverify/db/testing`) starts a Testcontainers Postgres the same way: every Phase 6 Testcontainers test uses it. The three `OPENVERIFY_TWILIO_LOOKUP_*` credentials are not yet passed to the worker in compose (see 17).
13. **Queues (C74.1, C74.4, C85.4).** `jobs/report-queues.ts`: `report-run` (job id the run id, `{ environmentId, reportId, runId }`, 5 attempts with 5 s exponential backoff) and `report-rescreen` (job id `rescreen-<sync id>`, `{ syncId }`, 3 attempts with 10 s backoff), both `removeOnComplete` and `removeOnFail`; `createReportRunQueue` and `createReportRescreenQueue` validate the ids and open their BullMQ queue on first use. `ApiDependencies.reportRuns` is the API's producer. The worker runs a `report-run` worker (its concurrency) and a `report-rescreen` worker (concurrency 1) when `startWorkers` gets `reports: WorkerReportOptions`; `reportRunProcessor` tells `processReportRun` whether this is the last attempt and maps a `PermanentJobError` to `UnrecoverableError`; the fencing, release and final failure are the framework's (`api-reports`).
14. **Repeatable jobs.** `phase6RepeatableJobs(deps, reports)` (beside `phase5RepeatableJobs`, scheduled after it) registers `report-sweep` every 15 s whenever the worker runs reports, calling `runReportSweep({ db, clock, logger, runs, rescreens, limits })` (`api-reports`), and `watchlists-refresh` every 15 minutes only with `OPENVERIFY_WATCHLIST_REFRESH=true`, calling `runWatchlistsRefresh({ db, clock, logger, outboundHttp, watchlists, rescreens })` (`api-watchlists`). Step (c) of the sweep calls `recoverPendingRescreens({ db, clock, logger, rescreens })` from `runReportSweep`.
15. **Package scripts.** `watchlists:sync`, `watchlists:rekey` and `pep:sync` run `scripts/{watchlists-sync,watchlists-rekey,pep-sync}.ts` like the other CLIs; the stubs change nothing and exit 1 until `api-watchlists` builds them.
16. **Routes (C72.1-C72.3).** Frozen with their contract schemas and scopes, every handler answering 501: `POST|GET /reports`, `GET|DELETE /reports/:id` (DELETE answers 200 with the redacted report), `POST /reports/:id/{add-tag,remove-tag,set-tags}` (`reports:write`), `/dismiss`, `/review` (`reports:review`), `/pause`, `/resume`, `/run` (`reports:write`, 429 documented), `GET /reports/:id/history`; `POST|GET /report-templates`, `GET|PATCH /report-templates/:id`; `POST|GET /report-template-versions`, `GET|PATCH /report-template-versions/:id`, `POST /report-template-versions/:id/publish` (`report-templates:publish`); and, registered in `dashboardAdminRoutes` behind an operator session, `GET /dashboard/reports` (the filters of `listReportsQuerySchema`), `GET /dashboard/reports/:id` (`reports:read`) and `GET /dashboard/watchlist-sources` (`watchlists:read`). The OpenAPI snapshot holds the 25 paths.
17. **Open for `integration-6a`.** `infra/tests/compose.test.sh` refuses any `^OPENVERIFY_TWILIO_` variable in the worker, which the plan's Lookup names also match. Narrow that check to the API's SMS variables (`OPENVERIFY_TWILIO_(ACCOUNT_SID|API_KEY_SID|API_KEY_SECRET|MESSAGING_SERVICE_SID|FROM)$`), require the worker to receive the three Lookup variables and the API none, then add them to `x-worker-provider-environment`. The compose test should also list the Phase 6 variables of `.env.example` and the report provider kinds (`ADVERSE_MEDIA`, `EMAIL_INTELLIGENCE`, `PHONE_INTELLIGENCE`, `ADDRESS_VERIFICATION`, `CRYPTO_EXPOSURE`), and check the Postgres command (C73.8).
18. **Existing tests changed with the plan:** the kind lists of `providers/index.test.ts`, `providers/phase5b-providers.test.ts` and `config.test.ts` (C83); the dependency keys of `services/context.test.ts` (`reportLimits`, `reportRuns`); the registered plugins of `routes/server/index.test.ts`; the four upgrade tests (`migration-v3`, `migration-v5`, `migration-0017`, `migration-0018`), which now seed their earlier schemas with `createTestInquiryOnEarlierSchema`, because the repositories name every column of the current schema and 0020 added `report_config`; and, in checkpoints 1 and 2, those listed in the TDD record (the redaction fakes, `subject-ids.test.ts`, `DASHBOARD_SETTINGS_DEFAULTS`, the blind-index rebuild lines).

## Workflow 6A Stage 2 amendments

Recorded by `integration-6a` on 2026-10-28 from the four Stage 2 agents' `contractDeviations`; each agent's evidence is in its `docs/tdd/phase6-<agent>.tdd.md`. Binding for 6B. Where Stage 3 or Stage 4 resolved an item, the resolution is named.

### watchlist-ingest

1. **Change signals need a column (C73.3, C77.4).** The sync can skip a download only when it keeps the last signal: `watchlist_sources.last_change_signal text NULL` (a `ChangeSignal.token`, never a URL), written with `last_checked_at` on every `applied` or `unchanged` sync. `last_published_at` cannot stand in: the UK file is dated to the day and a CDN may serve a day-old file. **Open** (see the Stage 4 amendments): until the column exists every check downloads and compares the SHA-256 (C76.2 step 4), which is correct, only slower.
2. **The PEP import format** (C76.2, C89.2) is `openverify/wikidata-pep-answers/v1`: the query service's SPARQL JSON answers keyed by question (`positions` per class QID, `persons`, optional `relatives`), read by `pepImportSource` with the live readers' variables. fixtures-6's wide answer (`PEP_SPARQL_VARIABLES`) is not an import document; Stage 4 added `pepImportAnswers` and the committed `wikidata-pep.synthetic.answers.json`.
3. **A relative's class** (C80.3, F12). The frozen record has no class outside `positions[]`: a relative has `positions: []` and `relations: [{ relation, entity_id: <PEP QID> }]`, and a consumer reads the class from the linked PEP. A class on relations would be a record amendment.
4. **Open-ended tenures (C80.2), ours:** a statement without a start date is not current (`end: unknown`, `in_office: no`); a future start reads `in_office: unknown` and counts as current for retention; a partial date is read at the latest instant its precision allows; a successor's start counts only on the same position, and a successor stated without a value ends the tenure at an unknown date.
5. **Head retention (C80.4, decision 9), ours:** the 60 months apply only to a national position (a deciding head row whose P1001 jurisdiction has an ISO 3166-1 code); regional heads and mayors keep 12. Single-holder status never applies to a position that is itself a class row's QID.
6. **`pep-positions.json` (C80.2), ours:** 23 classes; chief of police is class 2 (Wikidata does not tell a national chief from a city's); state-owned enterprises (class 3), central-bank boards (class 1) and international organisations (class 2) are reached through P2389; senior civil servants without a class of their own and other senior staff of international bodies are not covered (the PEP page says so). Sources are `fatf-2013` or `authored`; the EU national function lists were not read.
7. **PEP names (C80.5, H14), ours:** every label and alias kept, labels first; the `en` label primary, else `mul`, else the first; one-word aliases weak; at most 500 names; a label over 300 characters is dropped rather than refusing the source; a position label is cut to 200.
8. **Caps on real publications (C77.1, F12).** A record over a frozen cap refuses the source (`refused_schema`, previous data kept), except `remarks`, which are cut to 2,000 characters. Watch the first real syncs for an identifier over 100 characters or an entry over 500 names.
9. **What the record cannot hold (F12):** OFAC alias types, OFAC list membership within Consolidated, OFAC relationships and the UN's `VERSIONNUM`. Each would be a record amendment (owner).
10. **Parser mappings, ours:** every parser adds `countries[{ role: address }]` per address country; UN names carry no part roles; UK wallets carry the ticker `unlabelled`; an OFAC circa year is a range a year either side; an OFAC period that is not a whole day, month or year is a range with its first year as value; the EU's first `nameAlias` is primary; EU identification codes are mapped explicitly.
11. **Formats not checked against a real file:** the EU identification, address and date attributes follow the research (the XSD sits behind EU Login), and the UN shape follows research §2.2. The OFAC and UK parsers follow their XSDs (SHA-256 `0c56f9f2…2b97f` and `073d5db3…108a`, read 2026-10-27). Watch the first EU and UN syncs for `format` or `schema` refusals.
12. **No SPARQL query was ever sent** (robots.txt disallows `/sparql` for crawlers). The positions query pages with `ORDER BY ?statement LIMIT … OFFSET …` over `P279*`; broad classes may near the service's 60 s limit, and `pageSize` and `classes` can be narrowed.

### watchlist-matcher

1. **Existing test changed (rule 12):** `packages/watchlist/src/stubs.test.ts` asserted that the `keysVersion` stub throws; it now asserts the implemented format (`f0375a20`).
2. **Licence row:** rigour `resources/names/org_types.yml` @ `607fa82` (MIT; 433 forms, 1,960 phrases in the committed `legal-forms.json`). Added to `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` in Stage 4.
3. **C78.2 Arabic:** §6.C gives only the MRZ column; the matcher also reads a plain Latin reading of each row (ours), which is lossy and feeds keys and capped scores only. **Cyrillic:** the base column of §6.B; the bracketed language exceptions are not applied (neither side states a language; the variant groups carry those spellings); Ь is omitted; letters the table does not list go through `any-ascii` and make the token lossy.
4. **Lossy equality (C78.4):** scored at the lossy cap of 0.8 under `icao_transliteration_equal`; every preset holds that rule, so the choice changes no result.
5. **Titles, particles and generic words (C78.1)** are lists of our own (35, 28 and 28; provisional). C58's libpostal personal titles are not used for screening (they include name parts). Generational suffixes: C58's plus roman II to IV.
6. **Legal forms (C79.2):** read at both ends of a name, compared by generic tag, else by form id, with C58's company types as the fallback, weighing 0.5. A Han legal form written without a space is not detected.
7. **Variant groups (C78.3):** `data/variant-groups.json` (gitignored), format `openverify/variant-groups/v1`; Latin forms in both C58 renderings, other scripts in their original form; forms in C58's `other` script or in two scripts dropped, and groups left with fewer than 2 forms. `SHIPPED_VARIANT_GROUPS_SHA256 = 529216c9…7324`; another digest loads as `unexpected`, so a held-out build never passes as the shipped one.
8. **`keysVersion` format:** `names-1;identifiers-1;crypto-1;groups-<sha256>`; `matcherVersion` prefixes `scorer-1;`.
9. **Identifiers (C78.3, C79.2), ours:** a failing check keeps the value with `check: invalid`; a BIC compares by 8 characters, a website by host (lower case, no `www.`, IDN as punycode); a business registration number takes the address country; an identifier matches when kind and value are equal and the countries agree or either side lacks one.
10. **Crypto (C79.3), ours:** the canonical form never depends on the checksum; `chain` is `unknown` for EVM without a query chain id, for CashAddr and the raw formats; `litecoin:` and `dogecoin:` URIs are stripped too.
11. **Qualifiers and rules (C78.4), ours:** the two disjoint-date qualifiers add up (−0.40); `year_plus_minus_one` allows a year either side and ranges are widened by a year; a month-precision disagreement has no qualifier; `birthdate-filter: off` ignores negative date qualifiers only; identifier and weak-alias matches ignore `min-score` and the strict filters; the 0.70 floor applies to every preset; the exact preset matches on `namesExactlyEqual`; typos need a Levenshtein distance of 1, the shorter token at least the budget's length and Jaro-Winkler ≥ 0.88; a pair of initials weighs 0.5; a suffix on one side weighs 0; a PEP position's country always counts as an entry country.
12. **Coverage (C78.5)** needs each source's scripts. Stage 3 took them from the candidates loaded in the run (api-reports amendment 6); a source without evidence counts as Latin only, which can only flag more.
13. **Retrieval notes for the DF limit:** 2% of a small source is below 1, so the limit has a floor of 1; weak aliases are looked up by `normalised` equality; the identifier country is left to the matcher. Stage 3 built `retrieveCandidates` this way.

### fixtures-6

1. **SPARQL variables (C80.1):** the synthetic wide answer binds our `PEP_SPARQL_VARIABLES`. The import format of watchlist-ingest amendment 2 supersedes it for imports; the wide answer stays for answer-shape tests.
2. **The committed PEP answer's classes were fictional** (`Q-test-class-pep-<n>`). Stage 4 generated `wikidata-pep.synthetic.answers.json` with real `pep-positions.json` class QIDs (`SANDBOX_PEP_CLASS_QIDS`: 1 `Q2285706`, 2 `Q121998`, 3 `Q270791`, 4 `Q30185`), kept by the drift test.
3. **The held-out generator is not re-exported** from `@openverify/watchlist/testing` (H21): evaluation-6 imports `packages/watchlist/src/testing/held-out/index.ts` by path.
4. **Group ids** are the text after `' => '` on a rigour line, as the build filter reads them; both sides select held-out groups with `isHeldOutGroup(id, seed)` (V12).
5. **The monitored person's query has no birth date,** so version 3's new birth date lapses its dismissal instead of a date penalty losing the entry (C89.2).
6. **Twilio Lookup v2 states neither prepaid nor porting:** the stub router answers the C82.4 endings 02 and 04 as `mobile` and 05 as `unknown`; the sandbox provider answers them directly.
7. **Smarty ending 1002** answers no candidate (`[]`), as Smarty does for an unmatched address.
8. **The committed sandbox lists** hold 64 generated filler entries (and 55 PEP fillers), so the 2% key rule works on them, and no Han name (Han names from common characters read as real names, C89.1).

### risk-logic

1. **Builder location:** the dev-only builder of the generated tables is `packages/checks/src/reports/build-data.ts` (run directly); it was not moved to `packages/checks/scripts/`.
2. **The C82.1 build test is CI-safe:** it checks `email-tables-check.json` (the SHA-256 of the free-mail and relay lists and the disposable list's pin); any edit fails until the builder runs again after a real overlap check.
3. **Free mail** is consumer domains anyone can sign up to for free (237 domains, 39 providers); ISP and paid mailboxes are left out.
4. **The email band is also `null`** when a check that could reach a worse band could not run: no resolver (`provider_not_configured`), a failed breach provider (`execution_error`), an undated breach, the disposable list missing, or MX exchanges without host names (`undetermined`). A definite `low` always stands; RDAP answering `unavailable` does not block.
5. **Email facts the contract cannot say** go in the sealed details: an SPF `redirect=` (`spf-redirect`), DMARC `t=y`, where DMARC was found and how many queries it took. A domain age never asked (free mail, relay, disposable) is `null` with `undetermined`.
6. **SMTPUTF8 local parts** are valid syntax for the report (RFC 6531); only the domain and role are read.
7. **Phone types with no row** (`PREPAID`, `PAYPHONE`, `OTHER`, a valid number without a plan type) leave the level `null` (`undetermined`) unless SIM swap reaches `high`. **Proposed contract amendment (owner):** a reason `prepaid` (medium / flag, as the public mapping), and decide `payphone` and `other_line_type`. A bound network line type replaces the plan's even for an invalid number; `phone-is-valid` stays `false`.
8. **The phone port shape** is `PhoneIntelligenceLookup` (what was checked, and Lookup's full line-type vocabulary); the SIM swap and port confidences are ours (0.8, 0.6, 0.4). Stage 3 adopted it (api-providers amendment 2).
9. **Address designators:** there is no `general_delivery` value (only in the details; proposed contract amendment); a box after a military unit counts as military; `cmra-hint`'s confidence (0.6) is in the details.
10. **Media (C81.3):** the GDELT terms are English only; a title is read in its language's lexicon (all four when unknown; `language_not_covered` otherwise); German terms ending in `*` match compounds; `bank` and `group` are generic words; two or more generic words need the country; country evidence is the source country or the country's name in en, es, fr or de; no category holds criminal-procedure words.
11. **Contract tie:** checks cannot import contracts, so the vocabularies are copies; Stage 3 added the alignment test, and Stage 4 rebuilt the email, phone, address and media golden fixtures from the jobs with a tie test.
12. **The MX for reports needs real host names** (C56's cache keeps `exchange`); Stage 3 reads live names through an in-memory report cache.
13. **`email-domain` is capped at 253 characters** in the contract while C55.8 allows 255 octets; an owner or contracts decision (cap, or `insufficient_input`) is open.
14. **Licence row:** `us-zip-prefixes.json` under libaddressinput (CC BY 4.0); added to `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` in Stage 4.

## Workflow 6A Stage 3 amendments

Recorded by `integration-6a` on 2026-10-28 from the three Stage 3 agents' `contractDeviations`; their evidence is in `docs/tdd/phase6-api-{reports,watchlists,providers}.tdd.md`. Binding for 6B.

### api-reports

1. **A duplicate run (orchestrator incident, 2026-10-27 19:43-19:55):** a second api-reports copy worked in the same paths; its commits (`290f96f6`, `e6babfff`, `15193a98`: the watchlist source catalog; `988386b0`, `79ca54ac`: report templates and versions, with `listWatchlistSourceRows`) are api-reports' and recorded in its TDD record (slices T1 and T2).
2. **The seeded templates (C71.3)** were built by neither api-reports nor api-watchlists (each read the file as the other's). Stage 4 built `seedReportTemplates`.
3. **Existing tests changed (rule 12):** `jobs/report-types/index.test.ts` asserts the unbuilt rule through `unbuiltReportTypeJob('watchlist')`; foundation-6's 501 expectations in `routes/server/report-routes.test.ts` and `routes/dashboard/report-routes.test.ts` were replaced as each handler landed.
4. **Crypto keys version (C78.3), ours:** `crypto-address-watchlist` compares only the crypto normaliser part of a source's `keys_version` (`crypto-1`), so it screens without the variant groups; the name types compare the whole version and error `matcher_data_unavailable` without the groups.
5. **The exposure block (C83, F19), ours:** indirect exposure is served with `hops: 1` (at least one hop), `risk-score` is always `null`, `direct-sanctioned` is a direct share in `sanctioned_entity`; unbound gives `exposure: null` with `provider_not_configured`, an unrated address `undetermined`, a provider error `execution_error`, and the screening result stands. **Proposed port amendment (owner, `ports.ts`):** an optional `hops` on the rated answer.
6. **Coverage scripts (C78.5), ours:** the frozen reads expose no per-source script list, so `sourceScripts` comes from the scripts of the candidates loaded in the run; without evidence a source is Latin only. **Proposed:** a frozen read of the distinct `watchlist_names.script` per source.
7. **Retrieval per name (C78.3), ours:** one `retrieveCandidates` call carries every name's keys and the identifiers with the first name's trigram and weak-alias path; each additional name gets its own call with keys `[]`; candidates are merged and `truncated` is set when any call was cut.
8. **Completion caps (C72.5):** `createInquiryReportsInTx` holds no limits (`ReportHookDeps` is `{ events, clock }`), so the initial run of a completion report applies the recurring and media caps in the worker: beyond either it records `recurring_limit` and drops the schedule. The plan placed the check at creation.
9. **Completion query sources (C84.2), ours:** no identification number is taken from the ID extraction (it does not say which type it is); account fields are read with the inquiry fields' snake_case keys; the business types read `business_name` and `registration_number`, the crypto type the account's `crypto_address`; a value the type's served query schema refuses is dropped; `query-sources` records the first additional name's source for `additional-names`.
10. **Redaction writes no `redact` action (C73.2):** the frozen `redactReportInTx` options carry no actor; the redaction service's audit entry records it. **Proposed:** an optional actor on `ReportRedactionOptions`.
11. **Stored-copy clearing (C73.5):** `forgetReportCopies` (`repositories/reports.ts`) duplicates the SQL of the private `forgetStoredResponses` (`repositories/redaction.ts`). **Follow-up:** export and reuse it.
12. **Index rebuild (C73.4):** the crypto index is taken from the canonical address the run sealed in `reports.result` (`canonical-address`), each match's indexes from its sealed `entry-key` and `evidence-input`: stored values only (P10, F13).
13. **Review and dismissal (C75), ours:** a dismissal without `entity-id`, or an `all_records` review, with nothing open answers 404 `report_match_not_found`; a media dismissal with `dismiss-type: entity` dismisses every open article; review and dismissal also apply the C74.5 resume of a report paused on a match; the ignore list's `user` is the actor's `usr_` or `key_` id.
14. **The missing-variant-group log (C78.3, F14)** belonged in `worker.ts`; Stage 4 added `logMatcherDataAtStart`.
15. **API-log masking (P04), a clarification:** stored `api_logs` keep the bodies as sent; masking happens when they are read without `pii:view` (an API key sees PII unless it is PII-masked).
16. **Open (followups for 6B `fix-api`):** the C74.6 races not yet on Postgres: API creation against account redaction; completion-hook creation against consolidation and against account redaction; the initial run's account link against consolidation; re-screen against redaction; a budget-waiting run enqueued again within 15 s of `not_before`. An exhausted job leaving a live worker's run `running`, and the eleventh claim, are tested on PGlite only.

### api-watchlists

1. **The `last_change_signal` column** (watchlist-ingest amendment 1) does not exist; every check downloads, then compares the SHA-256. The exact change is in the Stage 4 amendments.
2. **`seedReportTemplates`** was left to api-reports (api-reports amendment 2); Stage 4 built it.
3. **`seed:dev` does not enqueue the re-screen itself** (the seed opens no Valkey connection): its syncs stay `rescreen_state = pending` and the worker's sweep enqueues them within 15 s (C74.3 (c)). A fresh seed holds no recurring report, so that job only marks the sync done.
4. **The re-screen's forward screening (C74.4), ours:** the frozen subject read returns each report's query, not its configuration, so the re-screen screens each candidate with the most permissive configuration a template can hold (`custom` at 0.70 with nicknames, variants and the `high` typo budget; filters off; weak aliases screened; every entity kind; no PEP class filter). A report it finds that its own configuration would not match gets a run that finds nothing new and emits nothing.
5. **The refresh tick (C76.2), ours:** a source is due when `last_checked_at` is null or older than `refresh_interval_hours`, so a failing source is asked again every 15 minutes (a back-off is a follow-up); a source keyed by another version is re-keyed on the tick even when not due; `WatchlistsRefreshResult` gains `rekeyed`, `WatchlistsRefreshDeps` the optional `connect`, `matcherData` and `manifest`.
6. **Two C76.2 Testcontainers tests built differently:** "two apply transactions with the older committing last" cannot happen (the session lock makes the second `busy`), so the rollback re-check is tested sequentially with the audited override; "two partial removals that together pass 10%" cannot compound (each publication is diffed against the live set under the lock), so the per-publication guard is tested under the lock.
7. **"Valkey down when a sync commits"** (C74.4) is covered on PGlite with a failing producer, not on Testcontainers.
8. **The C78.7 fix:** `analyzeWatchlistTables(db)` runs ANALYZE on the list tables inside the sync's transaction after the first 500 entries and each fourfold growth, and before the key-frequency rebuild; a 100,000-name import went from over ten minutes to 71-107 s. The gate reads the least disturbed of three rounds of 300 subjects (p95 61-75 ms against 100 ms), with empty variant groups.
9. **Existing test changed (rule 12):** `seed/dev-seed.test.ts` expected the reconciliation stub's zero counts; it expects `{ sources: 8, enabled: 7, retired: 0 }`.
10. **The apps image** must carry `packages/watchlist/fixtures/openverify-sandbox*.json`, `packages/watchlist/dist/testing` and `data/variant-groups.json`; Stage 4 did (F14).
11. **The PEP import** reads the answers format of watchlist-ingest amendment 2, not the wide synthetic answer; Stage 4 generated the answers file.

### api-providers

1. **Three helper files** beside the jobs in `jobs/report-types/` (owner api-providers): `provider-reports.ts` (the last-attempt rule, `recordedFailure`, `providerOutcome`, the OTP confirmation, `erroredResult`), `media-screening.ts` (`runMediaScreening`, `mediaQueryProblem`, `MEDIA_MAX_WAIT_MS`, `UNDETERMINED_LANGUAGE`) and `provider-testing.ts` (tests only).
2. **Proposed port amendments (owner, `ports.ts`):** `AdverseMediaSearch` gains `environmentId?` (whose GDELT share the request takes, V09) and `names?` (every screened name); the jobs send them today through `AdverseMediaSearchRequest`. `PhoneIntelligenceResult` gains `lookup` (risk-logic's `PhoneIntelligenceLookup`), because a date or null cannot tell "no swap" from "not checked"; both phone providers answer `PhoneIntelligenceAnswer` today.
3. **An article without a language** is served with ISO 639-2 `und` (the contract's `language` is not nullable); risk-logic reads all four lexicons for it. The alternative is a contracts amendment making `language` nullable.
4. **Articles, ours:** one that cannot be served in the public shape (a URL not http(s) or over 2,048 characters, a domain outside 1-253 characters, an empty title) is dropped before screening; the article key is `article:` plus 32 hex digits of the URL's SHA-256; the content hash is the SHA-256 of `["openverify/media-article-content/v1", url, stored title]`.
5. **The media wait (C81.2), ours:** 24 h is measured from the run id's time; a country re-query's articles are merged by key; an empty re-query keeps the search truncated.
6. **Retries (C69.4, C74.1), ours:** before the last attempt (`run.attempt >= 5`) a retryable DNS or provider failure is thrown so BullMQ retries; on the last attempt the partial types record the field `null` with `execution_error` and the report is `ready`, and the media types end `errored` with `provider_error`; a defect is always thrown.
7. **The RDAP cache question (Stage 1a amendment 4), decided:** the registered-domain mode writes no email domain's date to Valkey; dates stay in process memory per environment for a day, and the report's DNS answers (MX names, TXT, A, AAAA) are in memory only (1 h; 5 min for NXDOMAIN and NODATA).
8. **The email job never reads C56's masked MX cache;** NODATA falls back to the A and AAAA count (the implicit MX).
9. **The creation-time 422 for media names** (C81.2, C81.3) was a missing hook in api-reports' create path; Stage 4 added it.
10. **A completion query member that fails the type's served schema** (a typed phone number not in E.164, for example) is dropped by the completion hook (api-reports amendment 9), so the report can be served.
11. **Sandbox (C82.4):** a business-adverse-media sandbox name needs a second distinctive word (`docs/checks/sandbox-providers.md`).
12. **Thresholds served (ADR 0002 §3f), ours:** email `min-domain-age-days` and `young-domain-days`; phone `sim-swap-high-hours`, `sim-swap-medium-hours`, `sim-swap-low-hours` and `ported-recent-days`; media `max-article-age-days` and `max-records`; address `null`. `provider` names the provider that answered; RDAP and DNS are named in the sealed details.
13. **OTP confirmation (P05), ours:** `not_linked` for any origin but `inquiry_completion`; otherwise only a `confirmed` challenge whose destination equals the query (an email trimmed and lower-cased, a phone number exact in E.164) gives `confirmed`.

## Workflow 6A Stage 4 amendments (integration-6a)

Recorded by `integration-6a` on 2026-10-28; the evidence is in `docs/tdd/phase6-integration-6a.tdd.md`. Binding for 6B.

**Built here (missing hooks and integration defects):**
1. **`seedReportTemplates` (C71.3).** One published `Default <type>` template per type in each environment (`DEFAULT_REPORT_TEMPLATE_NAMES`); the sandbox list types pin only the enabled sandbox-only lists of their kind, production ones every enabled real source (`defaultSearchLists`); each seeded config passes the publish rules; a later run finds a template by type and name; an empty list is skipped and counted. `seed:dev` now reports `{ created: 18, existing: 0, skipped: 0 }` on a fresh database.
2. **Media names at creation (C81.2, C81.3, H13).** `POST /reports` calls `mediaQueryProblem` for both media types and answers 422 `report_query_invalid` at `/data/attributes/query/term` (or `name-first`) for a name with no letter or digit left or a business name that is one generic word once its legal form is stripped. No schema changed.
3. **The missing variant groups are said once at worker start** (`logMatcherDataAtStart` in `worker.ts`, C78.3, F14): a warning naming `python3 scripts/fetch_data.py` and `pnpm --filter @openverify/watchlist build:variant-groups`, or the keys version at `info` when loaded. Loading it there also spares the first report run the load.
4. **`crypto-address-watchlist` is `ga`** (C69.1, Stage 1a amendment 11): its C79.3 vectors pass. Every other type stays `beta`; the core registry test that every type is `beta` now excludes it (rule 12).
5. **`@openverify/node` `accounts.retrieve(id, { include: 'reports' })`** sends `?include=reports` and answers `accountIncludeDocumentSchema` (Stage 1a amendment 21). The second parameter was `RequestOptions` before; the only callers passed none.
6. **The golden provider-backed reports** (`contracts/fixtures/api/report-{email-address,phone-number,address-lookup,adverse-media,business-adverse-media}.json`) are rebuilt from the jobs over the sandbox providers and tied to them by `apps/api/src/jobs/report-types/report-golden-fixtures.test.ts` (`OPENVERIFY_WRITE_REPORT_FIXTURES=1` rewrites them). The business media query is now "Sandbox-Media Quarrystone Trading Ltd": a sandbox business name needs a second distinctive word.
7. **The PEP import answers** (`pepImportAnswers`, `SANDBOX_PEP_CLASS_QIDS` in `@openverify/watchlist/testing`; `packages/watchlist/fixtures/wikidata-pep.synthetic.answers.json`, kept by the drift test) in the `openverify/wikidata-pep-answers/v1` format, with real class QIDs. The wide `wikidata-pep.synthetic.sparql.json` stays for answer-shape tests and is not an import.
8. **`build:variant-groups`** (`packages/watchlist/package.json`) runs `scripts/build-variant-groups.mjs` with the flags it needs; the e2e stack runs it after `fetch_data.py`.
9. **`THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md`** gains the rigour legal-forms row and the libaddressinput US ZIP prefixes row.

**e2e (C89, F15):**
10. **Provider bindings:** `e2e/src/env.ts` binds `adverseMedia`, `emailIntelligence`, `phoneIntelligence`, `addressVerification` and `cryptoExposure` to `sandbox` for sandbox environments and none to production, and sets empty every vendor credential, the EU token, the Wikidata contact and `OPENVERIFY_WATCHLIST_SOURCES_DISABLED`, with `OPENVERIFY_GDELT_ACKNOWLEDGE_DISCLOSURE` and `OPENVERIFY_WATCHLIST_REFRESH` `false`, whatever the shell exports.
11. **The four specs** (each budgeted 90 s): `report-watchlist` (0.8 s), `report-monitoring` (31 s), `report-inquiry` (13 s), `report-risk` (5 s), with `e2e/tests/support/reports.ts` (`SANDBOX_LISTS`, `seededTemplateId`, `waitForReport`, `historyOf`, `runsOf`, `waitForCompletedRuns`, `assertInFixture`, `watchlistFixture`) and `importWatchlist` (`support/commands.ts`). The e2e package does not depend on `@openverify/watchlist`: the specs name the synthetic values and check each against the committed fixture file. `report-monitoring` imports versions 2 and 3 of `openverify-sandbox` into the run's stack, and `report-risk` imports the PEP answers into `wikidata-pep`; later specs of the same shard see those lists. Choices: `report-inquiry` uses the inquiry's typed name (no government ID step), so `query-sources` reads `inquiry_fields`; its second inquiry types an address that no code confirmed, so its email report is `not_confirmed`.
12. **Suite time (F15).** The full-stack suite is 86 tests: shard 1/2 43 in 4.0 min, shard 2/2 43 in 6.5 min, about 9 minutes of tests in all (the four specs add about 50 s); the mocked suite 69 in 19 s. The CI e2e job took 14 of its 70 minutes at the end of Phase 5 and Playwright's `globalTimeout` is 45, so neither limit needed raising and the job is not sharded. Locally, run `--shard=1/2` and `2/2` (each under 10 minutes).

**CI and images:**
13. **`unit-api` allows 45 minutes** (it used 19.7 of 20 in CI run 40540937935 with 3,786 tests; Phase 6 has 4,471, 13.5 minutes locally under load). Sharding the job with blob reports and a merge job is the better follow-up. Locally the API suite runs as two shards (`vitest run --coverage --shard=N/2 --reporter=blob`, then `vitest run --merge-reports=<dir> --coverage`, which applies the 80% gate to the whole); about 9 minutes in all.
14. **The CI e2e job needs no new step:** it already fetches every `data/manifest.json` file (the rigour file among them) and caches `data/*`, and the e2e global setup now builds the variant groups.
15. **The apps image (F14)** re-includes `data/variant-groups.json` and checks it against `SHIPPED_VARIANT_GROUPS_SHA256` (`529216c9…7324`); build it first with `build:variant-groups`. `apps-image-context.test.sh` checks the variant groups, the sandbox lists, the PEP classes, the legal forms, the report tables and the manifest are in the context, the unfiltered rigour file is out, and the Dockerfile's digest is the shipped one.
16. **Compose (foundation-6 item 17):** the worker receives `OPENVERIFY_TWILIO_LOOKUP_{ACCOUNT_SID,API_KEY_SID,API_KEY_SECRET}`; `compose.test.sh` keeps only the SMS settings (`OPENVERIFY_TWILIO_(ACCOUNT_SID|API_KEY_SID|API_KEY_SECRET|MESSAGING_SERVICE_SID|FROM)`) from the worker and migrate, keeps every report credential and list setting from the API and migrate, requires the report limits and the disabled sources in every process that runs reports, the report kinds per process, the Phase 6 settings in `.env.example` (the EU token commented out without a value), and Postgres started with `log_parameter_max_length=0` and `log_parameter_max_length_on_error=0`, with a live probe of both (C73.8).

**Open, for 6B `fix-api` or the owner:**
17. **`watchlist_sources.last_change_signal`** (watchlist-ingest amendment 1) is not built: every check downloads in full and compares the SHA-256. Exact change: migration `0021` adds `last_change_signal text NULL`; `recordWatchlistSourceCheck` and `recordWatchlistSourceApplied` (`packages/db/src/repositories/watchlists.ts`) write `ChangeSignal.token`; `syncWatchlistSource` asks the source's change signal before the download (`ofacChangeSignal` from the export headers or `/changes/latest`, `euChangeSignal` from the RSS feed, `ukChangeSignal` from the GOV.UK content API, through the list client with its manifest deadline) and stops with `unchanged`, setting `last_checked_at`, when `changeSignalMoved(row.last_change_signal, signal)` is false; a failed signal check falls back to the download. Tests on MockAgent answers from `@openverify/watchlist/testing`'s signal writers.
18. **The missing C74.6 races** of api-reports amendment 16, less API creation against account redaction, which Stage 4 added on Postgres (`report-races.integration.test.ts`, two tests): completion-hook creation against consolidation and against account redaction, the initial run's account link against consolidation, re-screen against redaction, and a budget-waiting run enqueued again within 15 s of `not_before`.
19. **Proposed port and contract amendments** (owner): `hops` on the crypto exposure answer; `environmentId` and `names` on `AdverseMediaSearch`; `lookup` on `PhoneIntelligenceResult`; an actor on `ReportRedactionOptions`; a frozen per-source script read; a nullable article `language` (or keep `und`); the phone reasons `prepaid`, `payphone` and `other_line_type`; `general_delivery` in `address-designators`; the `email-domain` length cap.
20. **`forgetReportCopies`** should reuse an exported `forgetStoredResponses` (api-reports amendment 11).
21. **The refresh tick** asks a failing source again every 15 minutes; a back-off is a follow-up (api-watchlists amendment 5).

## Workflow 6B Stage 1 amendments

Recorded by the Workflow 6B Stage 3 verifier on 2026-10-28 from the `contractDeviations` of the three Stage 1 agents. Their evidence is in `docs/tdd/phase6-{dashboard-reports,dashboard-report-config,evaluation-6}.tdd.md` and `docs/calibration/watchlist-phase6.md`. Each item below is binding for Stage 4 and later phases. Where an item says "proposed", the proposal is optional: the console works as built without it.

### dashboard-reports

1. **Navigation (C86).** A "Reports" group follows "Verifications" and holds three entries: Reports (`/reports`, `reports:read`), Report templates (`/report-templates`, `report-templates:read`, the scope of `GET /report-templates`) and Watchlist sources (`/watchlist-sources`, `watchlists:read`, the scope of `GET /dashboard/watchlist-sources`). The shared mock API has no registration point: `routeApi` answers any route, and each `testing/routes/<area>.ts` module stands alone over it.
2. **The inquiry and account reports sections (C70.5).** C70.5 places the reports in the dashboard inquiry and account documents, but `dashboardInquiryDocumentSchema` has no report member and there is no dashboard account document. Each section therefore reads the console's own masked list, `GET /dashboard/reports` with `filter[inquiry-id]` or `filter[account-id]` (home or linked): at most 25 reports, newest first, with a link to the full list. That fetches no data beyond what the list serves, and the API is unchanged. Proposed for `fix-api` or the owner: carry the reports in the dashboard inquiry document.
3. **"Decided before this report finished" (C84.3).** The report resource and the list do not serve run notes. The inquiry section reads `GET /dashboard/reports/:id` once for each completion report (`origin: inquiry_completion`, at most 10 per C84.1) and looks in its runs for `inquiry_already_decided`. It never reads a report the API created. Proposed for `fix-api` or the owner: serve the latest run's notes on the report resource, or a list filter, so these per-report reads go away.
4. **Review and dismissal need `pii:view` in the console**, beside `reports:review`. This is stricter than the API, which is unchanged. Without `pii:view` the API serves the matches and the ignore list as `null`, and nobody decides on evidence hidden from them (the note editor rule of Workflow 2E). A custom role with `reports:review` and no `pii:view` reviews only through the API.
5. **A review needs a comment in the console** (C86's "reason required"). C75.5 requires one only for a false positive. The server action refuses a blank comment at `/data/attributes/comment` ("Say why you decided this."), and the dialog marks the field required. The API is unchanged.
6. **Creating a report from the console (C72.1).** The form sends `meta.auto-create-account: false` unless the operator ticks "Create a new account for this report when no account ID is given", although the public default is `true`. Only an operator with `accounts:read` can name an existing account (`account-id`, C72.2). The create page needs `reports:write` and `report-templates:read`, because it lists the active templates.
7. **Result wording (C69.4).**
   - "Match" for a report with a match.
   - "No match" only beside `ready`.
   - "Not screened" for an errored report.
   - "Match; not fully screened" for an errored report that kept matches (H01).
   - "Fields computed" for a ready email, phone or address report, which has no matches to find.
   - "Not run yet" for `pending` or `submitted`.

   The status filter offers `pending`, `ready` and `errored`, not the reserved `submitted` (decision 3). The result filter's "No match" option is labelled "No match (ready reports only)" and sends `filter[has-match]=false`.
8. **Problem words (C72.4).** `DisplayProblem` (`lib/action-state.ts`) gains an optional `code`, which `displayProblemOf` (`lib/api/errors.ts`) keeps when a problem carries a string code. `describeReportProblem` titles a refusal that carries a report problem code in words, because the API's titles are the HTTP status words. It keeps the API's detail, field errors and request id.
9. **Out links (C80.5, C81.4).** A media article and a PEP's Wikidata source are plain `<a>` links with `rel="noopener noreferrer nofollow"`, `target="_blank"` and a screen-reader "(opens in a new tab)". An article's title is only the link text: nothing is fetched, previewed or embedded. An article language of `und` reads "Undetermined" (owner decision 5 (f)), and every other code goes through `Intl.DisplayNames`.
10. **A value outside a contract list shows as its code** when the console has no words for it. Match methods have words for the matcher's `NameScoreMethod` values plus `identifier-exact`, `weak-alias` and `crypto-address-exact`, so the golden fixture's `jaro-winkler` shows as its code. Crypto exposure categories show as their codes.
11. **Existing tests changed with the plan (rule 12).** C86 adds three navigation items, so `apps/dashboard/src/components/nav-items.test.ts` (the route list, the group labels, the admin count from 13 to 16) and `nav.test.tsx` (the link count from 13 to 16) changed. No other existing test changed; the settings, errors and event-words tests only gained cases. `InquiryView` and `AccountView` gained the optional props `reports` and `reportTemplatesRead`, so their callers are unchanged.
12. **Owner decision 3 reaches the console in the same change as the contract.** When `fix-api` adds `prepaid`, `payphone` and `other_line_type` to `PHONE_RISK_REASONS`, the dashboard's `PHONE_RISK_REASON_WORDS` (a `Record` keyed by that list, in `apps/dashboard/src/lib/report-field-words.ts`) stops typechecking until it has their words. That failure is intended. The same change adds those words, and `general_delivery` to `VALUE_WORDS['address-designators']` in the same file.

### dashboard-report-config

1. **Template warnings show by id.** Each entry of `dashboardWatchlistSourcesSchema['template-warnings']` carries only `report-template-id`. The sources page shows each template by its id, links it to `/report-templates/<id>` only for a principal with `report-templates:read`, and fetches nothing more. Proposed for `fix-api` (additive): `name` and `report-type` on each warning, in the contract schema and in `watchlistSourcesDocument` (`apps/api/src/routes/dashboard/watchlist-sources.ts`, from `listReportTemplateSourceLists`), with `docs/api/openapi.json` and `contracts/fixtures/api/dashboard-watchlist-sources.json` regenerated.
2. **The GDELT attribution line is the console's own.** Adverse media is not a list source, so the sources document has no attribution line for it. The console shows `GDELT_ATTRIBUTION` (`components/report-config/source-words.ts`), worded after the adapter's `GDELT_CITATION`: "When adverse media is bound to gdelt-doc, every result cites the GDELT Project (https://www.gdeltproject.org/)". It does not claim that the adapter is bound. Proposed for `fix-api` (additive): `media.attribution` in the document, carrying the `GDELT_CITATION` text, which the console then shows instead.
3. **"Needs re-key".** The console reads `keys-current: false` on a source that has been checked as "Needs re-key" (`matcher_data_unavailable` until `watchlists:rekey`). The API also answers `false` for every source when its own process holds no matcher data, and the page cannot tell the two cases apart. Proposed for `fix-api` (additive): a top-level `matcher: { loaded: boolean, 'keys-version': string | null }`, so the console can say that the API has no variant groups instead of asking for a re-key.
4. **What the JSON editor starts from (left open by C86).** The API serves only a version's resolved configuration. The editor therefore starts from it less the keys whose value equals their default, and always keeps the pinned `search-lists`. "New draft" copies the same.
