Saving that unchanged text changes no value, and an omitted key follows its default, as C71.2 intends. Beside the editor, the saved resolved configuration lists every key with its value and default, each default marked "Provisional" with its `REPORT_CONFIG_DEFAULTS` source.
5. **Sandbox lists depend on the session's environment (left open by C86).** In a production environment a sandbox-only list reads "Sandbox list, not screened here" and is not counted as "Screenable now", because the publish rules and retrieval leave it out there (P13, C76.6). In a sandbox environment it reads "Sandbox list, never stale". The page never shows a sandbox list as stale, whatever the API's flag says (V03).
6. **Publish refusals in words (left open by C86).** `publishReportDraft` answers a 422 or 409 from `POST /report-template-versions/:id/publish` in words (`publishProblemInWords`), carried through `runAction` as an `ActionInputError`. On a 422 only, it reads the saved draft once (`GET /report-template-versions/:id`, the same scope, no PII) to name each refused `search-lists` item by the id at that place. If that read fails, the words stand without ids. A refused save names list items from the text just submitted, never from the saved draft.
7. **Scope.** Besides C86's list, detail, versions, editor and publish, the console adds "New draft" and Archive/Activate, as the inquiry template pages have. It has no form to create a template: templates are created through `POST /report-templates` or by `seed:dev`, and structured forms wait for Phase 9.
8. **Process (rule 5).** `e2e/tests/ui-report-config.spec.ts` was written after slices 1 to 3, as the acceptance spec of pages already built test-first. It has no RED step of its own and passed on its first run. This is accepted for this spec only. Every dashboard unit slice went RED then GREEN, and slices 5 and 6 fixed two defects the agent found in its own committed code.

### evaluation-6

1. **Where the benchmark helpers are gated.** The benchmark scripts are `.mjs` files with `// @ts-check` and JSDoc types, like `scripts/build-*.mjs`. `packages/watchlist/tsconfig.json` includes only `src`, and the shared Vitest config collects only `src/**`. So the helpers' tests run as `benchmark:test` (`scripts/benchmark-vitest.config.mjs`, through `createVitestConfig()`, with the 80% gate over the helper modules), and they are type-checked as `benchmark:typecheck` (`scripts/benchmark-tsconfig.json`, strict `checkJs`). The package's own `test` and `typecheck` do not cover them; `lint` does. To fold them in, the owner adds `scripts` to the package tsconfig `include` and `scripts/benchmark-*.test.mjs` to its Vitest `include`.
2. **The CLIs reach into the API and the database package by relative path.** They import `syncWatchlistSource`, `reconcileWatchlistSources`, `screenNames` (through `useScreeningKit`), `watchlistReportJob`, `businessWatchlistReportJob`, `processReportRescreen`, and the repositories and testing helpers from the `apps/api` and `packages/db` sources. They load `@testcontainers/postgresql` with `createRequire` from `@openverify/db`'s development dependencies. `packages/watchlist` depends on neither package, and no dependency was added. These are benchmark tools only, never shipped.
3. **Re-screen sizes (C78.7).** The method named syncs that add 100, 1,000 and 10,000 entries. A development run took 130 s for 1,000 entries at 200,000 subjects, which puts 10,000 at about 19 minutes, beyond one 10-minute call. The measured sizes are 100, 300, 1,000 and 3,000 entries, a change recorded before the seed existed.
4. **The 2,000,000-name table (C78.7, owner decision 10).** The import goes through `syncWatchlistSource` as eight successive full publications, each 250,000 names larger than the last, because one sync of 2,000,000 names (a 450 MB normalised file) does not fit one 10-minute call. `VACUUM ANALYZE` runs before measuring. The entries table keeps the dead space of the successive syncs (3.0 GB for 412,549 entries), and a post-hoc round on a `VACUUM FULL` table is reported beside it.
5. **The benchmark lists live in the sandbox source.** Every benchmark list is imported into the synthetic source `openverify-sandbox`. The sync receives a copy of the manifest whose byte caps for that source are raised to 2 GiB; the committed cap stays 16 MiB. Screening runs for a sandbox environment kind. Matching and the retrieval SQL are the same as in production, and sandbox-only sources are never stale.
6. **What one screening is (C78.7).** One screening is the `watchlist` job's `screenNames` with the default `tolerant` configuration and the shipped variant groups: the source state, retrieval, entry loads, scoring and coverage, with nothing persisted.
   - **Tables:** names follow a Zipf law (exponent 1) over 40,000 fictional names and the rigour Latin forms, combined at random. The 100,000-name table is sanctions-like (1.65 names per entry). The 2,000,000-name table is PEP-like (4.85 names per entry, in several scripts).
   - **Subjects:** 150 listed primary names with their birth dates and 150 unlisted names drawn from the same law, screened after 20 warm-ups, in 3 rounds.
   - **Reporting:** the headline is the nearest-rank p95 pooled over the three rounds. The gate's least-disturbed-round p95 and each phase's p95 are reported too. The share of searches where the cap binds, and the would-be `result_truncated` share, come from the first round, with Wilson intervals.
7. **What the quality evaluation measures (C78.8).**
   - **Presets:** the three fixed presets `exact`, `strict` and `tolerant`, each with the type's other defaults. `custom` has no fixed constants and is not measured.
   - **Variants:** each person case is screened twice, by name only and with the listed birth date.
   - **Precision:** the matches to the expected entry over all kept matches of the case queries.
   - **Alerts per 1,000:** the customers with at least one kept match. Customers are persons only, so companies have no alert rate.
   - **Bootstrap:** each replicate resamples the listed identities and the customers, from one stream seeded by the evaluation seed, so every preset and set is paired.
   - **`own_transliteration`:** reported as a whole, and split by the script the entry is listed in.
8. **Post-hoc analyses are labelled as such.** Four analyses are outside the committed method and are marked post hoc in the report: the typo-kind breakdown, the alerts on unlisted subjects drawn from the tables' own law, the compacted 2,000,000-name round, and the Wilson upper bound for 0 of 10,000 alerts, used because the bootstrap interval is degenerate at zero events.
9. **Development conveniences that change no number.** The quality CLI's `--smoke` gives small sizes on a scratch seed, and no smoke number is published. The measure step's `--rounds n` caps the new rounds per call. The quality screen step resumes, and exits 3 when it stops at its 8-minute budget.

## Workflow 6B review amendments

Recorded by the Workflow 6B Stage 4 API fixer (`fix-api`) on 2026-10-28, for its own changes. The evidence is in `docs/tdd/phase6-review-fixes-api.tdd.md`. Each item is binding for later phases.

### fix-api

1. **The sweep is fair (R001, C74.3 (a)).** Each batch ranks the enqueueable runs within their environment and takes them by that rank: each environment's first run before any environment's second. Within an environment, runs that are not waiting on a budget (`not_before` null) come before its budget-waiting ones, then the oldest `scheduled_for`. This replaces "oldest `scheduled_for` first" install-wide. The batch sizes are unchanged.
   - Open for the owner: a per-environment cap on pending media runs at creation. It would answer 429 and needs a new problem code, because C72.5 limits recurring reports only.
2. **The XML reader measures each construct from its opening (R008, C77.2).** A text run, a tag with its quoted values, a comment, a CDATA section and an instruction are each measured from the `<` that opens them (text from the end of the last markup), across chunks. A `>` inside text, a quoted value, a comment or CDATA is an ordinary character. A DOCTYPE is refused `doctype` at `<!D`, before saxes buffers its internal subset. Any other `<!` that opens neither `<!--` nor `<![CDATA[` is refused `malformed`. The caps are unchanged.
3. **The recurring caps are read under a lock (R002, C72.5).** A recurring `POST /reports` takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended('openverify/report-recurring-caps/<environment>', 0))` first in its transaction (`lockRecurringReportCaps`), before the C73.7 account and inquiry locks. It then reads both caps there. A non-recurring creation takes no lock.
4. **A resume keeps a next run still ahead (R005, C74.5).** `nextBoundary(from, interval, now)` returns `from` when `from` is after `now`. Otherwise it returns the first `from + k` intervals after now, with `k >= 1`.
5. **Retrieval per name, revised (R007; revises Stage 3 api-reports amendment 7).** A subject with one name sends one search, as before. With additional names, the searches are:
   - the combined search: every name's keys and the identifiers, with no name path;
   - the first name's trigram and weak-alias paths, in a search keyed by that name's own keys (`queryNameKeys` of that name alone);
   - each additional name's own search, unchanged.

   An additional name's rare key therefore never skips the first name's trigram fallback.
6. **An empty publication is refused (R009, C76.2).** A publication with no entries is refused `refused_schema`, a source's first publication included, unless `--allow-mass-removal` is given (recorded once as that override).
   - Open for the owner: screening availability does not yet read a source's live entry count (`WatchlistSourceState` has none), so a source emptied by that override reads screenable.
7. **Unchanged entries are not written (evaluation-6 finding, C73.3, C76.2).** A sync writes only added and changed entries. It removes the entries its diff found gone with `markEntriesRemoved(db, { sourceId, entryIds, at })`, which replaces `markUnseenEntriesRemoved`. `watchlist_entries.last_seen_sync_id` is now the sync that last wrote the entry, not the last sync that saw it. Measured again with evaluation-6's steps, a sync adding 100 entries to 60,784 took 19.3 s against 23.6 s, with the re-screen unchanged (28.2 s, 5,401 runs).
8. **`general_delivery` joins `address-designators` (owner decision 3 in part, C82.3).** The contract enum and `ADDRESS_DESIGNATORS` list it, and it is stated, never scored. `docs/reports/address-lookup.md` gives each designator's basis.
   - Open, for one change across both fixers' paths: the phone reasons `prepaid`, `payphone` and `other_line_type`. The dashboard's `PHONE_RISK_REASON_WORDS` is a `Record` keyed by `PHONE_RISK_REASONS` (Stage 1 dashboard-reports amendment 12).
9. **Port amendments (owner decision 5 (a)-(c)), all optional.**
   - (a) **`hops`** on the rated `CryptoExposureResult`. The served distance is the provider's when it is a whole number of at least 1, else 1.
   - (b) **`environmentId` and `names`** on `AdverseMediaSearch`. api-providers' `AdverseMediaSearchRequest` is folded in and removed.
   - (c) **`lookup`** on `PhoneIntelligenceResult`. A bare answer keeps the conservative reading.

   Open for the owner, as (d): an actor on `ReportRedactionOptions`. It touches every redaction path, retention and the action log the console reads.
10. **The refresh back-off (owner decision 6 in part, K6A-11).**
    - Migration `0021_watchlist_check_backoff` adds `watchlist_sources.check_failures integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` (non-negative) and `last_failed_at timestamptz NULL`.
    - A download error or a refusal counts, through `recordWatchlistSourceError`'s `failedAt`. `source_not_configured` and `refused_matcher_data` make no request and do not count. A good check resets both columns.
    - The tick asks a failing source again after 15 minutes times two to the power of (failures - 1), capped at its refresh interval and at 24 hours.
    - Deviations from decision 6: `last_failed_at` is added, because the back-off needs the failure's time. `last_change_signal` is not added: the change signal (K6A-6) is not built, and its migration adds that column.
11. **CI shards the API suite (owner decision 9, K6A-13).** The `unit-api` job runs as a two-shard matrix (`fail-fast: false`, 30 minutes). Each shard runs `vitest run --coverage` with its own four thresholds at 0 and `--reporter=blob`. A new `unit-api-coverage` job downloads the blob reports (`actions/download-artifact` v8.0.2) and runs `vitest run --merge-reports=.vitest-reports --coverage`. That run applies the 80% gate to the whole suite and uploads the coverage. A shard alone covers about half the code: measured on `packages/watchlist`, one shard of four read 57% and the merge 98.7%.
12. **The infra job runs the static parts of every infra test (P5-7).** Beside `vision-container.test.sh --static-only`, it runs:
    - `compose.test.sh --static-only`;
    - `apps-image-context.test.sh`;
    - `check-connected-results.test.sh`;
    - `ci-workflow.test.sh`;
    - `pre-commit-hook.test.sh`, after installing gitleaks with `infra/scripts/install-gitleaks.sh`.

    The compose contract's live half and the gitleaks installer's download test stay local. The workflow contract step needs PyYAML on the runner, which a `workflow_dispatch` run must confirm.
13. **The e2e stack retries `compose up` once (P5-5).** After a Docker port-allocation error (`port is already allocated`, `ports are not available`, `bind: address already in use`), it takes the project down, waits 5 s and runs `up` once more (`e2e/src/compose-retry.ts`). Any other failure fails the run at once.
14. **Postgres race tests (K6A-7 in part, C74.6).** `report-races.integration.test.ts` now covers four cases on Postgres:
    - a budget-waiting run is enqueued at the first sweep after `not_before`;
    - an exhausted job leaves a live worker's run `running`;
    - an eleventh claim errors the run;
    - a re-screen during a redaction waits for it, because the run's foreign-key check waits on the report row lock, and any run it inserts is never enqueued, claimed or run.

    Open for the owner: completion-hook creation against consolidation and against account redaction, and the initial run's account link against consolidation. Each needs a Postgres harness for the inquiry transition or the consolidation service.
15. **The C73.8 log test has controls (R003).** It waits for the statement itself in the log, and a Postgres started without the flags shows that a logged parameter is seen.
16. **`un-sc` cannot be enabled in this release (R010, docs).** `docs/reports/README.md` says so.
    - Open for the owner: an opt-in setting with an acknowledgement of the UN terms. It needs `.env.example`, compose and the six reconcile callers.
    - Open for the owner: the manifest's note (`watchlists/sources.json`) still says "enable it only after reading those terms".
17. **E2E suite time (F15), measured again with the UI specs.** The run was `flock /tmp/openverify-e2e.lock pnpm e2e` on 2026-10-28, on the shared development machine:
    - the full-stack suite: 91 tests in 9.7 minutes, one worker, all passed;
    - the mocked flow suite: 69 tests in 20.1 s;
    - the whole run: 604 s.

    That is well under Playwright's `globalTimeout` of 45 minutes, and under the CI e2e job's 70 minutes, which took 14 minutes at the end of Phase 5. So neither limit is raised, the job is not sharded, and `infra/tests/ci-workflow.test.sh` keeps its checks. Locally, the suite still fits one call when it runs alone. Under load, run `--shard=1/3` to `3/3` (owner decision 9).
18. **Not done, for the owner or the orchestrator:**
    - K6A-3 (the frozen-record additions: both hashes, three parsers, the snapshot);
    - K6A-6 (the change signal, an honesty risk without tests against each publisher's signal semantics);
    - P5-6 (16 db test files to stamp `created_at`);
    - the LOW items K6A-5, K6A-9, K6A-10 and K6A-12.
19. **Evaluation-6's other findings stay with their owners.** No constant, threshold, preset or cap changed (C78.4):
    - the transposition typo rule;
    - silent misses with complete coverage;
    - the screening budget at both scales, with the 2% share and the 2,000-candidate cap;
    - the re-screen's permissive forward screen;
    - the held-out set's precision;
    - the C78.7 gate's easy case;
    - the benchmark helpers in the package's own gates (`packages/watchlist/tsconfig.json` and its Vitest include, the package owner's).

### fix-console

Recorded by the orchestrator from the Workflow 6B Stage 4 console fixer's (`fix-console`) return, 2026-10-28. The evidence is in `docs/tdd/phase6-review-fixes-console.tdd.md`. Each item is binding for later phases.

1. **The create-report action takes only the fields its form renders (R012, dashboard-reports amendment 6).** `queryOf` no longer walks field names as property paths, so a field such as `query/__proto__/x` can no longer pollute `Object.prototype` in the dashboard process. It accepts two shapes:
   - `query/<member>`, where the member is in any type's `REPORT_QUERY_FIELDS` other than `additional-names`;
   - `query/additional-names/<0..additionalNames-1>/<name-first|name-middle|name-last|term>`.

   Values are gathered in `Map`s and the query is built with `Object.fromEntries`. Any other `query/...` field is refused at `/data/attributes/query` ("has a field the query does not take") before the API is called. The API is unchanged.
2. **A document's preview shows once, in its evidence (P5-2; amends Phase 5 dashboard-documents amendment 11).** The Files section on the card and on the verification page still lists every file of the verification. It names the preview the evidence shows by kind, media type and id, with "Shown with the evidence", instead of showing the image again. A photo original stays shown with the files. The chip portrait is unchanged: it shows in the evidence and with the files, as Phase 5 pinned.
3. **Files whose kinds cannot be read (P5-3; extends Phase 5 dashboard-documents amendment 2).** `EvidenceProps.files` gains `null`: the verification has files the page cannot tell apart, because their kinds are in the inquiry document, which needs `inquiries:read`. The verification page passes `null` when the inquiry document does not name every one of the verification's files.
   - The chip portrait then reads "Listed with the files", with "Which file it is needs the inquiries:read permission."
   - The document evidence leaves its files to the files section.
   - No evidence says a file was not stored when its kind is unknown.
4. **Verification events in words (P5-1).** `eventWords` words every `verification.*` event ("Verification started", "submitted", "passed", "failed", "canceled", "skipped") on the events list and the event page. `VERIFICATION_EVENT_ACTION_WORDS` is a `Record` keyed by core's `VerificationEventName`, so an event added without words fails the typecheck. The inquiry timeline reads the same table.
5. **Template pickers page through the templates (R013, dashboard-reports amendment 6).** The create-report page and the `/reports` template filter read the templates 100 a page, up to 1,000 (`listTemplateChoices`, server-only). Beyond that, each picker says "Only the first 1,000 (active) templates are listed.", and a chosen template that is not listed reads "Template not listed", not "Template not available". The 1,000 is a console paging bound, not a matcher constant.
6. **Post-hoc figures are marked as such (R006, evaluation-6).** `docs/calibration/watchlist-phase6.md` says that three figures come from run 1 only, through steps outside the committed benchmark, and that run 2 did not compute them again:
   - the typo-kind breakdown;
   - the compacted-table round;
   - the unlisted-name alerts.

   Its Reproduction section gains "What run 2 does not cover". No number and no constant changed. A committed benchmark-report step for these figures belongs in `packages/watchlist/scripts`; see the owner decisions after Workflow 6B.
7. **Not changed, with the reason:**
   - K6A-1 and K6A-2 were already done in Stage 1 (`2c4e36ed`, `e03e8583`);
   - P5-8 is LOW and was not taken.

## Owner decisions after Workflow 6A (2026-10-28)

Taken by the orchestrator under the owner's standing approval ("take decisions yourself which are industry best"), recorded here and in PROGRESS. Binding for 6B; the known items `K6A-*` carry each one to its fixer.

1. **CLAUDE.md layout table** (contracts-6 F11, Stage 4 follow-up): the table already names `packages/watchlist` (decision 35). No change.
2. **Frozen watchlist record additions** (watchlist-ingest F6). Three optional, additive members, parsed where the source publishes them:
   - `alias-quality` (`strong` | `weak`): OFAC marks weak AKAs. A weak-alias match stays a match and carries the reason `weak_alias`, so reviewers see weaker evidence (industry practice: weak AKAs are screened, not dropped);
   - `list-memberships[] {list, program}` for entries of OFAC's Consolidated (non-SDN) lists, so a sectoral (SSI) or other non-blocking listing is never shown as an SDN-level block;
   - `relative-of-class` on a PEP relative or close associate: the class of the PEP they relate to, so the class filter treats them consistently.

   Scores do not change and `keysVersion` stays. Owner: `fix-api` (K6A-3).
3. **Phone and address** (risk-logic F4; Stage 2 risk-logic amendments 7 and 9; Stage 4 amendment 19). The names follow `PHONE_RISK_REASONS`' existing style (`voip`, `ported_30d`):
   - `PREPAID`: level `medium`, reason `prepaid` (the public mapping flags it);
   - `PAYPHONE`: level `medium`, reason `payphone` (rare for a person verifying, not fraud evidence by itself);
   - `OTHER`: no contribution, reason `other_line_type`; the level stays `null` (undetermined) unless SIM swap sets it;
   - `general_delivery` joins `address-designators`. The address report states designators and has no risk level, so it carries no risk of its own.

   This corrects the 2026-10-27 draft, which named the reasons `prepaid_line`, `payphone_line` and `line_type_other` and gave general delivery a risk. Each constant is documented with its basis in `docs/reports/`. Owner: `fix-api` (K6A-4).
4. **`POSTGRES_TEST_COMMAND` for the older suites** (foundation-6 F5): the 31 Phase 1–5 Testcontainers files adopt it, so no test Postgres logs bind parameters (P18). Owner: `fix-api` (K6A-5).
5. **Stage 4 amendment 19, the port and contract amendments.** All additive and optional:
   - (a) `hops` (an integer ≥ 1) on the rated crypto exposure answer. The served `hops` is the provider's when given, else 1 ("at least one hop") as now. Chain-analytics vendors state direct and indirect exposure with the hop distance.
   - (b) `environmentId` and `names` on `AdverseMediaSearch`. The port then states what the adapters already read, and api-providers' `AdverseMediaSearchRequest` folds into it.
   - (c) `lookup` on `PhoneIntelligenceResult` (risk-logic's `PhoneIntelligenceLookup`), so "not checked" is never read as "no SIM swap". A bare answer keeps the conservative reading.
   - (d) An actor on `ReportRedactionOptions`, so every redaction writes a `redact` action with its actor: `user`, `api_key`, or `system` for cascades and retention.
   - (e) A frozen read of the distinct `watchlist_names.script` per source, so coverage states `lossy_script` from the source rather than from the candidates loaded in the run.
   - (f) The article `language` stays non-nullable. GDELT's unknown language is served as `und`, the BCP 47 and ISO 639-2 code for undetermined, and `docs/reports/` says so.
   - (g) The phone reasons and (h) `general_delivery`: see decision 3.
   - (i) `email-domain` keeps its 253-character cap, the longest textual DNS name; RFC 1035's 255 octets are the wire form, with its length bytes. No query can exceed it: `email-address` is capped at 254 characters, so its domain has at most 252. No change.

   Owner: `fix-api`: (a)–(d) as K6A-8, (e) as K6A-9; (f) is docs only.
6. **The change signal and the refresh back-off** (Stage 4 amendments 17 and 21).
   - Migration `0021` adds `watchlist_sources.last_change_signal text NULL` and `watchlist_sources.check_failures integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`.
   - The sync asks the change signal first, as amendment 17 says.
   - A source whose check fails waits a doubling interval: 15 minutes after the first failure, capped at its `refresh_interval_hours` and at 24 hours. A successful check resets the count. This is exponential back-off, polite to the publishers and to Wikidata.

   Owner: `fix-api` (K6A-6, K6A-11).
7. **The missing races** (Stage 4 amendment 18), on Postgres. Also there: the two C74.1 cases that run on PGlite only, an exhausted job leaving a run held by a live worker `running` and an eleventh claim erroring the run. Owner: `fix-api` (K6A-7).
8. **`forgetStoredResponses`** (Stage 4 amendment 20) is exported from `packages/db/src/repositories/redaction.ts` and reused by `forgetReportCopies`. Owner: `fix-api` (K6A-12).
9. **Test time.**
   - CI's `unit-api` job runs as two shards with blob reports, plus a merge job that applies the 80% gate to the whole, instead of one 45-minute job (Stage 4 amendment 13).
   - Locally, the full-stack e2e runs in three shards, so each fits one 10-minute call under load; CI keeps one e2e job (14 of its 70 minutes).

   Owner: `fix-api` (K6A-13, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`).
10. **For evaluation-6** (api-watchlists follow-up): the 2,000,000-name benchmark imports through `syncWatchlistSource`, as the C78.7 gate does, so `analyzeWatchlistTables` runs. Without it, the first import of a large source is quadratic.
11. **Carried past Phase 6** (optional performance work, not 6B):
    - an incremental key-frequency rebuild from the diff (about 7 s at 25,000 names);
    - folding the rare-key lookup into the candidate query (retrieval is most of the 57 ms screening p95).

## Owner decisions after Workflow 6B (2026-10-28)

Taken by the orchestrator under the owner's standing approval ("take decisions yourself which are industry best"), recorded here and in PROGRESS. They are binding for Workflow 6C and later phases.

**Why Phase 6 is not done after 6B.** Two acceptance lines are not met.
- "Matching meets the C78.7 budgets on synthetic tables": evaluation-6 measured a screening p95 of 290.8 ms at 100,000 list names (budget 100 ms) and 2,309 ms at 2,000,000 (budget 250 ms). At PEP scale the candidate cap bound in 47.7% of searches, and 7.0% of screenings ended `result_truncated` (`docs/calibration/watchlist-phase6.md`, finding 3).
- The published alert rate (0 per 1,000) and precision (100%) come from a list too sparse in name space to produce a false alert (finding 5). Post hoc, a quarter of unlisted names drawn from a realistic law alerted against the 100,000-name table. False positives on common names are this plan's first risk.

Phase 7 reuses the matcher for its fuzzy name lists. So **Workflow 6C** closes Phase 6: it brings matching within its budgets, measures false alerts with a calibration report, and finishes the items carried out of 6B.

1. **Matching performance** (finding 3; decision 11 of the 6A decisions; C78.3, C78.7). Retrieval must meet C78.7 on a heavy-headed name law with the shipped variant groups. At list scale each retrieval key must stay selective in absolute terms, and a name is retrieved by combinations of its tokens rather than by a share of the list:
   - **The document-frequency rule becomes an absolute bound,** a fraction of the candidate cap, so no key alone returns more candidates than the cap allows. The 2% share is dropped (a C78.4 constant change, fixed only by the calibration report). At 100,000 names the old share equals the cap (2% × 100,000 = 2,000), so the bound must sit below the cap for the change to act there. The frequency counts entries, as the cap does, not names.
   - **Pair keys.** Every strong list name stores an unordered pair key for every pair of its keyed tokens, in each fold and through their variant-group ids, so order swaps and variant spellings still meet. At query time the pair key of every two subject tokens is queried when it is under the bound, whatever the other tokens. When neither a subject token's own key nor any pair containing it is under the bound, the search cannot reach every name holding that token, so it records `truncated` (C78.5) instead of missing silently.
   - **The test case for retrieval and for the reverse index:** the synthetic subject "Maria Elena Garcia", with `elena` rare and `maria` and `garcia` over the bound, retrieves the listed "Maria Garcia" through the pair key {garcia, maria}. Today it is missed with complete coverage, because neither the rare keys nor the trigram path reach it.
   - `KEYS_VERSION` changes, and the upgrade runs `watchlists:rekey` (C78.3).
   - **The rare-key lookup folds into the candidate query** (decision 11).
   - **Loads and scoring.** Scoring already stops early on `nameScoreUpperBound` (`packages/watchlist/src/score/name-score.ts`), so 6B's 144 ms of scoring at p95 includes it; that is the baseline. `matcher-6c` names the mechanism that brings 100,000 names within 100 ms (for example a tighter bound with the pair keys, or loading names first and records only for the candidates whose name bound can reach the preset's minimum) and measures each one's share.
   - **Write cost.** `matcher-6c` reports the sync and re-key times at 2,000,000 names with the pair keys, and sizes the benchmark's import steps by time.

   `matcher-6c` chooses the exact constants on its own development tables, generated by the benchmark from a development seed it draws and records. That seed's listed subjects include variant cases (order swap, dropped and added middle names, initials, typo, transposition), so it sees recall at scale. The calibration report fixes the constants. The targets are:
   - both C78.7 p95 budgets, on the heavy-headed law;
   - `result_truncated` in at most 1% of screenings at 2,000,000 names;
   - recall non-inferior to the base's at every preset and both scales: the lower bound of the paired 95% interval of the difference (head − base) is above −1 percentage point.

   If a budget is still missed, the report gives the measured gap and the owner revisits the budget. The budget is never moved to fit.
2. **The C78.7 gate measures the real case** (finding 6).
   - The Testcontainers gate draws its table from a heavy-headed law, with a typed generator beside the gate in `apps/api/src/watchlists/test-support/` (never the held-out generator), and uses the shipped variant groups when the file is present. Its header says which case ran.
   - CI's integration job fetches the data and builds the file first, as the e2e job does; `matcher-6c` owns that job and its checks in `infra/tests/ci-workflow.test.sh`.
   - The gate asserts what does not depend on the machine, everywhere: candidates p95 within the cap, how often the cap binds, and every listed subject found. It prints its timings. The latency budget is asserted only when `OPENVERIFY_C787_TIMING=assert` is set, as the calibration's runs under the e2e lock set it. A timing assertion on shared CI runners or under a full local test run measures the machine, not the matcher. The calibration report is the authority for the budgets.
3. **The re-screen fits its budget** (finding 4). 6B's figures (25.6 s to 254.3 s) are about 17 s plus 79 ms per changed entry, all in the forward screen (`apps/api/src/jobs/reports/rescreen.ts`); the inserted runs execute later and are not counted. So:
   - **The forward screen gets faster per entry:** each changed entry is analysed once and reused across the subject chunks, and the subject preset's score bound prunes candidates before scoring. The target is 60 s at 1,000 changed entries and 200,000 subjects.
   - **Fewer downstream runs:** the forward screen scores an added or changed entry against each candidate subject at the subject's preset. It inserts a `list_update` run only where the score would keep a match, or where it cannot decide (a lossy comparison, a truncated search). The report gives the inserted runs and their cost (runs × the screening p50) beside the forward screen's time.
   - **Stale evidence:** a run is also inserted for every report with an `open` or `delisted` match on a changed entry, found through `entry_index` per environment. A match whose entry lost an alias, or had a birth date corrected, is then re-scored, and C75.8 marks it if it no longer holds (`match_not_found_in_run`, `evidence_changed`).
   - **No miss between the two screens:** a `list_update` run screens its trigger sync's changed entries beside retrieval, so whatever the forward screen found, the run finds too.
4. **Transposition typos** (finding 1). The typo rule uses the optimal string alignment distance, in which an adjacent transposition is one edit, within the same budget. This is a rule change (C78.4). It is kept unless the calibration report shows a material rise in false alerts on the new held-out set: the head's alerts per 1,000 customers exceed the base's by more than 10% of the base's rate, and the paired 95% interval of the difference lies above zero. Otherwise it is reverted.
5. **A held-out set that measures false alerts** (finding 5; H21). The held-out generator gains:
   - a listed population drawn from a heavy-headed law over name forms, embedded in the 100,000- and 2,000,000-name performance tables;
   - a customer population drawn from the same law with no listed identity.

   How it is measured:
   - A sample of the listed identities' variant cases (order swap, dropped and added middle names, initials, typo, transposition) and the customers are screened against both tables. Recall, precision and alerts per 1,000 are reported per scale, so the checks run where decision 1 acts.
   - A customer who equals a listed entry in name and birth date cannot be told apart from it by any screening on those fields. Such customers are counted and reported separately, outside the false-alert rate.
   - The agent writing the set never reads the matcher's code (as `fixtures-6` did). The cases are generated at evaluation time from a fresh seed and never committed.
   - The existing variant cases stay, so recall per variant source is comparable with 6B's. The shared fixture helpers they use (`packages/watchlist/src/testing/{names,random,records,normalised}.ts`) keep their output for a given seed, pinned by a golden test.
6. **Silent misses** (finding 2). No new coverage limit: `transliterated` would be raised on almost every non-Latin name and would stop meaning anything. Instead, `docs/reports/watchlist.md` and the console's coverage words say what complete coverage does not promise:
   - romanisations outside the variant groups and ICAO's tables;
   - unknown spellings;
   - typos beyond the budget.

   Screening products disclose their transliteration scope in the same way.
7. **Post-hoc figures become steps** (6B R006). The typo-kind breakdown, the alerts on unlisted names and the compacted-table round become committed benchmark steps with tests, so the 6C report reproduces every number it prints.
8. **The benchmark helpers join the package's own gates** (evaluation-6 deviation 1). The `typecheck` and `test` includes of `packages/watchlist` cover `scripts/benchmark-*`.
9. **The frozen record additions** (K6A-3; decision 2 of the 6A decisions) are built in 6C, amended as follows:
   - **`alias-quality` is not added.** The record's `names[].quality` already carries OFAC's weak AKAs (decision 16 of this plan, H10): strong names are keyed, and weak ones match only by exact equality, with the reason `weak_alias`. A second member would only duplicate it.
   - **`list-memberships[] {list, program}`** is added on entries of OFAC's Consolidated (non-SDN) lists.
   - **`relative-of-class`** is added on a PEP relative or close associate: the class of the PEP they relate to, derived by ingestion on each PEP pass.
   - **How they are hashed.** Both are risk data, so they enter the risk-data hash, only when present, and never the content hash. Records without them keep both hashes, and dismissed matches never lapse.
   - **What a change triggers.** An entry that gains either member has a change of risk data: it gets `changed_sync_id` and is re-screened, as C76.2 step 6 says. A reviewed notify-on-change match on it returns to pending once, which is what a change of risk data means. `docs/reports/README.md` says so in the upgrade note.
   - **Who consumes them, each in its owner's files:**
     - `ingestion-6c` parses and derives the members and hashes them. It lands the record type first, in a commit of its own, so the others can build on it.
     - `matcher-6c`'s class filter (`politically-exposed-person.ts`) reads `relative-of-class` from the relative's own record instead of the linked holder, and `docs/reports/politically-exposed-person.md` says so.
     - `api-reports-6c` adds both as optional members of the match evidence and the snapshot.
     - `console-tests-6c` words them, so an entry listed only on a non-blocking Consolidated list is shown with that list, never as an SDN-level block.
10. **The change signal is dropped** (K6A-6; this amends decision 6 of the 6A decisions). Every check downloads the publication and compares its SHA-256, which is always correct. A publisher signal that failed to move would mark a changed list fresh, which is the honesty risk ADR 0005 guards against, and each publisher's signal has semantics of its own to test. `last_change_signal` is not added. An HTTP conditional request (ETag or Last-Modified), where a publisher serves one, is the standard safe saving. It is carried to Phase 11 as optional, and a `200` still goes through the SHA-256 comparison. This amends C76.2 step 2 (the change-signal pre-check), the C76.1 manifest's `change_signal` and the C77.4 signal readers (`packages/watchlist/src/parsers/signals.ts`). `ingestion-6c` removes them test-first, except a reader that also gives the publication time for provenance, which stays for that.
11. **The three missing races** (K6A-7) are built in 6C, with a Postgres harness for the inquiry transition's report hooks and for the consolidation service:
    - completion-hook creation against consolidation;
    - completion-hook creation against account redaction;
    - the initial run's account link against consolidation.
12. **The redaction actor** (K6A-8 (d)) is built in 6C. Every redaction writes a `redact` action with its actor: `user`, `api_key`, or `system` for cascades and retention. The console's history shows it, because an audit trail names who removed data.
13. **The per-source script read** (K6A-9) is built in 6C, with the coverage work.
14. **The phone reasons** (K6A-4, the rest of decision 3 of the 6A decisions) are built in 6C. One change covers the contracts, the evaluator, the API and the dashboard's words, as Stage 1 dashboard-reports amendment 12 requires.
15. **Test hygiene** is done in 6C:
    - the Phase 1–5 Testcontainers files adopt `POSTGRES_TEST_COMMAND` (K6A-5, P18);
    - the db test files that assert newest-first order stamp `created_at` (P5-6);
    - `forgetReportCopies` reuses `forgetStoredResponses` (K6A-12);
    - the `und` language note is checked in the media pages (K6A-10).
16. **Review and dismissal need `pii:view` in the API too** (6B privacy-security reviewer). Without `pii:view` the API serves the matches as `null`, so the caller would decide on evidence it cannot see. The API refuses review and dismissal with 403 when `requirePiiView` (`apps/api/src/auth/pii.ts`) fails: a user without `pii:view`, or an API key minted masked. An unmasked API key keeps reviewing, as `report-review.test.ts` does, and tests cover all three. This matches the console (a C75.5 amendment) and is least privilege.
17. **The sources document** gains three additive members, as dashboard-report-config proposed:
    - `name` and `report-type` on each template warning;
    - `media.attribution`, carrying the GDELT citation;
    - `matcher: { loaded, keys-version }`, so the console can say that the API lacks the variant groups instead of asking for a re-key.
18. **The media count on the sources page** (6B R004) is the asking environment's own scheduled media runs per day, beside the install budget. Instead of the other tenants' total, the page gets one boolean that says whether the install is above 80% of the budget. The install-wide member is removed in the same change, and the console's share (`source-words.ts`) is computed from the environment's own count.
19. **The variant groups' MIT notice** (6B R011). `build-variant-groups` writes `license`, `copyright` and `licenseText` into the file, as `build-legal-forms` does. The file's SHA-256 and `keysVersion` move with it: one re-key, which decision 1 needs anyway.
20. **The redirect keeps its port** (6B privacy-security reviewer). The stream client's one redirect must keep the scheme, the host and the port.
21. **The UN list** (6B R010). `watchlists/sources.json` says, as the docs now do, that `un-sc` cannot be enabled in this release. An opt-in with an acknowledgement of the UN's terms is carried to Phase 11.
22. **No cap on pending media runs per environment** (the open part of 6B R001). The fair sweep ends the starvation, and the per-minute limit bounds the growth. Per-tenant queue quotas belong to Phase 11 hardening.
23. **An emptied source stays screenable** (the open part of 6B R009). A source becomes empty only through the operator's recorded `--allow-mass-removal`, which states the operator's intent.
24. **Query fields in the console** (6B R014) take each member's own length limit from the contract.
25. **Not built in Phase 6** (dashboard-reports' optional proposals): the reports in the dashboard inquiry document, the latest run's notes on the report resource, and the `FormDialogButton` options. The inquiry section's reads are bounded (25 reports and at most 10 detail reads) and masked. These are carried to Phase 9, with the structured template forms and a create-template form.
26. **P5-8** (the README rows for the records evidence views) is done by the console agent in 6C.

## Workflow 6C (matching calibration and carried fixes)

**Before it starts**, the orchestrator splits `packages/db/src/repositories/watchlists.ts` without changing behaviour, with the suites green, so that each agent owns separate files:
- `watchlist-reads.ts` takes the frozen reads (`retrieveCandidates`, `loadEntries`, `getWatchlistSourceStates`), `WatchlistReads`, `DB_WATCHLIST_READS`, and the `WatchlistEntryRecord` and `WatchlistSourceState` types.
- `watchlist-key-df.ts` takes `rebuildWatchlistKeyDf` and `WATCHLIST_NAME_COUNT_KEY`, the frequency that retrieval reads.
- `watchlist-rows.ts` (internal) takes the row helpers, and the entry and source-state columns with their row readers, which both halves use.
- `listReportTemplateSourceLists` moves to `report-templates.ts`, and `scheduledInstallMediaRunsPerDay` to `reports.ts`. These are the sources page's reads of the report tables.
- The tests split the same way, with their shared setup in `watchlists.test-support.ts`, which is frozen in 6C. The sources page's two tests join `report-repositories.test.ts`.

Everything stays exported from `@openverify/db`.

**What the benchmark imports is frozen in 6C** (`syncWatchlistSource`, `useScreeningKit`, `DB_WATCHLIST_READS`, `processReportRescreen` and the `@openverify/db/testing` helpers):
- `WatchlistReads` keeps its three methods. Decision 13's scripts are an optional member of `WatchlistSourceState`.
- An agent that must change one of those signatures fixes the benchmark's call site in the same commit, as a borrowed edit (see the rules).

**Ownership.** Each agent owns the tests beside its files.
- **Stage 1** (parallel):
  - **`evaluation-set-6c`** (decisions 5, 7 and 8).
    - Owns `packages/watchlist/src/testing/held-out/`, `packages/watchlist/scripts/benchmark-*` with their tests, the `scripts` includes of `packages/watchlist/tsconfig.json` and `vitest.config.ts`, and the benchmark entries in `packages/watchlist/package.json`.
    - It never reads `packages/watchlist/src/{names,translit,keys,score,match}/` or `matcher-6c`'s commits during 6C.
    - The existing steps keep their output for a given seed, and the golden test of decision 5 pins the shared fixture helpers.
    - `timedReads` wraps every member of `WatchlistReads` generically.
    - It proves, with a scratch seed, that every step runs at both scales at the head, and at Workflow 6C's base with the head's `scripts/benchmark-*` and `src/testing/held-out/` overlaid on a worktree.
  - **`matcher-6c`** (decisions 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 13 and 19, and the class filter of 9).
    - Owns `packages/watchlist/src/{names,translit,keys,score,match,crypto,reverse-index}/`, `packages/watchlist/scripts/build-*`, `packages/watchlist/data/legal-forms.json` and `packages/core/src/report-matching.ts`.
    - In `packages/db/src/repositories/`: `watchlist-reads.ts`, `watchlist-key-df.ts`, `watchlist-rows.ts` and `report-rescreen.ts`. The only Stage 1 migration (`0022`, with its table definitions), if one is needed.
    - In `apps/api`:
      - `src/watchlists/{rekey,matcher-data,watchlists.integration.test}.ts` and `src/watchlists/test-support/`;
      - `src/jobs/reports/{rescreen,screening}.ts`;
      - `src/jobs/report-types/{watchlist,business-watchlist,crypto-address-watchlist,politically-exposed-person}.ts`;
      - their tests, `matching-reports.test.ts` and `matching-reports-db.test.ts` included;
      - `src/test-support/fakes.ts` with `watchlists-fake.test.ts`.
    - The integration job of `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and its checks in `infra/tests/ci-workflow.test.sh` (decision 2), and the `SHIPPED_VARIANT_GROUPS_SHA256` line of `infra/docker/apps.Dockerfile` (decision 19).
    - Decision 19 keeps the build file's `format` tag and header and only adds members, because `scripts/benchmark-independence.mjs` parses them.
    - Docs: `docs/reports/{watchlist,business-watchlist,crypto-address-watchlist,politically-exposed-person}.md`.
    - It tunes on development tables only, never on held-out output.
  - **`ingestion-6c`** (decisions 9, 10, 20 and 21).
    - Owns `packages/watchlist/src/{parsers,zip,xml,diff,pep,record}/`, `packages/watchlist/src/testing/` except `held-out/`, `packages/watchlist/fixtures/`, `packages/watchlist/data/pep-positions.json` and `packages/watchlist/src/index.ts`. In `testing/{names,random,records,normalised}.ts` it only adds; existing exports keep their output for a given seed.
    - In `packages/db`: `repositories/watchlists.ts` and its test.
    - In `apps/api`: `src/watchlists/` except the files `matcher-6c` owns, `src/http/stream.ts` and `scripts/{watchlists-sync,pep-sync}.ts`.
    - Also `watchlists/sources.json` and `docs/reports/README.md`, including the upgrade note for `KEYS_VERSION`, `watchlists:rekey` and decision 9.
  - **`api-reports-6c`** (decisions 11, 12, 14, 15 (K6A-12, K6A-10), 16, 17 and 18, and the evidence members of 9).
    - Owns `packages/contracts/src/`, `packages/checks/src/reports/`, `apps/api/src/services/reports/`, `apps/api/src/services/{redaction,retention}.ts`, `apps/api/src/routes/server/{reports,report-templates,report-template-versions}.ts` and `apps/api/src/routes/dashboard/{reports,watchlist-sources}.ts`.
    - `apps/api/src/jobs/reports/` and `apps/api/src/jobs/report-types/`, except the files `matcher-6c` owns.
    - In `packages/db/src/repositories/`: `{reports,report-templates,report-runs,report-matches,report-actions,redaction}.ts`.
    - `docs/api/openapi.json`, `contracts/fixtures/`, and the Android `ContractFixturesTest` when a fixture needs it.
    - In `apps/dashboard`, in Stage 1 only, and only so the dashboard typechecks and passes on the new shapes:
      - `src/lib/report-field-words.ts`;
      - `src/testing/report-config-fixtures.ts`;
      - `src/testing/routes/report-config.ts`;
      - `src/components/report-config/source-words.ts`.
    - `docs/reports/{email-address,phone-number,address-lookup,adverse-media,business-adverse-media}.md`.
- **Stage 2** (parallel, after Stage 1). Every whole-suite run (a package's test script, `test:integration`, e2e) and every timing takes the e2e lock, so the two agents never overlap on load.
  - **`calibration-6c`** changes no matcher code, constant, generator or threshold. It owns `docs/calibration/watchlist-phase6c.md`.
    - It may fix, test-first, a defect that stops a benchmark step from running, in `packages/watchlist/scripts/benchmark-*` only (never a metric's definition or a generator's law), and records it.
    - It commits its method and its decision rules before drawing the seed:
      - base and head on one seed;
      - paired bootstrap intervals;
      - the margins of decisions 1 and 4.
    - It then draws a fresh evaluation seed with a CSPRNG.
    - It measures quality (the 6B variant cases and the new populations, at both scales) and performance (C78.7 at both scales, the cap, `result_truncated`, the re-screen) in two worktrees pinned to commits:
      - Workflow 6C's base, with the head's `scripts/benchmark-*` and `src/testing/held-out/` overlaid, uncommitted;
      - Stage 1's head.
    - Each worktree is prepared with `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --offline`, the `data/` files copied in, and the variant groups built with that tree's own script.
    - A timing counts only when the 1-minute load average is at most 4 at its start and end. It is repeated three times and the median is reported, with the load average.
    - It gives a verdict, with numbers, on decisions 1 to 5: kept, or reverted with the reason.
  - **`console-tests-6c`** (decisions 6 in words, 9 in words, 15 (K6A-5, P5-6), 17, 18 in the console, 24 and 26).
    - Owns `apps/dashboard/`, `e2e/tests/ui-*.spec.ts` with their helpers, `packages/db/src/testing.ts` and the Phase 1–5 test files the two hygiene items name.
- **Stage 3:** two read-only reviewers. One covers matching, the evaluation set, the calibration and ingestion. The other covers the report API, the console and the tests.
- **Stage 4:** one verifier for every CRITICAL and HIGH finding. It records the Stage 1 and 2 deviations as "## Workflow 6C Stage 1 and 2 amendments".
- **Stage 5:** one fixer, `fix-6c`, over every path.
  - It fixes the blocking findings, then the MEDIUM ones.
  - It reverts what the calibration rejected, test-first.
  - It re-measures with the calibration's command after any change to the matching code.
  - It records "## Workflow 6C review amendments".
  - It runs every gate.

**Rules for 6C.** These apply on top of "Rules for every agent", and replace its "Shared files" rule, since 6C has no integration agent and each list above names the shared files its owner may change.
- **Frozen in 6C** (a change is a follow-up for `fix-6c`):
  - `apps/api/src/{container,worker,config}.ts`;
  - `apps/api/src/test-support/harness.ts`;
  - `packages/db/src/schema.ts`;
  - `packages/db/src/repositories/index.ts`;
  - `packages/db/src/repositories/watchlists.test-support.ts`;
  - every file that no list names.
- **Borrowed edits.** A test in another agent's file that the plan's change breaks gets a borrowed edit. Wait until `git status --porcelain <file>` is empty, then make the minimal change, commit it under the git lock in the same commit as the change that needs it, and list it in `contractDeviations`.
- **Timings** are taken under `flock /tmp/openverify-e2e.lock`, the lock the full-stack suite uses, with the load average written beside each one.
- **Migrations:** only `matcher-6c` adds a migration in Stage 1. Any other schema need is a follow-up for `fix-6c`.
- **Held-out data:** the held-out cases and every seed's output stay outside git.

**Acceptance for 6C:**
- The calibration report publishes C78.7 at both scales and C78.8 with false alerts measurable, at Workflow 6C's base and head.
- Every changed constant or rule has its verdict.
- Every decision above is done or carried with its reason.
- Every validation command passes locally and on GitHub.

### Review of this addendum (2026-10-28)

An adversarial review of the decisions after 6B and of this section, made against C78, the 6A and 6B sections, the rules, the calibration report and the code, returned 22 findings: 8 HIGH, 10 MEDIUM and 4 LOW. Each was checked against the code. All are applied above:
- **HIGH:**
  - partly common names missed silently: the all-pairs keys, `truncated` instead of a silent miss, and the test case (decision 1);
  - recall and false alerts measured where decision 1 cannot act: the populations embedded in both tables (decision 5);
  - "the hashes follow the record" would cause a wave of re-alerts: `alias-quality` dropped, both members risk data only when present (decision 9);
  - api-reports-6c needed `watchlists.ts`: the pre-split moves the sources page's reads;
  - the Dockerfile's digest pin had no owner (`matcher-6c`);
  - matcher-6c's tests sat in other files: each owner owns the tests beside its files, plus borrowed edits;
  - the benchmark's imports: frozen, with `timedReads` generic, and the base overlay proven in Stage 1;
  - the base measurement could not run: worktrees pinned at base and head, prepared as stated.
- **MEDIUM:**
  - the CI job and the gate's generator now have owners, and the timing is asserted only under the calibration (decision 2);
  - the 100,000-name mechanism is named, with the existing early stop as baseline (decision 1);
  - the re-screen's per-entry speed-up, the stale-evidence runs and the trigger's entries (decision 3);
  - the consumers of the record members, by owner (decision 9);
  - `requirePiiView` semantics (decision 16);
  - the console half of decision 18, and the dashboard's typed fixtures in Stage 1;
  - the rules override, with a frozen list;
  - `rebuildWatchlistKeyDf` and the row helpers moved to matcher-6c's files;
  - Stage 2 contention, with decision rules fixed before the seed and margins stated (decisions 1 and 4);
  - the shared fixture helpers pinned (decision 5).
- **LOW:**
  - the contract text that decision 10 amends;
  - indistinguishable namesakes reported separately (decision 5);
  - the documentation owners;
  - the pair keys' write cost (decision 1).

One fix differs from the reviewer's proposal. Instead of a `record_hash` column and a write in place, `alias-quality` is dropped as a duplicate of `names[].quality`, and the other two members are risk data hashed only when present. No migration is needed, and C76.2 step 6 stands.

## Workflow 6C Stage 1 and 2 amendments

Recorded by the Workflow 6C Stage 4 verifier on 2026-10-29. The source is the `contractDeviations` of the four Stage 1 agents and the two Stage 2 agents, plus the calibration's verdicts. The evidence is in `docs/tdd/phase6-{evaluation-set,matcher,ingestion,api-reports,console-tests,calibration}-6c.tdd.md` and `docs/calibration/watchlist-phase6c.md`. Each item below binds Stage 5 (`fix-6c`) and every later phase. An item that names a follow-up gives it to `fix-6c`, unless it names another owner.

### evaluation-set-6c

1. **The package gates cover the helpers (decision 8).** `packages/watchlist/tsconfig.json` sets `allowJs` and `checkJs` beside its `scripts` include, because TypeScript skips `.mjs` files in `include` without `allowJs`. The helpers' coverage list has a single source: `vitest.config.ts` imports `BENCHMARK_HELPERS` from `scripts/benchmark-vitest.config.mjs`. That module exports its config as a typed const, because the base config's `declaration: true` rejects an inferred default export (TS2883).
2. **Three CLIs stay under `benchmark:typecheck` alone.** `benchmark-postgres.mjs`, `benchmark-performance.mjs` and `benchmark-quality.mjs` import apps/api and packages/db sources. Both of those packages depend on `@openverify/watchlist`, so the package typecheck must not check them, and turbo would cache it without tracking their files. `scripts/benchmark-gates.test.mjs` enforces the split. Every other benchmark module (the helpers, render, the Vitest config and the tests) is in the package's own typecheck and test, under the 80% gate.
3. **One held-out population serves both scales (decision 5).** It is drawn from the performance tables' own name law (`namePool` of `<seed>/pool`). The benchmark passes that law in, so the held-out generator never reads the scripts. Each scale table is the performance benchmark's table (the same generator, seed `<seed>/100k` or `<seed>/2m`, profile and id prefix) with the population first in every part. The scale tables have their own containers, so the performance steps and their output are unchanged.
4. **The scale sets are keyed with the shipped variant groups.** The shipped build is the deployed matcher, and the scale sets hold no held-out-group case, so their false alerts are the ones production would raise. The 6B set keeps the hold-out build and the V12 independence assertion.
5. **Sizes, fixed in `SCALE_SIZES` before any evaluation seed:**
   - 1,050 listed persons: 150 for each of exact, order swap, dropped middle, added middle, initials, typo (substitute, insert or delete) and transposition;
   - 400 listed companies: 100 for each of exact, typo, transposition and legal form;
   - 1,500 person customers and 500 company customers. Company customers are new; 6B's customers were persons only.

   Every person query carries the person's birth date, and 85% of the listed entries carry one. A typo or transposition case redraws its token until the token has at least 3 letters.
6. **Indistinguishable customers.** A person customer is indistinguishable when an individual entry has a name equal to the customer's as a folded token bag (NFKD, marks removed, case folded, sorted) and lists the customer's birth date. A company customer is indistinguishable when a name is equal and, if the customer states a country, an entity country is equal. A namesake entry without a birth date is a false alert, as the plan reads. Indistinguishable customers are left out of the false-alert rate and reported apart, with how many alerted at each preset.
7. **Intervals at scale (decisions 1, 4 and 5).**
   - The bootstrap resamples the population's listed identities and the customers. The table's background entries are the fixed list under test, and a match to one of them counts in every replicate.
   - Each set has one stream, `evaluation-6c/bootstrap/<seed>/scale-<scale>/<set>`, so the presets are paired. For the 6B sets, `compare` uses `bootstrapQuality`'s stream, so each run's intervals equal its own report's.
   - Base and head are drawn together, and the difference interval is head minus base, replicate by replicate.
   - A Wilson interval is printed beside any count of zero or of all.
   - The calibration found that this definition biases the company intervals (calibration-6c item 13).
8. **The post-hoc analyses are committed steps (decision 7).**
   - The compacted round runs `VACUUM (FULL, ANALYZE)`.
   - `typo-kinds` reads each kind back from the listed and typed tokens, because the 6B cases record only `typo`. It splits token length at 5, the C79.1 budget.
   - `<scale>-unlisted` counts the first round, as 6B did.
   - On a copy of 6B's run 1, the steps reproduce the published figures exactly: 142 of 145, 0 of 98, 37 of 150 and 93 of 150 (3,771 matches).
9. **`<scale>-variants` is a development tool for `matcher-6c`.** It screens 210 cases (30 per source) plus the measure step's 150 unlisted subjects, at the three presets. Its rules live in `scripts/benchmark-variants.mjs`, apart from the held-out generator, and the calibration does not use it.
10. **The CLI surface.**
    - Every 6C step belongs to an existing CLI, so `package.json` is unchanged and the base overlay runs with the base's own `package.json`.
    - No 6B step or flag is renamed.
    - New flags: `--smoke` (performance), `--namespace` (both CLIs) and `--parts` (the 2,000,000-name import and `scale-prepare`).
    - With `--parts N`, the final table holds the same entries. Only the last part's publication time differs, and with it the file's digest. Base and head use the same N.
    - The 6B quality CLI's smoke container is `evaluation-6-quality-smoke`.
11. **The reads wrappers.** `timedReads` and `cachedReads` keep their names and defaults and delegate to generic wrappers. The three frozen reads are reported as state, retrieve and load, in that order, and any other member is timed and reused under its own name.
12. **Process (accepted).** The golden tests are characterization tests and have no RED step. They were pinned and checked at the base, and mutations in a base worktree show that each one guards its shared helper. The renderer fixture committed RED in b6cad26d used `count` where the cell is `found`. It was corrected in the GREEN commit 7ee94e76.

### matcher-6c

1. **Development measurements are not calibration numbers.**
   - `matcher-6c` tuned with a scratch driver outside git, which calls the committed benchmark helpers. The driver used its own containers, 300 variant cases of its own (50 per source) and the development seed a362daebb975e71a3e330ffe86234816.
   - At 100,000 names the head ran on the base's database, re-keyed (62,631 entries). At 2,000,000 names, base and head each imported their own database from that seed.
   - `docs/calibration/watchlist-phase6c.md` is authoritative for every number.
2. **Key frequencies (decision 1).** `watchlist_key_df` counts the distinct live entries whose strong names hold a key. It stores a row only for a key that two or more entries hold, because a key without a row holds at most one entry, which is within every bound of at least one. The reserved rows are `#names` and, for decision 13, one `#script:<script>` row per script.
3. **Bound constants (C78.4, provisional).**
   - `key-frequency-bound-share` is 0.25, with `keyFrequencyBound(cap) = max(1, floor(0.25 × cap))`, which is 500 at the cap of 2,000. It replaces `key-document-frequency-max` (2% of names).
   - `pair-max-tokens` is 6: only the first six keyed tokens pair.
   - `pair-max-groups` is 4: a token in more than four variant groups pairs by its own spellings only.
   - The calibration kept all three (see calibration-6c).
4. **Reach and truncation are counted per pair, stricter than decision 1's per-token wording.**
   - A group is the own keys of two keyed subject tokens plus the pair keys they share. A one-token name has its own keys as its only group.
   - A search is unreachable, and so `truncated` (C78.5), when every key of some group is over the bound in a screened source.
   - `truncated = capReached || unreachable`. `capReached` holds only when the cap actually cuts the result. A ranked set that reaches its slack of 2 × (cap + 1) before the live check counts as cut.
5. **Out-of-reach pairs are still looked up, up to the cap.** The pair keys of an out-of-reach group are looked up beyond the bound, up to the cap, so namesakes that hold both tokens are still screened. The search is marked truncated all the same. Limiting this lookup to the bound (slice 15) missed 3 listed namesakes at 2,000,000 names, and it is not to be restored without a new calibration.
6. **The trigram path (C78.3 as amended).** Trigrams run only in three cases:
   - a single word;
   - a lone keyed token out of reach (a group with no pair key);
   - a name whose keys reach no entry: no key within the bound has two holders, no key gave a candidate, and no pair is out of reach.

   The reverse index applies the same rules without the "gave a candidate" condition, and it always indexes additional names by trigrams.
7. **Plan stability (not in the plan).**
   - Each per-key lateral probe and the pair lookup's holder scan are fenced with `offset 0`.
   - `retrieveCandidates` runs in one transaction that begins with `set local jit = off`. Decision 1's single statement for a search by keys is counted without that setting; the transaction adds the BEGIN, SET and COMMIT round trips. A test that records `db.execute` must also wrap `transaction`.
   - `rebuildWatchlistKeyDf` ends with `gin_clean_pending_list` on `watchlist_names_keys_idx` and `watchlist_names_normalised_trgm_idx`. Like its ANALYZE, this needs the index owner.
8. **Optional members; the benchmark interface is unchanged.** The new optional members are:
   - `RetrieveCandidatesQuery.reach`;
   - `RetrievedCandidates.capReached` and `unreachable`;
   - `ScreeningOptions.keptOnly`;
   - `LoadEntriesQuery.changedInSync`;
   - `RecurringReportSubject.config`;
   - decision 13's `WatchlistSourceState.scripts`.

   `WatchlistReads` keeps its three methods, and nothing the benchmark imports changes signature.
9. **Loads and scoring.** The analysed-name cache is an LRU of 50,000 names (`ANALYSED_NAME_CACHE_SIZE`). There are six scoring shortcuts, switched by `useScreeningShortcuts`: name-cache, unit-cache, shared-matrix, minimum-floor with `keptOnly`, pair-memo and exact-stop. Each has equivalence tests that keep every kept match identical at every preset, and a new shortcut must have them too.
10. **The re-screen (decision 3).**
    - The changed entries are read and prepared once and held in memory up to `RESCREEN_ENTRY_MEMORY` (20,000). Beyond that they are re-read for each chunk.
    - Each candidate report is screened at its own pinned configuration, through the exported screening specs of the watchlist, business-watchlist and politically-exposed-person jobs, with `keptOnly` and the preset's floor.
    - A PEP relative stored without `relative_of_class` counts as "cannot decide", so it gets a run.
    - Stale evidence is found through the `entry_index` blind index, per environment. This needs the `blindIndexKey` dependency. Without it, the re-screen logs a warning and gives a run to every report with a live match on the source.
    - `apps/api/src/worker.ts` (frozen in Stage 1) does not pass the key yet; this was checked at Stage 4. `fix-6c` adds `blindIndexKey: options.blindIndexKey` to the `reportRescreenProcessor` call, with a worker test. In the same change, the stale-evidence lookup and the run insert must stay within the driver's bind-parameter limit (review finding C001).
    - The result reports the matched and stale counts.
11. **A `list_update` run screens its trigger's changes.** It screens every live entry that its trigger sync added or changed on the run's screenable sources, in pages of 1,000 (`loadEntries.changedInSync`), beside retrieval.
12. **The script read (decision 13).** `LIST_NAME_SCRIPTS` in `packages/core/src/report-matching.ts` copies C58's `WRITING_SYSTEMS`, and a test ties the two together. The read looks up exact `#script:<script>` keys, because a key range behaves differently under PGlite's collation.
13. **Version moves (decisions 4 and 19).**
    - `SCORER_VERSION` is `scorer-2`.
    - `KEYS_VERSION` is `names-2`. The golden string 'names-2;identifiers-1;crypto-1' hashes to 97966ecbcc8d3131cd67300915b72cdce2cb73f62f8deb2525d8ebea61ed58d6.
    - `SHIPPED_VARIANT_GROUPS_SHA256` is 29d0583854563f81084c05d78b5ea8805ca14630cbf655349cfa09fca84c3bfb.
    - The variant-group file adds `license`, `copyright` and `licenseText` after the header members that the benchmarks read.
    - A source stored under an older keys version answers `matcher_data_unavailable` until it is re-keyed, by `watchlists:rekey` or by its next sync.
14. **The C78.7 gate (decision 2).**
    - The machine-independent checks always run: listed found 150 of 150, candidates p95 below the cap, the cap binding in at most 6 of 300 searches, and a would-be `result_truncated` in at most 3 of 300.
    - The p95 of at most 100 ms is asserted only with `OPENVERIFY_C787_TIMING=assert`.
    - A plan test checks that no statement scans `watchlist_entries`. It also checks that statements looking names up by their keys read `watchlist_names` only by bitmap scans: for the key statement, also while 2,000 written names are pending; for the pair lookup, after `rebuildWatchlistKeyDf`. The trigram statement's access is left to the planner.
15. **Borrowed edit (accepted).** `packages/db/src/repositories/watchlists.test.ts` (owned by `ingestion-6c`) gained `scripts: []` in one expected `WatchlistSourceState`, in c78fa9bf.
16. **Its own existing tests changed (accepted).**
    - The reverse-index case from slice 7 now meets its out-of-reach subject through a pair key, not trigrams.
    - `rescreen-coverage.test.ts` asserts that the runs cover every report whose scheduled screening matches a changed entry, that each run's `list_update` screening keeps that match, and that there are fewer than 165 runs. It used to assert exact equality.
    - Slice 15's pair-lookup expectations went back to the cap in slice 22.
    - The one-statement test counts statements without `set local jit = off`.
    - The integration plan test's names assertion covers only the statements that look names up by their keys.
17. **Write cost is reported, not budgeted.**
    - The 2,000,000-name import took 1,819 s at the base and 3,509 s at the head, in 8 parts.
    - A re-key took 2,414 s at 2,000,000 names, and 64 to 70 s at 100,000.
    - A key-frequency rebuild took 184 s at 2,000,000 names.
    - At 2,000,000 names the names table grew from 999 MB to 2,166 MB.

    Sizing the import steps belongs to the benchmark's owner. No migration 0022 was added.
18. **Development gaps.** The development tables gave a 2,000,000-name p95 of 625.7 ms against 250 ms, and 92.8 and 105.8 ms against 100 ms at 100,000 names. The re-screen took 136.7 s against 60 s, and 3 typo cases were lost at 2,000,000 names. The calibration's numbers below supersede these.

### ingestion-6c

1. **Record member names (decision 9).** In `watchlist_entries.data` the members are snake_case, `list_memberships` and `relative_of_class` (Stage 1a amendment 24). The kebab-case names belong to the API evidence (api-reports-6c item 8).
2. **`list_memberships`.**
   - It holds 1 to 100 items of `{list (≤ 200), program (≤ 200) | null}`, and is never empty: `finishRecord` leaves an empty list out, so `[]` and absence cannot hash differently.
   - `program` is nullable. OFAC's enhanced XSD (SHA-256 0c56f9f2…2b97f) ties no programme to a list. So a membership names a programme only when the entry is on exactly that one list, which gives one membership per programme. Otherwise `program` is `null`, including for a single list with no programme. Every programme stays in `programs`.
3. **Which OFAC lists are memberships.** Every list other than the SDN List, in whichever OFAC file the entry appears. Lists are compared by their cleaned lower-case name, taken once each in the file's order, and cut to 200 characters. An SDN-only entry carries no member, so its hashes are unchanged.
4. **`relative_of_class`.**
   - It is an integer from 1 to 4, and the schema refuses it on a record without `relations`.
   - The PEP build sets it on every record with `relations`, a holder who is also a relative included. Its value is the most senior (lowest) class of the PEPs the record relates to, where each PEP's class is the most senior class of its kept positions.
   - In practice it is 1 or 2: relatives are synced only for class 1 and 2 holders, and close associates are not built (C80.3).
   - Accepted consequence: a template whose `pep-classes` leaves out class 1 admits fewer relatives than the holder-based rule did, for a relative of a PEP who holds both a class-1 position and a lower-class one. A record without the member keeps the holder-based rule, as `matcher-6c` built it.
5. **Normalised imports and the sandbox list.** Only the PEP build derives the class; a normalised import carries it as written. The sandbox PEP relative `Q-test-1002` carries `relative_of_class: 1`, in the generator and in the committed fixture. A stack seeded before 6C re-screens that one entry once, at its next `seed:dev` import.
6. **The hash projection.** `riskDataHash` adds both members with the value `undefined` when they are absent, and the canonical JSON leaves that out. `list_memberships` is hashed as a set of `{list, program}`. Every 6A golden vector holds, and the new vectors are pinned in `record.test.ts`. `contentHash` is unchanged.
7. **No `alias-quality`.** `names[].quality = weak` already marks OFAC's low-quality names (`isLowQuality`), and the EU, UK and UN parsers do the same.
8. **No change signal (decision 10).** C76.1's `change_signal` manifest member, C76.2 step 2 and C77.4 are void.
   - Every signal reader (`parsers/signals.ts`) and the synthetic signal answers (`testing/formats/signals.ts`) are removed, together with `WATCHLIST_CHANGE_SIGNALS`. The manifest's strict schema refuses the member.
   - Every parser reads its publication time from the file itself; the PEP build uses the sync's time.
   - ADR 0005 §3 still names the change signal. `fix-6c` drops "change signal," from the manifest's contents, and adds that every check downloads the publication and compares its SHA-256. In the same paragraph, it adds that the one redirect stays on the request's own scheme and port.
9. **The redirect keeps scheme and port (decision 20).** A redirect target must have the request URL's protocol and port. The check uses `URL.port`, so a scheme's default port counts the same whether written or not. A client allowed plain HTTP can no longer change scheme on its redirect, in either direction.
10. **Existing tests changed with the plan (accepted).**
    - `manifest.test.ts`: the change-signal case is replaced, and two references to `entry.change_signal` are dropped.
    - `stream.test.ts`: one assertion now uses an http base.
    - Two test helpers take their tables as parameters, with assertions unchanged: those of `pep/build.test.ts` and `parsers/ofac.test.ts`.
11. **Process (accepted).** The sync acceptance tests (65016ac0) and the no-score guard (6240b074) passed on their first run, because the unit slices had built the behaviour. The acceptance test was shown failing under a mutated projection.
12. **The README upgrade note was written before `matcher-6c`'s sentence.** `fix-6c` reconciles `docs/reports/README.md` "Upgrading to Workflow 6C" with `matcher-6c`'s exact `watchlists:rekey` sentence. It also adds decision 12's `redact` action and decision 9's evidence members to the note. `ingestion-6c` made no borrowed edit and touched no frozen file.

### api-reports-6c

1. **The OTHER line type (decision 14).** "The level stays null unless SIM swap sets it" means: unless a SIM swap within 24 h reaches `high`. With OTHER, a SIM swap within 7 days (`medium-high`) or a port within 30 days (`medium`) leaves the level null, and the level is `undetermined` in `unavailable-fields`.
2. **The phone constants.**
   - `phone-confidence-prepaid` and `phone-confidence-payphone` are 0.4 each, provisional, weighed like a recent port.
   - `PHONE_RISK_REASONS` lists the type reasons in level order, then the provider reasons: `prepaid` and `payphone` come after `voip`, and `other_line_type` after `line_type_undetermined`.
   - `other_line_type` adds no reason confidence.
   - The old test in `phone/report.test.ts` that expected prepaid to give a null level and no reason is replaced by the decision's mapping tests. Its OTHER-with-SIM-swap case is kept.
3. **Where `pii:view` is checked (decision 16).** `requirePiiView(requirePrincipal(request))` runs in both route handlers, after the `reports:review` scope check and before the service. So the 403 comes before any 404, 409 or 410, and the service functions do not check it. No existing flow reviewed as a principal without `pii:view`, so no test changed. The API is now as strict as the console was (this supersedes the 6B dashboard-reports amendment 4's "the API is unchanged").
4. **The sources document (decision 17).** `matcher` is `{loaded, keys-version}`, with `keys-version` served exactly when `loaded` is true. It is read from `loadMatcherData()`; `matcher-data.ts` is unchanged. `media.attribution` is the adapter's `GDELT_CITATION`. Each template warning carries `name` and `report-type`.
5. **The media load (decision 18).**
   - `media.scheduled-runs-per-day` is removed. `environment-scheduled-runs-per-day` and `install-above-warn-share` are added.
   - The flag means strictly above 80% of 17,280, so 13,824 is not above.
   - The environment's figure counts recurring, unpaused and unredacted media reports, as the install's figure does. It is not the creation cap's count, which includes paused reports.
   - `scheduledMediaRunLoad(db, environmentId)` reads both sums in one statement and replaces `scheduledInstallMediaRunsPerDay`.
6. **Tests changed for decisions 17 and 18 (accepted).** In `watchlist-sources.test.ts`, the inputs and expectations take the new shape. In `report-repositories.test.ts`, the source-list expectation gains `name`, and the install-only media test became the test of `scheduledMediaRunLoad`. The `dashboard-watchlist-sources` contract fixture is in the new shape.
7. **The redaction actor (decision 12).**
   - `ReportRedactionOptions.actor` is required: a `user` or `api_key` with its id, or `system` with a null id.
   - `redactReportInTx` writes the `redact` action on a first redaction only, after `redactReportRecords`, with null details. `insertReportAction` stores no ciphertext for null details. The audit entry's `action-count` still counts only the actions that were redacted.
   - The inquiry cascade, the account cascade and its catch-up write `system`, whoever redacted the parent. Retention writes `system` too.
8. **The evidence members (decision 9).**
   - `list-memberships` sits on `listEntryBase`, so person, business and crypto entities all take it: 1 to 100 items of `{list (1–200), program (1–200) | null}`, absent rather than empty.
   - `relative-of-class` (1 to 4) is on the PEP entity, refined to `pep-type: relative`.
   - Both are optional. `recordEvidence(record)` maps the record's members only when they are present, and the API serves the snapshot's entity as stored.
   - At Stage 4, no snapshot builder calls `recordEvidence` yet, so no run writes the members. `fix-6c` wires it test-first:
     - `...recordEvidence(record)` in the entity callbacks of the watchlist, business-watchlist and crypto-address-watchlist jobs;
     - the relative's class on the PEP result entity;
     - a job test for each.
9. **Races (decision 11) and an amendment to C84.2.** A completion whose inquiry row is already redacted when the transition updates it creates no report (71ad3bf8). All six orders are in `report-races.integration.test.ts`. The transition itself still moves a redacted pending inquiry to `completed` and stages `inquiry.completed`, because those files were frozen. `fix-6c` brings that to the owner, with two options: guard `transitionInquiryStatus` with `redacted_at is null` for system transitions, or lock the inquiry in `settleInquiry`.
10. **Decision 15.** `forgetStoredResponses` is exported from `redaction.ts`, and so is public through the index's `export *`. K6A-10 is pinned against the job's `UNDETERMINED_LANGUAGE`.
11. **OpenAPI timing (accepted once).** c19d96d2 left `docs/api/openapi.json` without the three phone reasons until f57e971e. The rule stands: a contract change regenerates the document in the same commit.
12. **An unexplained integration failure.** One `test:integration` run failed `trust-anchors/sync-races.integration.test.ts` and `routes/flow/one-time-links.integration.test.ts` at file level, under load. Both passed alone and in two later full runs. `fix-6c` captures the cause under load and makes the failing hook deterministic.
