### console-tests-6c

1. **Without the API's matcher data (decision 17).**
   - When `matcher.loaded` is false, a checked source shows the state `keys_unknown`, "The API has no variant groups". Its tone is attention, and its detail names `python3 scripts/fetch_data.py` and `build:variant-groups`. This replaces "Needs re-key" in that case.
   - Freshness is still shown. A disabled, unconfigured or never-checked source shows nothing new.
   - "Screenable now" reads "Not known: the API has no variant groups".
   - A "Matcher data" term in the summary gives the keys version, or says that the API has none.
   - "Need attention" does not count `keys_unknown`.
   - This supersedes the 6B dashboard-report-config amendment 3.
2. **"Screenable now" leaves out a source keyed by another matcher version.** A fresh source, or a sandbox list in a sandbox environment, counts only when it is not keyed by another matcher version.
3. **Template warnings and the GDELT line.** A warned template shows its `name` above its id, linked to `/report-templates/<id>` for `report-templates:read`, with a Type column. The GDELT line is the API's `media.attribution`, and the console's `GDELT_ATTRIBUTION` is removed. This supersedes the 6B dashboard-report-config amendments 1 and 2.
4. **The media words (decision 18).** The line gives the environment's own scheduled runs and their share of the install's GDELT budget, which every environment of the install shares. The badge "The install is above 80% of the budget" follows `install-above-warn-share` only, never the environment's own share. No other tenant's figure appears.
5. **Complete coverage (decision 6).** `COMPLETE_COVERAGE_WORDS` is `matcher-6c`'s sentence, with the console's typographic apostrophe. It shows under "Complete" in the report detail's Coverage term, only for `watchlist`, `business-watchlist` and `politically-exposed-person`. The reports index cell and the history's run coverage still read "Complete" alone.
6. **List memberships and a relative's class (decision 9).**
   - The matched-list entry tables gain an "Other lists" column. Each membership reads "<list> (programme <p>)", or "<list> (no programme tied to this list)" when `program` is null; the cell reads "None" when there are no memberships.
   - When any entry carries memberships, `LIST_MEMBERSHIPS_NOTE` says that a listing on those lists alone is not an SDN-level block.
   - A crypto entry shows "Other lists of <entry>" when it has memberships.
   - A PEP relative shows "Class of the related PEP", or "Not recorded: the list was synced before relatives carried their class". A holder shows no such term.
7. **Query lengths (decision 24).** Each create-form input takes its `maxLength` from `REPORT_QUERY_SCHEMAS` (`queryMaxLength`, `lib/report-query-limits.ts`). Additional-name parts take theirs from the element schema. A member whose schema states no maximum gets no `maxlength`: LEI, IMO, MMSI, SWIFT/BIC, aircraft tail, E.164, birth date and country codes. Its pattern and the API decide.
8. **The `pii:view` refusal in words (decision 16).** The console's own refusal and any API 403 on a dismissal or review answer `REVIEW_NEEDS_PII_VIEW`. Its title stays "You do not have access". Its detail names both permissions and why, and it keeps the API's status and request id. The mapping lives in `reviewAction` (`app/reports/actions.ts`), so that `lib/report-problem-words.ts` stays free of the server-only errors module.
9. **The redaction actor in words (decision 12).** "By" reads "User <usr_ id>", "API key <key_ id>" or "System". A system `redact` reads `REDACTED_BY_SYSTEM`: "System: the redaction of its inquiry or account, or the report retention setting".
10. **The records README rows (decision 26, P5-8).** The dashboard README gains a records-types table. `apps/dashboard/src/readme-records.test.ts` holds each row's pass sentence to the view's own pass line. The documents views keep their file-header descriptions.
11. **K6A-5.** All 31 Phase 1–5 Testcontainers files start Postgres with `POSTGRES_TEST_COMMAND`; only their imports and container chains changed. A guard test in `packages/db/src/testing.test.ts` scans `apps/*/{src,scripts}` and `packages/*/{src,scripts}`. Its one exemption is the log test's control container in `postgres-logging.integration.test.ts` (6B fix-api amendment 15).
12. **P5-6 found the files by test, not by grep.** The db suite was run under a clock that steps back on every read. That found 28 tests in 20 Phase 1–5 files, where the plan estimated about 16, including oldest-first lists and "latest" picks. Each of them also stays under `onTestFinished(wallClockStepsBack())` as a guard, and every assertion is unchanged.
13. **`createdAtClock`.**
    - It starts at the real wall clock, and its stamps are one second apart.
    - It also sets `updated_at` where the table has one.
    - It writes in its own transaction with `session_replication_role = replica`, so no row trigger runs: the append-only `audit_logs` accepts the stamp, and `updated_at` is not bumped again.
    - It finds rows by any key column.
14. **Left out, with reasons.** `constraints.test.ts` and `constraints-v2.test.ts` assert that a trigger moves `updated_at` forward, which is not an ordering assertion. Phase 6's `report-repositories.test.ts` is not a Phase 1–5 file. Both are follow-ups.
15. **Existing tests changed with the plan (accepted).**
    - `watchlist-sources-view.test.tsx`: a warning row is found by its template's name, and the badge text changed.
    - `source-words.test.ts`: the test of `GDELT_ATTRIBUTION` became one that checks it is gone.
    - `e2e/tests/ui-reports.spec.ts`: the Coverage term matches "Complete" followed by decision 6's sentence.
    - The 28 db ordering tests and the 31 integration files, as above.
16. **Process (accepted).** Two of the agent's own RED tests were corrected before their GREEN, and both corrections are recorded in its TDD file. The e2e spec updates are acceptance checks of slices already built test-first, with no RED of their own.

### calibration-6c

**What was measured.**
- The seed is e8223c34916200e2b8e51f0b47981804, drawn with `openssl rand -hex 16` at 2026-10-29T02:04:51Z, after the method commit e0c7cebe.
- The base is 94ab63f9, with the head's `benchmark-*` and `held-out/` overlaid. The head is e880a9fc.
- Every generated file was byte-identical in the two trees, and the V12 independence assertion passed.

**Deviations, binding as recorded:**
1. **What a take is.**
   - A screening take is one measure round: one `--rounds 1` call, with 20 warm-ups and then 300 subjects. The headline is the median of the three round p95s, with the pooled p95 beside it.
   - A re-screen take is one `rescreen-<size>` on its own freshly built 100,000-name database (namespaces `-r2` and `-r3`).
   - A gate take is one run of the gate test.
   - A take whose 1-minute load exceeded 4 at its start or end was discarded and retaken.
2. **The decision rules, committed before the seed (e0c7cebe).** These are the rules for any later re-measurement:
   - **Decision 1** is kept when all 12 comparisons of "Recall, all cases" (persons and companies of the scale population, 3 presets, 2 scales) have a paired 95% lower bound of at least −1 pp, and the median round p95 falls at both scales. The 6B set is reported but does not bind.
   - **Decision 4** applies at `tolerant` only, for person and company customers at both scales.
   - **Decision 5's** "measurable" means non-degenerate at each type's default preset: at least one false alert and one clean customer, and at least one expected match and one other match.
   - **Decision 3** is kept when no subject is missed and the head is faster at 1,000 entries. A time over 60 s is reported with its gap.
3. **The no-miss check ran outside git.** It used a scratch script, `check-rescreen.mjs`, which calls only the head's `benchmark-postgres.mjs`, its watchlist job and its repository. `fix-6c` commits it as a `rescreen-check --size <n>` step, with a tested pure comparison helper.
4. **The 2,000,000-name `scale-prepare` ran with `NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=12288`.** `fix-6c` adds that flag to the `benchmark:quality` script, as `benchmark:performance` already has it.
5. **Calls over the tool's 10-minute cap ran in the background.** The base's 2,000,000-name `scale-screen` checks its 8-minute budget only after each batch of 200 queries, so a call took about 11 minutes. Each call was waited for before the next step. `fix-6c` checks the budget after every query, test-first.
6. **The re-screen sizes outside the target.** The 3,000-entry size was taken once per tree, and the base's take started above the load rule. The head's 100-entry size has two valid takes (median 53.2 s).
7. **"The cap binds" counts the first round's searches with at least 2,000 candidates.** The step's own column counts `truncated`, which at the head includes out-of-reach searches. Both are reported. `fix-6c` records `capReached` and `unreachable` separately in the benchmark, test-first.
8. **Two runs are context only:** the base's own C78.7 gate (73.2 ms, candidates p95 260), and a final reproduction of the head's 100,000-name steps (93.0 ms).
9. **No ablation was run.** Each constant and rule takes the verdict of its decision. Decisions 13 and 9 were not measured, and decision 19 is not a matching rule.
10. **No code was changed.** No benchmark step needed a fix, so the agent has no RED or GREEN commit.

**Verdicts (binding).** No commit was reverted.

| Decision, constant or rule | Verdict | Numbers |
|---|---|---|
| Decision 1: the absolute key bound, pair keys, the folded rare-key lookup, the load and scoring shortcuts | kept | All 12 recall lower bounds are ≥ +0.0 pp (persons at `tolerant`: +14.3 pp at 100,000 names, +14.2 at 2,000,000). Median round p95 282.8 → 109.5 ms (100,000 names) and 1,529.9 → 399.4 ms (2,000,000). The cap binds 129 → 6 of 300; `result_truncated` 19 → 0 of 300, and 0.55% on the scale population at `tolerant`. |
| Decision 2: the C78.7 gate on a heavy-headed list with the shipped groups | kept | Three asserted takes: 81.8 / 75.4 / 70.8 ms, median 75.4 ms; candidates p95 503; listed found 150 of 150. |
| Decision 3: the faster forward screen of the re-screen | kept | At 1,000 entries and 200,000 subjects: median 97.7 → 95.1 s. Runs 11,312 → 2,465; run cost 487 → 52 s. 0 of 2,429 missed (Wilson 0–0.16%). |
| Decision 4: the OSA typo rule (`TYPO_EDIT_DISTANCE`, `scorer-2`) | kept | Person false alerts per 1,000 at `tolerant`: −12.7 (100,000 names) and −90.1 (2,000,000). Companies unchanged. Transposition recall 0 → 86.0% / 84.7% (persons) and 50.0% (companies). |
| Decision 5: the held-out set measures false alerts | kept | Non-degenerate at both scales. Persons at `tolerant`: 261 of 1,500 alerted (100,000 names), 692 of 1,499 (2,000,000). Companies at `strict`: 120 of 484. |
| `key-frequency-bound-share` 0.25 | kept | Decision 1's numbers. |
| Pair keys, `pair-max-tokens` 6, `pair-max-groups` 4 | kept | Strict persons at 100,000 names: dropped middle 144 → 150, initials 138 → 150. |
| Reach and truncation, the out-of-reach pair lookup up to the cap, the trigram conditions | kept | Truncated 15 of 300 at 2,000,000 names. Typos 117 → 116 of 150 (−0.7 pp, −4.5 to +3.2), within the margin. |
| Key frequencies over live entries, rows only for shared keys | kept | Key-frequency table 508 → 225 MB at 2,000,000 names. |
| The folded statement, fenced probes, JIT off for retrieval, the GIN pending lists emptied | kept | Retrieval p95 74.4 → 18.9 ms (100,000 names) and 878.8 → 35.9 ms (2,000,000). |
| The six shortcuts and `ANALYSED_NAME_CACHE_SIZE` 50,000 | kept | Rest-phase p95 161.4 → 69.4 ms and 624.1 → 301.6 ms. |
| The gate's machine-independent checks, timing only under `OPENVERIFY_C787_TIMING=assert` | kept | All pass in 3 takes. |
| The re-screen rules (pinned config with `keptOnly`, runs when kept or undecidable, stale-evidence runs, `list_update` screening its trigger's changes, `RESCREEN_ENTRY_MEMORY` 20,000) | kept | 0 missed, 36 extra runs. |
| Decision 13: per-source scripts in coverage | not measured | No rule was fixed for it, and no scale case is in a lossy script. |
| Decision 19: the MIT notice in the variant-group file | kept | Both builds hold the same groups (156,706 shipped, 140,875 hold-out); only the digests differ. |
| Decision 9, matcher side: the PEP class filter reads `relative_of_class` | not measured | No set holds PEP positions, classes or relatives. |

**What the verdicts leave open.** Each of these is a finding for the owner, and no budget moves without the owner:
11. **Two budgets are still missed.** At 100,000 names the head gives 109.5 ms against 100 ms (93.0 ms on a quieter reproduction). At 2,000,000 names it gives 399.4 ms against 250 ms; scoring takes 301.6 ms of that p95, on namesake floods.
12. **The re-screen misses its target.** It takes 95.1 s against the 60 s target at 1,000 entries. Small syncs regress: 53.2 s against 25.2 s at 100 entries, and 63.4 s against 42.7 s at 300.
13. **The company figures are limited.**
    - The bootstrap counts a false match only in the replicates that draw its identity, so for companies the interval excludes its own point estimate. The point estimates and Wilson intervals are correct, and no verdict rests on those intervals. Changing the definition needs a new calibration.
    - Company customers meet only the population's 400 listed companies, so the company rates are the same at both scales and do not measure a large company list.
14. **The gate's list is lighter than the calibration's 100,000-name table.** The same head gives 109.5 ms on that table, with a candidates p95 of 630. Either the gate's generator is aligned with `benchmark-tables.mjs`, or its header states that a pass is not the calibration's budget.
15. **`result_truncated` misses the 1% target at the stricter presets.** At 2,000,000 names on the scale population it is 1.33% at `strict` and 3.26% at `exact`. The default `tolerant` meets the target (0.55%).
16. **The constants' source strings still say "fixed by" the calibration.** `fix-6c` changes them to "kept by docs/calibration/watchlist-phase6c.md", naming the seed and the evidence, and keeps the basis `provisional`.

## Workflow 6C review amendments

Recorded by the Workflow 6C Stage 5 fixer (`fix-6c`) on 2026-10-29, for its own changes, as plan owner. The evidence is in `docs/tdd/phase6-review-fixes-6c.tdd.md`, and the re-measurement in `docs/calibration/watchlist-phase6c.md`. Each item is binding for later phases.

1. **The re-screen's id lists are bounded per statement (review C001; amends the frozen `report-rescreen.ts` reads of 6A Stage 1b).** `listReportsWithLiveMatches` takes its `entryIndexes` and `insertListUpdateRuns` its `reportIds` 500 to a statement (`chunks`, the `BATCH` of the sync's writes), because one bound parameter per id passes the protocol's 65,535 for a large sync or environment. The lookup returns the reports of every statement, distinct and sorted; without `entryIndexes` it is still one statement. The insert returns the sum of each statement's runs, and each statement stays idempotent on `(report_id, trigger_sync_id)`, so a re-screen that fails between two statements inserts the rest on its retry. The tests count the parameters each statement binds (a spy on the PGlite client), at most 1,000, instead of sending 65,536 ids through PGlite, which misreads long parameter lists.
2. **The worker passes the blind-index key to the re-screen (matcher-6c amendment 10, done).** `startWorkers` gives `reportRescreenProcessor` `blindIndexKey: options.blindIndexKey`, so the deployed re-screen gives stale-evidence runs only to the reports with an open or delisted match on a changed entry, found by `entry_index`, and no longer logs that it runs without the key.
3. **Decision 9's evidence reaches the snapshots (api-reports-6c amendment 8, done).** Two narrow helpers in `jobs/report-types/record-evidence.ts`:
   - `listEvidence(record)`: a sanctions entity (person, business, crypto address) takes `list-memberships` only, because its strict schema has no PEP class;
   - `relativeEvidence(record)`: a PEP relative's entity takes `relative-of-class`; a holder's takes none, as the contract's refinement requires.

   The watchlist and business-watchlist entity callbacks, `screenCrypto`'s entity and the PEP entity callback spread them. The evidence hash does not read the entity, so no match lapses or alerts because of them alone; a match on an entry that gains a member is re-scored through the change of risk data, as `docs/reports/README.md`'s upgrade note now says.
4. **The constants' sources name the calibration that kept them (calibration-6c item 16).** `RETRIEVAL_6C` ends "kept by docs/calibration/watchlist-phase6c.md (evaluation seed e8223c34916200e2b8e51f0b47981804, decision 1)", and `TYPO_EDIT_DISTANCE.source` ends "kept by docs/calibration/watchlist-phase6c.md: no rise in false alerts (persons −12.7 and −90.1 per 1,000 at 100,000 and 2,000,000 names, companies unchanged)". No value changed, and the basis stays `provisional`.
5. **The benchmark (calibration-6c deviations 3 to 5 and 7, and review C003).**
   - `benchmark:quality` runs with `--max-old-space-size=12288`, as `benchmark:performance` does; `benchmark-gates.test.mjs` checks both.
   - `stepWriter` (`benchmark-scale.mjs`) is the observation writer of `screen`, `scale-screen` and `<scale>-variants`: lines are appended a batch at a time, and the 8-minute budget is checked after every query, with what is pending written before the step stops. What each step writes, and when it exits 3, is unchanged.
   - Each retrieval records `capReached` and `unreachable` beside `truncated` (a tree before 6C answers without them: `capReached` is then `truncated` and `unreachable` false). `PerformanceSummary` gains `capReached` and `unreachable` shares of the first round, `null` when a round file lacks the member. `capBinds` keeps counting `truncated`, the figure the calibration published, now documented as such. The performance table renders "Truncated (first round)", "Cut at the cap" and "Out of reach", and the compacted-round table "Truncated", "Cut at the cap" and "Out of reach"; a summary without the members reads "not recorded". The golden test digests the summary without the two new members and checks them `null`, so its pin covers what it held at the base, with the same digest.
   - **`rescreen-check --size <n> [--workers <k> --worker <i>]`** (review C003) is decision 3's no-miss check as a committed step, after `rescreen-<n>` and before the next size: it refuses unless the last applied sync added `n` entries; it screens every recurring subject with the `watchlist` job at its pinned configuration resolved as a run resolves it (`resolveReportTemplateConfig`), against the whole table, with nothing persisted; it counts, through the tested `rescreenSubject` and `rescreenCheck` (`benchmark-posthoc.mjs`), the subjects with a kept match on a changed entry, those with a `list_update` run, the misses with a Wilson interval, and the runs without such a match. It resumes from every worker's file, reading complete lines only (`rescreenSubjectLines`, so a worker never fails on another's write in progress), exits 3 while a subject is unscreened, and writes `rescreen-check-<n>/summary.json`. Lines hold report ids and counts only.
   - `rescreen-<n>` passes a synthetic blind-index key (SHA-256 of `<seed>/rescreen/blind-index`) to `processReportRescreen`, as the worker now does, so a measurement covers the stale-evidence lookup by `entry_index`. The benchmark's reports hold no match, so the key changes no run.
   - **The scale tables mark a biased interval (review C002, interim).** An interval that excludes its own point estimate reads "which excludes the point", with the point's Wilson interval beside it and a note under the table. The bootstrap's definition (`benchmark-paired.mjs`, `bootstrapQuality`'s) is unchanged: changing it is the owner's, with a new calibration (calibration-6c item 13). The calibration report's company tables carry the marks.
6. **Test hygiene (console-tests-6c item 14).** `report-repositories.test.ts`'s match-writing test stamps the lapse a minute after the open match; `constraints.test.ts` and `constraints-v2.test.ts` stamp the row's `updated_at` an hour back without its trigger (`session_replication_role = replica`), check the stamp, and assert that the update moved it past that instant. All three run under `onTestFinished(wallClockStepsBack())`. No assertion is weaker.
7. **Documentation.**
   - ADR 0005 §3 drops the change signal from the manifest's contents, says that every check downloads the publication and compares its SHA-256 with no change signal read (decision 10), and that the one redirect stays on the request's own scheme and port (decision 20).
   - `docs/reports/README.md`: the 6C upgrade note takes matcher-6c's re-key sentence and a third step on the decision 9 evidence and the decision 12 `redact` action; Privacy says a redaction writes that action and who it names; Monitoring describes the re-screen as decision 3 built it, at each report's pinned configuration, in place of "the most permissive configuration a template can hold".
   - `docs/calibration/watchlist-phase6.md`'s "Not reproduced by committed code" notes name the 6C steps that now reproduce its figures (`typo-kinds`, `<scale>-unlisted`) and the compacted-round step.
8. **The calibration's verdicts stand; nothing is reverted.** Every decision was kept or not measured, so `fix-6c` reverted no commit. Items 1, 2 and 5 touch the re-screen, so decision 3's head numbers were taken again at `f19085a0`, with the calibration's commands, seed and timing rule (three fresh 100,000-name databases with 200,000 subjects for each size, loads 1.3 to 2.4): 49.5 s at 100 added entries, 62.0 s at 300 and 91.0 s at 1,000 (medians), with the calibration's run counts (171, 2,270, 2,465); the committed `rescreen-check` finds 0 missed of 2,429 and 36 extra runs, the scratch script's counts exactly. Decision 3 stays kept under its rule (91.0 s against the base's 97.7 s); the 60 s target is missed by 31.0 s. They are recorded in `docs/calibration/watchlist-phase6c.md`, "Re-measurement after the Workflow 6C review", beside decision 3's verdict. Nothing else that the calibration measured changed: no matching code, constant, threshold, preset or cap, and no retrieval read.
9. **Carried, with the reason** (each for its owner; the evidence is in the TDD file):
   - **C004:** a `list_update` run holds and scores every entry its trigger sync changed. The local prefilter needs a key-frequency read in the frozen `WatchlistReads`; a cap would be a new C78.4 constant. Proposed: a frequency read in the kit, then a one-subject reverse index over each page of changed entries, scored page by page.
   - **The re-screen's fixed cost at small syncs** (calibration finding 2). At 200,000 subjects: key frequencies 15.0 s (about 400,000 keys a chunk), preparing the index 9.4 s, the subjects' screening specs 8.4 s, reading the subjects 6.5 s, building the index 4.6 s. Proposed: frequencies kept across chunks or read once per re-screen, then a re-measurement.
   - **A redacted pending inquiry can still settle** (api-reports-6c item 9). Recommended: lock the inquiry in `settleInquiry` and return when it is redacted, with a Postgres race test.
   - **The unexplained integration failure** (api-reports-6c item 12): not reproduced. `pnpm test:integration` passed whole at load 6.1 to 8.9 (db 12 files, 60 tests; API 27 files, 153 tests), `trust-anchors/sync-races` and `routes/flow/one-time-links` among them. Without a failure there is no RED, so nothing changed; a failing run's log is what the owner needs.
   - **The interval's definition** (C002): the marks of item 5 are the interim; the owner changes `benchmark-paired.mjs` and runs a new calibration.
   - **matcher-6c's optional performance items:** the incremental key-frequency rebuild, the re-key's batch size, the GIN pending lists during a long sync, JIT off for the whole application. Each changes write cost or plan stability and needs its own measurement.
   - **The owner's items of the calibration:** the two missed budgets and the re-screen target, the gate's lighter list (align `heavy-headed-list.ts` with `benchmark-tables.mjs`, or say in its header that a pass is not the calibration's budget), company entities in the tables, and `result_truncated` at `strict` and `exact`.
   - **Optional:** an `ui-reports.spec.ts` check of "Other lists" on a synthetic OFAC Consolidated entry, which needs such an entry in the sandbox list (ingestion's fixtures and their hashes).
10. **Process (accepted).**
    - Stub commits (`chore`) came before the RED tests of names that did not exist yet (`stepWriter`, the two summary members, `rescreenSubject` and `rescreenCheck`, `rescreenSubjectLines`), each stub carrying the old behaviour or nothing, so each RED failed on behaviour, not on a missing name.
    - Three RED tests were corrected before their commits: C001's lookup test first seeded its matches on the default source, the constants' first regex could never match, and the line reader's test did not typecheck. The golden test's digest was narrowed to the summary's pre-existing members in the RED commit of item 5, with the two new members pinned to `null`.
    - Existing tests changed with the plan: the retrieval expectation of `benchmark-reads.test.mjs`, the performance and compacted rows of `benchmark-report.test.mjs`, and the golden digest above. Each now checks more, not less.
    - `src/match/shortcuts.test.ts` (matcher-6c) timed out once at `strict` (77.6 s against 60 s) while the fixer ran the gates beside its six-worker no-miss check (load 11 to 12); on a quiet machine the package passed whole (610 tests, the file's 8 in 98.8 s). Its four equivalence cases take about 20 to 50 s each, close to the 60 s test timeout under load: a note for the matcher's owner, not changed here.

## Owner decisions after Workflow 6C (2026-10-29)

Taken by the orchestrator under the owner's standing approval ("take decisions yourself which are industry best"), recorded here and in PROGRESS. They are binding for Workflow 6D and later phases. An adversarial review of the draft returned 16 findings (3 HIGH, 7 MEDIUM, 6 LOW), and all of them are applied below. It is summarised in "Review of this addendum".

**Where Phase 6 stands after 6C.** Workflow 6C (`94ab63f9..7ced006b`, run `wf_f5b00ff4-1ba`) met its own acceptance:
- `docs/calibration/watchlist-phase6c.md` publishes C78.7 at both scales and C78.8 with false alerts measurable, at 6C's base (`94ab63f9`) and Stage 1's head (`e880a9fc`), on the evaluation seed `e8223c34916200e2b8e51f0b47981804`.
- All five decisions after 6B, and every changed constant and rule, were kept.
- The review's one HIGH finding (C001, unbounded bind parameters in the re-screen) was confirmed and fixed.

The Phase 6 acceptance line "Matching meets the C78.7 budgets on synthetic tables" is still not met:
- **100,000 names:** a screening p95 of 109.5 ms against 100 ms, the median of three takes at loads of 3.4 to 3.8, beside another Stage 2 agent. A quieter reproduction gave 93.0 ms.
- **2,000,000 names:** 399.4 ms against 250 ms. The p95 of "the rest", mostly scoring, is 301.6 ms of it.
- **The re-screen:** 91.0 s at 1,000 changed entries and 200,000 subjects, against 60 s. At 100 entries it takes 49.5 s, where 6C's base took 25.2 s.

Two confirmed defects in Phase 6 code were carried by the fixer:
- review C004: a `list_update` run holds every entry its trigger sync changed;
- api-reports-6c item 9: a redacted pending inquiry can still settle.

1. **The C78.7 budgets do not move.** Owner decision 1 after 6B: a missed budget is reported with its gap, the owner revisits it, and it is never moved to fit.
   - **The budgets.** At 100,000 names the budget stays 100 ms p95. At 2,000,000 names (PEP scale) it stays 250 ms p95. The re-screen stays 60 s at 1,000 changed entries and 200,000 subjects. 6D works toward each one.
   - **Why no budget moves.** A budget changed after the number it judges has been measured is moved to fit, and every scale is screened the same way, inside report jobs behind the outbox sweep. The change that came closest at 2,000,000 names was limiting the out-of-reach pair lookup to the key bound: 319.9 ms on matcher-6c's development tables, against their 625.7 ms. It lost 3 listed namesakes, and this plan never trades recall for latency.
   - **The base beside the head.** 6D's Stage 2 runs alone on the machine, so its base (6C's head) may already measure differently from 6C's loaded takes. The calibration states the base's figures beside the head's.
   - **The stopping rule: after 6D, these figures are final for Phase 6.** If 6D's calibration still misses a budget, PROGRESS records the gap as an open line of the Phase 6 acceptance, with its profile. It is carried to Phase 11's performance hardening, measured with `benchmark:performance` beside the HTTP load test, and added to PLAN §6 Phase 11. There is no further Phase 6 Workflow. The acceptance line reads "Matching meets the C78.7 budgets on synthetic tables, or the gap is recorded under owner decision 1 after Workflow 6C".
2. **Workflow 6D closes Phase 6.** Its scope is exactly decisions 3 to 8. Everything else is carried, under decision 9.
3. **The 100,000-name budget: profile first, then remove repeated work** (`matcher-6d`).
   - **Profile.** The screening is profiled at both scales on development tables from a fresh development seed, with the phase split (state, retrieval, loads, rest).
   - **What may change.** A change is allowed only if it leaves every created match identical. That is tested as the 6C shortcuts are (`src/match/shortcuts.test.ts`), on seeded subjects at every preset, and the change is switchable so the calibration can measure its share.
   - **"No constant changes"** means C78.4's matching constants, thresholds, presets and caps. A performance constant such as `ANALYSED_NAME_CACHE_SIZE` may change when the equivalence tests hold.
   - **Candidates named by 6C** (the profile decides which):
     - **Grouping namesake candidates**, so qualification, admission and match building run once per group. A group shares every field `screenEntry`, qualification and the PEP admission read: written names with their quality, primary flag, script and kind; birth dates; identifiers; weak aliases; the countries of `entryCountries(record, options.countryFields)`; gender; and positions, relations and `relative_of_class`. `matcher-6d` lists them from the code. The equivalence test adds groups that differ in exactly one of those fields each, at every preset. The pair memo already scores a shared list name once, so grouping saves analysis, qualification and building, not pair scores.
     - **The analysed-name cache** at 2,000,000 names (50,000 entries).
     - **Per-entry work** in `screenEntry` beyond the pair memo.
     - **`SET LOCAL jit = off` in `loadEntries`**, as `retrieveCandidates` already does, if its plan shows JIT time at scale.
   - **Retrieval stays as 6C built it.** The out-of-reach pair lookup up to the cap is what finds the listed namesakes.
   - **Candidates scored, not kept, drive the p95.** At 2,000,000 names the candidates are p50 151 and p95 869, against 19 kept matches per unlisted subject. So a bound that skips a candidate early is a lever, if it keeps every created match identical.
   - **`screening.ts` is not `matcher-6d`'s.** C004 restructures `screenNames`, so a lever there goes to `fix-6d` after `rescreen-6d`'s C004 commits.
   - **Tests for the call sites.** A change at a report type's call site is tested in a new `apps/api/src/jobs/report-types/watchlist-screening.test.ts`.
   - **Truncation at the stricter presets.** At 2,000,000 names `result_truncated` ends 1.33% of `strict` and 3.26% of `exact` screenings, against 0.55% at the default `tolerant`.
     - The code explains why: `result_truncated` is a truncated search that kept nothing (`screening.ts`), and a stricter preset keeps less.
     - **Owner decision:** the 1% target of decision 1 after 6B applies to the default preset. A truncated screening is reported as truncated, never as no match (C78.5).
     - `docs/reports/watchlist.md` and `docs/reports/politically-exposed-person.md` say so, in a note `matcher-6d` adds.
   - **Done before 6D, by the orchestrator.** `src/match/shortcuts.test.ts` took 90 to 157 s per test at a full validation's load, against a 60 s timeout. It now shares one baseline per preset and runs one pass per test, at most 3.8 s alone (`22d76091`).
4. **The re-screen within 60 s, and review C004** (`rescreen-6d`).
   - **Where the time goes.** From the re-measured medians (49.5 s at 100 entries, 62.0 s at 300 and 91.0 s at 1,000), the re-screen costs about 45 s fixed plus about 46 ms per changed entry. Both parts are in scope.
   - **The fixed part.**
     - The key frequencies are read once per re-screen and reused for every chunk and environment: the frequencies current when the re-screen first reads them, one consistent set. They are bound as one `text[]` parameter per statement; drizzle's per-key parameters cost 4.4 s of the 15.0 s.
     - The configuration is resolved once per pinned configuration in `subjectOf`.
     - Then whatever the new profile names.
     - `prepareReverseIndex`'s time (9.4 s) is in `queryNameKeys` and `queryNameReach` (`keys/`, `matcher-6d`'s). `rescreen-6d` changes them only as output-identical borrowed edits under a golden test.
   - **The per-entry part.** One `screenCandidates` per candidate subject over every entry its keys reach, sharing the pair memo, and a name-bound check before the screen.
   - **Small syncs.** At 100 and 300 entries, the head's total cost (the re-screen plus its runs × the screening p50) is no higher than 6D's base on 6D's seed. 6C's 25.2 s and 42.7 s are context only.
   - **C004.** A `list_update` run reads its trigger sync's changes a page at a time and scores each page with `keptOnly`.
     - **Identical by construction.** `screenCandidates` scores each candidate on its own and sorts at the end, and the pair memo lives per call. So page-wise scoring creates the same matches.
     - **What the run keeps:** the kept matches, their entries, and the per-source scripts the coverage fallback needs (`sourceScriptsOf`). It loads the PEP holders per page.
     - **No frequency prefilter.** A run executes later, through the sweep, after a later sync may have rebuilt the frequencies, so a frequency prefilter could drop an entry the forward screen kept. Any prefilter is therefore frequency-free and a superset of the reverse index for any frequencies: every query and pair key, and trigrams for every name.
     - `WatchlistReads` gains no member.
   - **C004's test.** A sync of 2.5 pages, at every preset, with a PEP relative whose holder is on another page. The run's whole result (matches, coverage, errors) equals the whole-set screening's. The development report states runs × pages read.
   - **JIT.** JIT is turned off in the re-screen's own reads if their plans show JIT time at scale: in `report-rescreen.ts`, and in `listChangedWatchlistEntries` (`watchlists.ts`, frozen otherwise) as a borrowed edit.
   - **Measured on development tables** at 100, 300 and 1,000 changed entries and 200,000 subjects. `rescreen-check` (6C) finds no miss.
5. **A redacted open inquiry never completes or fails** (`platform-6d`; api-reports-6c item 9, left to the owner by the plan). Redaction ends an inquiry's processing, but not its expiry.
   - **The guard.** `transitionInquiryStatus` adds `redacted_at is null` to its WHERE clause when `to` is `pending`, `completed` or `failed`.
   - **What it does not block.** A move to `expired`, by the sweep or the `expire` action, still applies to a redacted inquiry. A redacted open inquiry therefore ends `expired` at its `expires-at` and leaves the sweep's selection. Moves out of `completed`, `failed` and `needs_review` are unchanged.
   - **Why that closes the race.** Redaction locks the inquiry (`redactInquiryRecords`: `SELECT … FOR UPDATE`, then UPDATE). Under read committed, Postgres re-checks the conditional UPDATE's WHERE clause against the row the redaction committed, so whichever commits first wins without a new lock.
   - **Every caller sees a null transition and stages nothing:** `settleInquiry`, which `failVerificationAfterError` also reaches; the flow's attempt limits; and `startInquiry`. The sandbox's `start_inquiry`, `complete_inquiry` and `fail_inquiry` on a redacted inquiry answer 409 `invalid-transition`, the documented answer (the action routes document no 410, and the routes and OpenAPI are frozen).
   - **What it amends.** Phase 3's G002 (`docs/plans/phase-3.plan.md`, item 3): the verification still fails without data, and the inquiry now stays open until it expires. Webhook consumers get `inquiry.expired`, never `inquiry.completed` or `inquiry.failed`, for a redacted open inquiry. This is a recorded change to Phase 1-5 behaviour; no Phase 1-5 test asserts a redacted inquiry's later status.
   - **Tests.**
     - A unit test of the guard.
     - A Postgres race test (Testcontainers) that redacts while a settlement waits, in both orders. Redaction first leaves the inquiry pending and redacted, with no `inquiry.completed`, `inquiry.failed` or `inquiry.transitioned` (the redaction's own `inquiry.redacted` and `inquiry-session.canceled` are expected). Settlement first leaves it completed, then redacted. Its RED runs without the guard.
     - The sweep with `DEFAULT_SWEEP_LIMIT + 1` redacted overdue inquiries beside ordinary ones expires all of them in one run.
     - In `apps/api/src/services/reports/completion.test.ts` (a borrowed edit), the redacted case asserts that the `pending → completed` move returns null. A case through `failed → approved` keeps the hook's own guard tested.
6. **The C78.7 gate draws the calibration's list, by construction** (`platform-6d`; calibration-6c item 18).
   - **The shared module.** `platform-6d` moves the 100,000-name table's definition into a typed `packages/watchlist/src/testing/heavy-headed.ts`, exported by `@openverify/watchlist/testing`. It is add-only, and existing exports keep their output. It holds:
     - the pool from the raw `rigour_person_names` groups plus 40,000 fictional names;
     - a Zipf exponent of 1;
     - `TABLE_PROFILES.sanctions`;
     - 100,000 names, with 150 listed and 150 unlisted subjects.
   - **The gate builds from it.** It parses the raw file outside `held-out/`, for example with a `parseRigourPersonNames`, with a test that its items equal the benchmark's `groupItem` output. The gate never uses the held-out generator (decision 2 after 6B).
   - **In Stage 2, the benchmark follows.** `calibration-6d` makes `benchmark-tables.mjs` and `benchmark-performance.mjs` take the pool, entries and queries from that module, by relative path. `benchmark-golden.test.mjs`'s table digests stay unchanged, which proves the benchmark's output did not move. Nothing is copied, so no pin test is needed.
   - **The base overlay.** The base takes `heavy-headed.ts` with the head's `benchmark-*`.
   - **The header.** The gate's header says the calibration report is the authority for the budgets (the gate runs one round; the report takes the median of three), and it names the case it ran. The machine-independent assertions stay as decision 2 after 6B set them.
7. **The bootstrap interval (review C002).** The list under test is fixed: a match to any listed entry counts in every replicate. `pairedSamples` (`benchmark-paired.mjs`) and `bootstrapQuality` (`benchmark-metrics.mjs`) change together, test-first and before the seed (`calibration-6d`).
   - Recall and precision resample the listed identities. A drawn case counts its hit and every other match it kept, once per draw.
   - The false-alert rate resamples the customers. A drawn customer alerts when it alerted against the whole list.
   - The two draws are independent and shared across presets and trees, so every difference stays paired.
   - **Test:** a replicate that draws every unit once reproduces `pairedPoint` and `qualityPoint` exactly, for every metric, on a set built like 6C's company case. The C002 mark, for an interval that excludes its point, stays as a flag. A percentile interval of a ratio need not contain its point, so containment is not the test.
   - **Existing tests:** the golden `bootstrap` and `intervals` digests are re-pinned, with a test that the old and new definitions agree when no case or customer matches another resampled identity.
   - The 6C report's marked company intervals stay as published. 6D's report gives them under this definition, from 6D's observations.
8. **6D's calibration measures performance, and checks quality is identical instead of re-deciding it** (`calibration-6d`). In 6D no C78.4 constant, rule, threshold, preset or cap changes, and every matcher change keeps the created matches identical.
   - **Identity at base and head**, keyed by query and preset (steps resume, so lines are not compared in order):
     - the kept entry ids, `fixed`, `limits` and `errors`;
     - a SHA-256 of the full created matches (entry key, score, match types, qualifiers, alias type, list name, name score, methods, lossy).

     `calibration-6d` adds the digest to the observation lines test-first, before the seed; the base takes it through the overlay. The sets are the scale population at both scales and the 6B variant set on its own 2,600-entry list.
   - **Runs.** After each `rescreen-<n>`, the inserted `list_update` runs are executed with nothing persisted, at both trees. Every forward-screen hit must be kept by its run, which tests C004 at scale.
   - **Measured,** under 6C's timing rule (the e2e lock, loads of at most 4 at the start and end, three takes, the median):
     - C78.7 at both scales against decision 1's budgets, with the base's figures beside the head's;
     - the cap and `result_truncated`;
     - the re-screen at 100, 300 and 1,000 changed entries and 200,000 subjects, with `rescreen-check` and each size's run cost;
     - the gate three times with the timing asserted;
     - quality at the head under decision 7's intervals.
   - **Shares.** Each switchable change's share is measured at the scale it targets, switching it off and on alternately within one lock hold, three takes each, as the median difference with its range.
   - **The seed** for the tables is fresh, drawn after the method is committed.
   - **The verdict** per change is `kept` when the digests are identical and its p95 is not worse by more than a margin fixed in the method commit, at either scale; `revert` otherwise. Each budget is `met` or `missed`, with its gap.
9. **Carried, each to its place:**
   - **Company entities in the tables** (calibration-6c item 15): to the next quality calibration, which any change to a C78.4 constant triggers. Phase 7's fuzzy name lists reuse the matcher, so a constant change there brings them in.
   - **Write cost** (matcher-6c's optional items: an incremental key-frequency rebuild, 184 s a sync at 2,000,000 names; the re-key's batch size, 2,414 s; GIN pending lists during a long sync): to Phase 11's performance hardening.
   - **JIT off for the whole application:** a recommendation for Phase 11's self-hosting guide (`ALTER ROLE ... SET jit = off` for the application's role). It is not a pool option, because PgBouncer in its default configuration refuses a connection-level `options` startup parameter.
   - **"Other lists" in the console:** an `ui-reports.spec.ts` check on a synthetic OFAC Consolidated entry. It needs such an entry in the sandbox list, so it goes to Phase 9.
   - **Coverage words:** the reports index and history cells that read "Complete" without decision 6's sentence, to Phase 9.
   - **Phase 11, already there:** HTTP conditional requests (ETag, Last-Modified), whose 200 is still compared by SHA-256, and the UN opt-in acknowledgement (decisions 10 and 21 after 6B).
   - **The unexplained integration failure** (api-reports-6c item 12): every validation keeps its logs. If it recurs, it is fixed test-first from that log.
10. **CI minutes** (owner, 2026-10-28).
    - **The cost.** A full CI run bills about 100 GitHub Actions minutes: 13 jobs, each rounded up to the minute.
    - **The cadence.** Main is pushed once per Workflow, after its full local validation is green, and never for a single fix. There are no workflow_dispatch check branches and no pull requests, and only failed jobs are re-run.
    - **Not waiting on CI** (owner, 2026-10-29). After a push, the next Workflow starts at once, and CI is read when it finishes. A CI fix is proven locally and goes with the next push.
    - **October.** October's one remaining run is the push after 6C (origin/main was `94ab63f9`), so 6D's "on GitHub" falls after the minutes reset in November.

### Review of this addendum (2026-10-29)

An adversarial review of the draft, made against the code, the plan and 6C's full result, returned 16 findings: 3 HIGH, 7 MEDIUM and 6 LOW. Each was checked and applied above.
- **HIGH:**
  - The draft guarded every move out of `created` and `pending`. That blocked expiry: a redacted overdue inquiry would stay selected by the install-wide sweep, and 500 of them would stop every expiry. Decision 5 now guards only moves to `pending`, `completed` and `failed`.
  - The draft pinned the gate's parameters to the benchmark's with a test that 6D's ownership could not build. The benchmark's table also draws from the held-out parser. Decision 6 now defines the table once, in `src/testing/heavy-headed.ts`.
  - The draft moved the 2,000,000-name budget to 500 ms after 6C had measured 399.4 ms. That is moving it to fit, and one of its reasons was wrong: 319.9 ms does not reach 250 ms. Decision 1 now keeps every budget.
- **MEDIUM:**
  - Decision 4's mechanisms could not reach 60 s alone: its cost model and both its parts are now in scope.
  - C004 no longer uses a frequency prefilter, which could break the no-miss rule, nor a borrowed edit in `watchlist-reads.ts`.
  - Decision 7's precision and `bootstrapQuality` are corrected, and the containment test is replaced by the full-draw test.
  - Decision 8 gains the digest of full matches, executed runs and per-scale shares.
  - The grouping key is complete, and the test-time item is done.
  - Ownership is completed: `screening.ts`, the call-site tests, `keys/`, `listChangedWatchlistEntries`, migration 0022, and the overlay rule.
  - `fix-6d` re-measures what its fixes touch.
- **LOW:** the 45 s intercept; where the gap is carried and the acceptance wording; the code cause of truncation, recorded as a decision; performance constants; the frequency wording; and the stage's operations (imports and lock waits).

## Workflow 6D (Phase 6 closure)

**Agents (8):**
- **Stage 1** (in parallel): `matcher-6d` (decision 3), `rescreen-6d` (decision 4) and `platform-6d` (decisions 5 and 6).
- **Stage 2:** `calibration-6d` (decisions 6's benchmark half, 7 and 8), alone, so every timing runs on a machine no other agent loads.
- **Review:** two read-only reviewers: matching and the re-screen; the inquiry guard, the gate and the calibration.
- **Verify:** one adversarial verifier for every CRITICAL or HIGH finding, who also records the 6D Stage 1 and 2 amendments.
- **Fix:** `fix-6d` over every path. It re-measures, with `calibration-6d`'s commands, seed and timing rule, every figure a fix touches, and records it in `docs/calibration/watchlist-phase6d.md`.

**Ownership** (each list includes the test files beside it and the agent's `docs/tdd/phase6-<agent>.tdd.md`):
- **`matcher-6d`:**
  - `packages/watchlist/src/{names,translit,keys,score,match,crypto}/`;
  - `packages/db/src/repositories/watchlist-reads.ts` and `watchlist-rows.ts`;
  - `apps/api/src/jobs/report-types/{watchlist,business-watchlist,crypto-address-watchlist,politically-exposed-person}.ts`, only where a matcher change needs its call site, with the new `watchlist-screening.test.ts`;
  - `docs/reports/watchlist.md` and `politically-exposed-person.md` (decision 3's note).
- **`rescreen-6d`:**
  - `packages/watchlist/src/reverse-index/`;
  - `apps/api/src/jobs/reports/rescreen.ts` and `screening.ts`;
  - `packages/db/src/repositories/watchlist-key-df.ts` and `report-rescreen.ts`;
  - `apps/api/src/test-support/fakes.ts` with `watchlists-fake.test.ts`;
  - `apps/api/src/jobs/report-types/matching-reports.test.ts` and `matching-reports-db.test.ts`;
  - borrowed edits: output-identical ones in `keys/` under a golden test, and JIT in `listChangedWatchlistEntries` (`watchlists.ts`);
  - the only migration, if one is needed: `0022_*` in `packages/db/drizzle/` with its `meta` entry, and its table definitions in `schema.ts` as a borrowed edit.
- **`platform-6d`:**
  - `packages/db/src/repositories/inquiries.ts`;
  - the `apps/api/src/services/` and `apps/api/src/jobs/` files that move an inquiry to `pending`, `completed` or `failed`, only for decision 5;
  - the expiry sweep's test;
  - a new race test under `apps/api/src/services/`;
  - `apps/api/src/services/reports/completion.test.ts` (a borrowed edit);
  - `packages/watchlist/src/testing/heavy-headed.ts` (new) with its test, and its add-only export from `packages/watchlist/src/testing/index.ts`;
  - `apps/api/src/watchlists/test-support/` and `watchlists.integration.test.ts`.
- **`calibration-6d`:**
  - `packages/watchlist/scripts/benchmark-*`: decision 6's switch to the shared table; decision 7; decision 8's digest and run execution; a defect that stops a step; a switch to measure a share;
  - `docs/calibration/watchlist-phase6d.md`.

**Frozen in 6D:**
- `apps/api/src/{container,worker,config}.ts` and `apps/api/src/test-support/harness.ts`;
- `packages/db/src/schema.ts`, except a migration's borrowed edit;
- `packages/db/src/repositories/index.ts` and `watchlists.test-support.ts`;
- `packages/core/src/report-matching.ts`;
- every file that no list names.

**What the benchmark imports** keeps its names and signatures: `syncWatchlistSource`, `useScreeningKit`, `DB_WATCHLIST_READS`, `processReportRescreen`, `screenOnce` and the `@openverify/db/testing` helpers. An agent that must change one fixes the benchmark's call site in the same commit, so the head's `benchmark-*` overlaid on the base still runs.

**Borrowed edits** follow 6C: wait until `git status --porcelain <file>` is empty, make the minimal change, commit it at once, and list it in `contractDeviations`.

**Timings** run under `flock /tmp/openverify-e2e.lock`, with the load average beside each one.
- A 2,000,000-name development import (about an hour) runs in `--parts` of under 10 minutes, each part under the lock, so no timing runs beside it.
- Expect up to about 8 minutes of lock wait per gate call.
- Stage 1's development numbers are context; the calibration's decide.

**Calibration worktrees** follow 6C. The base is at 6D's launch commit and the head at Stage 1's head. The base carries the head's `packages/watchlist/scripts/benchmark-*` and `packages/watchlist/src/testing/heavy-headed.ts`, overlaid uncommitted. The variant groups are built with each tree's own script. Held-out data and seeds' output stay outside git.

**Acceptance for 6D:**
- `docs/calibration/watchlist-phase6d.md` publishes, at 6D's base and head:
  - C78.7 at both scales against decision 1's budgets;
  - the re-screen at 100, 300 and 1,000 changed entries with its run cost;
  - each change's share and verdict;
  - identical match digests;
  - every forward-screen hit kept by its executed run.
- A redacted open inquiry never completes or fails, with the race test in both orders. It expires at its `expires-at`, and the expiry sweep is never held by redacted inquiries.
- A `list_update` run holds at most one page of changed entries, and its result equals the whole-set screening's.
- The gate and the benchmark draw the 100,000-name table from one module, with the benchmark's golden digests unchanged.
- A replicate that draws every unit once reproduces every point estimate.
- Every decision above is done or carried with its reason.
- Every validation command passes locally, and on GitHub after the November reset.
- The Phase 6 acceptance line on C78.7 is then met, or its gap recorded under decision 1.

## Workflow 6D Stage 1 and 2 amendments

Recorded by the Workflow 6D verifier on 2026-10-30. The source is the `contractDeviations` of the three Stage 1 agents and of `calibration-6d`, plus the calibration's verdicts. The evidence is in `docs/tdd/phase6-{matcher,rescreen,platform,calibration}-6d.tdd.md` and `docs/calibration/watchlist-phase6d.md`. Each item binds `fix-6d` and every later phase. An item that names a follow-up gives it to `fix-6d`, unless it names another owner.

### matcher-6d

1. **The 2,000,000-name budget was missed on development tables, and the calibration decides.** On `matcher-6d`'s development seed (`4c6eb06e18c08fe1161ec09c048d822f`) the head measured 326.6 ms p95 (median of 326.6, 275.4 and 332.7 at loads 2.0 to 3.7) against 250 ms, from the base's 515.5 ms. The 100,000-name budget was met there (63.2 ms against the base's 97.8 ms). These are context only; `calibration-6d`'s figures are the verdict.
2. **`token-memo` is removed, not measured** (`1d1478d7`). It saved no time at either development scale (100,000 names: 2,081 and 2,104 ms a round with it against 1,937 to 2,010 ms without; 2,000,000 names: 24.1 and 25.4 s CPU against 24.6 and 24.0 s). Its row in `shortcuts.test.ts` and its guard in `name-score-bound.test.ts` went with it. `token-pairs`, keyed by the shared token objects rather than by content strings, takes its place. A benchmark call that names `token-memo` fails on the unknown switch.
3. **Two refactors carry no switch.** The analysed-name cache keyed without JSON (`48de21ca`) and the unit refactor (`a84158cf`: variant groups read without temporary lists, the content key kept on the unit, one cache slot per name) produce the same output and count the same work in different code. Their share is part of the base-to-head difference; `a84158cf` was also compared over 1,146,158 forms.
4. **Levers not built, because the profile does not support them.** Namesake grouping: at 2,000,000 names 4 of 87,926 candidates share every field `screenEntry`, qualification and the PEP admission read (14 of 51,988 at 100,000). `SET LOCAL jit = off` in `loadEntries`: EXPLAIN ANALYZE at 2,000,000 names shows no JIT (plan cost about 1,380). `ANALYSED_NAME_CACHE_SIZE` stays 50,000 (200,000 gave 283.4 against 286.7 ms for four times the memory).
5. **`scoreNamePairAbove` may return null when the bound passes** (`ratio-check`). When a relaxation of Dinkelbach's parametric form proves no alignment reaches the floor, the pair is refused. This is within its documented contract ("null when it could not be returned"). Its only caller, `bestPair`, treats null and a below-floor score alike, which is sound because the display floor equals the qualifier floor (0.70), `createdFloor` already subtracts the best qualifier, and an entry returned by identifier or weak alias is scored again in full. `PairMemo` gains an optional `below`.
6. **Two of the agent's own new tests were corrected after RED (accepted).** The guard test "gives every pair the same bound decision and score with %s off" (RED `e7de5d50`) compared raw answers; in GREEN (`95251b36`) it checks the property the screening relies on: every pair returned with the switch on has the same score with it off, and every pair refused scores below the floor with it off. The `token-pairs` row added at RED (`ffcb37a7`) failed for the wrong reason (the seeded list shares no token); `e53a50fa` replaces it with names that share tokens, which fails on the stub. No earlier test was changed.
7. **No call site changed.** No report type's call site needed a change, so `apps/api/src/jobs/report-types/watchlist-screening.test.ts` was not created. No migration and no borrowed edit was made.
8. **Interface changes (binding for later callers).** `ScoreAboveOptions.tokens` is removed (only `screen.ts` used it). `scorerWork()` gains `matrixMaps` and `ratioExits`; `analysedNameCacheWork()` and `nameTokenWork()` are new test counters. The new switches are `name-lru`, `token-cache` (up to `NAME_TOKEN_CACHE_SIZE` 100,000 forms), `indexed-matrix`, `ratio-check` and `token-pairs`, all on by default.

### rescreen-6d

1. **No JIT setting was added (decision 4).** EXPLAIN (ANALYZE) of every read the re-screen makes, at 100,000 and 2,000,000 names, shows no plan above `jit_above_cost` (100,000) and no JIT block; the highest is 81,208, a changed-entry page of a 51,592-entry sync. `report-rescreen.ts` and `listChangedWatchlistEntries` are therefore unchanged, and no borrowed edit was made in `watchlists.ts`. A sync of about 65,000 or more changed entries would cross the threshold there; see item 9.
2. **What "one consistent set" of key frequencies means.** A source with at most `RESCREEN_FREQUENCY_TABLE_ROWS` (500,000) `watchlist_key_df` rows is read whole, in one statement, at the re-screen's first need, and reused for every chunk and environment. A larger source (the 2,000,000-name table holds 872,899 rows) is read key by key, each key once at its first need. The re-screen takes no source lock, so on that path a sync committing between two reads may give later-read keys the new counts: each key keeps one value for the whole re-screen, but the set may mix two syncs. This is accepted, because the later sync's own re-screen keys every subject with its frequencies, and a `list_update` run screens its trigger's changes with no frequencies at all. `RESCREEN_FREQUENCY_TABLE_ROWS` is a performance constant, not a C78.4 matching constant.
3. **Additive interface changes.** `RecurringReportSubject.templateVersionId` (selected by `listRecurringReportSubjects`; the one existing expectation in `report-rescreen.test.ts` gained the member, its only change); optional `ReportRescreenDeps.subjectsPerChunk` and `frequencyTableRows` (test seams, not benchmark flags); optional `ReportRescreenResult.candidatePairs`, `screenings` and `phases` (`RescreenPhases`); a new db export `watchlistKeyFrequencyTable`. `WatchlistReads` gains no member, and nothing the benchmark imports changed signature.
4. **The borrowed edit in `keys/name-keys.ts`** (`df572f64`): `nameTokenKeys` keeps a one-slot memo by name and groups identity, `tokenGroups` looks each distinct form up once, and `queryNameKeys` sorts once. Its output is identical under the golden digest of 832 names and 60 multi-name calls pinned at `9882aee3`, and under the reverse-index digest. It was committed at once on a clean file.
5. **The fake's page order.** In `apps/api/src/test-support/fakes.ts`, a `changedInSync` load without ids returns its page in entry id order, as the database does; a load with ids is unchanged. The page-wise run relies on that order.
6. **C004's test fixture was corrected.** Its first version gave both lists the same trigger sync, which no real sync does (a sync is of one source), so its whole-set reference read some entries twice. Each list now has its own sync, and the corrected test is RED on the base's `screening.ts` for the intended reason.
7. **The name-bound check's cutoff is the display floor (0.70)**, which no preset's minimum is below. A candidate with one of the subject's identifiers, an entry of a kind the subject does not meet by name, or a weak alias under `screen-weak-aliases` is always screened. No C78.4 constant, threshold, preset or cap changed.
8. **Measurement scope (context only).** The base was taken once per size; its 100-entry take ran at loads of 15 to 11 and overstates it. The head was taken at `42895e6a`, so its screening p50 (17.3 ms) and screen phase include `matcher-6d`'s commits up to then. `rescreen-check` ran at 1,000 entries only. The development median at 1,000 entries, 64.5 s against 60 s (base 112.4 s, quietest take 54.6 s), is superseded by `calibration-6d`'s measurement.
9. **Follow-ups named by the profile, not built in 6D.** (a) A token-pair memo passed to `nameScoreUpperBound` by the re-screen's name-bound check (827,914 pairs, 11.7 s CPU at 1,000 entries), test-first with every bound and created match identical. (b) Migration 0022: an index on `watchlist_entries (source_id, changed_sync_id, (entry_id collate "C")) where removed_at is null`, so `listChangedWatchlistEntries` reads a page by index range instead of top-N sorting the whole changed set (697 ms a page, about 36 s a run, after a 51,592-entry sync at 2,000,000 names), with a Postgres plan test. (c) The fixed part of about 25 to 28 s (subjects' read and decryption, specs, `prepareReverseIndex`, build): worker threads per chunk or a prepared index kept between re-screens, under the no-miss check. (a) and (c) are carried to Phase 11's performance hardening. (b) goes to `fix-6d`, test-first; if `fix-6d` does not build it, it is carried to Phase 11 with (a) and (c).

### platform-6d

1. **Phase 3's G002 is amended (decision 5).** A redacted inquiry's verification still fails without data, but a redacted open inquiry is no longer settled: it stays open until it expires, and webhook consumers get `inquiry.expired`, never `inquiry.completed` or `inquiry.failed`, for it. No Phase 1-5 test asserted a redacted inquiry's later status. `fix-6d` adds a note to G002 in `docs/plans/phase-3.plan.md` saying so.
2. **Every caller handles a null transition without a code change.** `transitionInquiryInTx` returns null before staging any event, session cancellation or report hook. `settleInquiry` (also reached from `failVerificationAfterError` and `processVerification`) re-reads and returns the inquiry's status with nothing staged, and its second move never runs. `startInquiry` returns the inquiry as it is. `cancelAtCaptureLimit` and `cancelAtChallengeLimit` push nothing (they run after `assertInquiryOpenUnderLock`, which already answers 410 to a redacted inquiry). `applyInquiryAction` throws `invalidTransition`, so the sandbox answers 409 `invalid-transition` and rolls back. `runInquiryExpiry`, the `expire` action and `persistRunResult` stay unguarded; `createInquiryReportsInTx` keeps its own `redactedAt` guard. The caller table in the TDD file is the reference.
3. **The expiry-sweep test is a regression guard, not a RED step (accepted).** It passes without the guard because the guard never touches expiry; it fails on a draft that also guarded `expired`. Its 501 redacted rows are stamped `redacted_at` with one SQL statement, never through `fields` (which breaks the sealed column); the other decision 5 tests use the real redaction.
4. **Test files.** `apps/api/src/services/reports/completion.test.ts` (borrowed edit): the redacted case asserts that `pending → completed` returns null, no report exists and the inquiry stays pending; a new case approves a redacted failed inquiry, keeping the hook's own guard tested. `apps/api/src/services/simulate.test.ts` is new; the routes and OpenAPI are unchanged.
5. **The gate's seed is the 6C evaluation seed** (`e8223c34916200e2b8e51f0b47981804`, `GATE_SEED`), so the gate screens the table that `docs/calibration/watchlist-phase6c.md` measured and its machine-independent figures reproduce that report's (candidates 99/630, the cap 0/300, out of reach 1/300, 150/150 found). A fresh seed tried first (`abde99306e4f5542e2fc330093a2758a`) bound the cap in 11 of 300 against matcher-6c's limit of 6; the limit was not loosened. The owner question this raises is answered by `calibration-6d`'s finding 1 (below).
6. **The gate runs one round after 20 warm-ups** (the benchmark's `100k-measure --rounds 1`), where it pooled three before. Its header names the calibration report's median of three as the authority for the budgets. Its timing is asserted only under `OPENVERIFY_C787_TIMING=assert`.
7. **The gate generator's old tests moved with the generator.** `heavy-headed-list.test.ts`'s tests of `zipfSampler`, `heavyHeadedPool`, `heavyHeadedList`, `gateSubjects` and `groupSpellings` were replaced by equivalent tests of the shared module in `packages/watchlist/src/testing/heavy-headed.test.ts`. The gate's machine-independent assertions are unchanged, with one added: the gate has 300 subjects.
8. **`writeNormalisedImport` stays in `benchmark-tables.mjs`**, because it writes a file and is not the table's definition. `heavy-headed.ts` imports nothing from `held-out/`; only its test uses the held-out `parseVariantGroups`, as the reference.
9. **Stage 1's gate take is context.** One asserted take at `42895e6a` gave p95 102.6 ms (load 2.38 to 4.80), 2.6 ms over; `calibration-6d`'s three asserted takes decide (below: passes).

### calibration-6d

**What was measured.** The seed is `9dd9b3530f47966b9f1b88e78445c08f`, drawn at 2026-10-29T23:30:18Z after the method commit `5c1420ca`. The base is `9882aee3` with the head's `benchmark-*` and `heavy-headed.ts` overlaid; the head is Stage 1's code (`60483a3b`), run from `5c1420ca`. No benchmark code changed after the seed.

**Deviations, binding as recorded:**
1. **The identity helpers live in `benchmark-scale.mjs`.** The stub `scripts/benchmark-identity.mjs` (`f271aed7`) was folded into it in GREEN (`3d1a852b`), because `benchmark-gates.test.mjs` requires every pure helper to be in `packages/watchlist/tsconfig.json`'s include, which 6D froze. The tests stay in `benchmark-identity.test.mjs`. `tsconfig.json` needs no change.
2. **What the match digest covers (decision 8).** It is the SHA-256 of the canonical JSON (keys sorted, arrays in order) of every member a run returns for each created match: the entry key, score, content and risk-data hashes, matched name, the snapshot's entity with its alias type, match types, qualifiers, and the components (list name, name level, methods, preset, min-score). The scorer's raw `nameScore` and `lossy` are not in a run's result, so the score and the components stand for them. Because the components carry the preset, one match digests differently at each preset; identity is therefore compared per (query, preset).
3. **Decision 7's existing tests changed with the definition.** "Counts a false match only when the replicate drew the entry it points at" became "counts every match a drawn case kept, once per draw". "Alerts a customer only when the replicate drew an entry it matched, and resamples customers" became two tests: a drawn customer alerts as it alerted against the whole list, and customers are resampled. The C002 note now calls the mark a flag. The golden `bootstrap` digest is re-pinned (`ec8e95ff…` → `74c127e2…`); the golden `intervals` digest does not move under decision 7 and is not re-pinned.
4. **Decision 8's steps go beyond the plan's list (accepted).** `benchmark:performance --step identity` compares each scale's first measure round subject by subject. Executed-run lines and the runs summary carry a subject digest, so two trees' run sets compare without report ids. `rescreen-check` ran at 1,000 entries at the base too. `screenOnce` takes an optional `run: { runType, triggerSyncId }`.
5. **The share method as built.** A priming round with every switch on (not recorded), then rounds with the switch off and on alternately; before each round the analysed-name and token caches are emptied and the 20 warm-ups run again, and each round's digests are compared with the priming round's. At 100,000 names `name-lru` and `array-parameters` broke the load rule in their three-take calls and were retaken as three single-take calls (`--takes 1`), each still off and on within one lock hold.
6. **The head worktree is pinned to `5c1420ca`, not `60483a3b`.** Between them only `docs/` and `packages/watchlist/scripts/benchmark-*` differ, so the head runs Stage 1's code, and both trees ran byte-identical benchmark files (checked with `sha256sum -c`).
7. **Open points fixed in the method before the seed.** The margin is 5% of the reference p95 (the switch off for a share, the base for an unswitchable change). Every switch is measured at both scales, because the verdict reads both. A size's run cost is runs × that tree's pooled 100,000-name screening p50. `rescreen-6d`'s frequency-table read has a test seam but no switch, so its share is bounded by the record's `frequencies` phase (0.1 s), not measured.
8. **Wording correction.** Decision 8's "the 6B variant set on its own 2,600-entry list" is read as the 6B list of 3,600 entries: 2,000 persons, 600 companies and the 1,000 group cases' entries. 2,600 is the count of listed persons and companies.
9. **Non-timing steps ran side by side (accepted).** The quality steps, the 2,000,000-name scale screening, parts 1 to 4 of both 2,000,000-name imports, and the 6 workers of `rescreen-check` and `rescreen-runs` ran at both trees at once in one lock hold. No timing ran beside them.
10. **The two identity outputs get distinct names.** Both CLIs write `identity.json` into their `--out`; `fix-6d` renames them `quality-identity.json` and `performance-identity.json`, test-first.

**Verdicts (binding).** No commit was reverted.

**What the verdicts leave open** (binding as decided here; no budget moves):
11. **The three missed budgets are final for Phase 6** under the stopping rule of decision 1 after 6C. PROGRESS records each as an open line of the Phase 6 acceptance with its gap and profile, and each is carried to Phase 11's performance hardening (and PLAN §6 Phase 11): (a) retrieval at 2,000,000 names, the cap-reaching and out-of-reach pair lookups, now the p95; (b) list-name analysis stored at sync time, the lever named by `matcher-6d` (a schema change); (c) the re-screen's fixed part and screen phase, with `rescreen-6d`'s levers (rescreen-6d item 9).
12. **The C78.7 figures depend on the seed's draw** (finding 1). On this seed the cap binds in 25 of 300 searches and the head measures 180.9 ms at 100,000 names; on the 6C seed's table it binds in none and the gate measures 65.0 ms. The gate's limit "the cap binds in at most 6 of 300" is a property of the published 6C seed's table, not of the matcher, and the gate keeps that seed (platform-6d item 5). Whether a later calibration judges C78.7 over several seeds, with the spread or a percentile over seeds, is for the owner at Phase 11.
13. **`result_truncated` at `tolerant` exceeds the 1% target on this draw**: 2.55% at 100,000 names and 1.80% at 2,000,000 on the scale population, identical at base and head (retrieval is unchanged in 6D). It is carried with item 11(a); a truncated screening is still reported as truncated, never as no match (C78.5).

## Workflow 6D review amendments

Recorded by the Workflow 6D fixer (`fix-6d`) on 2026-10-30, for its own changes, as plan owner. The review raised no blocking finding and no MEDIUM item, and the calibration reverted nothing, so `fix-6d` built the builders' follow-ups addressed to it that are local. The evidence is in `docs/tdd/phase6-review-fixes-6d.tdd.md`, and the re-measurement in `docs/calibration/watchlist-phase6d.md`. Each item is binding for later phases.

1. **The identity steps write distinct files (calibration-6d deviation 10, done).** `benchmark:quality --step identity` writes `<out>/quality-identity.json` and `benchmark:performance --step identity` writes `<out>/performance-identity.json`, so one `--out` holds both; the records are unchanged. `benchmark-identity.test.mjs` runs both CLIs as their package scripts do into one directory (`7c615bf3`, `15158303`). The calibration's runs, made before, wrote `identity.json`; its finding 6 says so.
2. **Migration 0022 (rescreen-6d item 9(b), done; amends C74.4's read and C73.3's indexes).** `watchlist_entries_changed_page_idx` on `watchlist_entries (source_id, changed_sync_id, entry_id collate "C") where removed_at is null`, defined in `packages/db/src/tables/watchlists.ts` (the table module `schema.ts` re-exports) and generated by `drizzle-kit` as `0022_watchlist_changed_page_index` with its snapshot and journal entry (`7eccf9d0`). `listChangedWatchlistEntries` is unchanged; its page is now an index range with no sort. The existing `(source_id, changed_sync_id)` index stays. Tests: a Postgres 17 plan test (`packages/db/src/watchlist-query-plans.integration.test.ts`: 60,000 of 180,000 entries changed by one sync, both pages use the index with no `Sort`, and paging returns the 58,379 live entries once each in "C" order), the migration's content (`migrate.test.ts`) and the catalog's definition (`phase6-tables.test.ts`) (RED `2bba8855`).
3. **The re-screen was re-measured after item 2, and every 6D verdict stands.** With the calibration's commands, seed and timing rule at `7eccf9d0` (three takes on fresh databases, loads 1.6 to 2.3): 30.5 s at 100 added entries, 36.6 s at 300 and 71.2 s at 1,000 (medians), with the calibration's runs (626, 213, 2,676), sync files and pair counts in every take. The changed-entry read is 82 to 87 ms at 1,000 entries with or without the index, so these sizes cannot show it; its effect is at a large sync (697 ms a page after a 51,592-entry sync at 2,000,000 names, per rescreen-6d). The 60 s budget is still missed (+11.2 s) and stays carried (item 11 of the Stage 1 and 2 amendments); small syncs stay met (42.5 s and 40.7 s against the base's 88.6 s and 90.8 s).
4. **Phase 3's G002 carries a note (platform-6d item 1, done).** `docs/plans/phase-3.plan.md`, Workflow 3B review item 3: after owner decision 5 after Workflow 6C, a redacted open inquiry is never settled; it stays open until it expires at its `expires-at`, and webhook consumers get `inquiry.expired`, never `inquiry.completed` or `inquiry.failed`, for it (`7748c524`).
5. **Carried, with the reason** (each to its owner):
   - **The token-pair memo for `nameScoreUpperBound`** (rescreen-6d item 9(a)): to Phase 11's performance hardening, as item 11(c) of the Stage 1 and 2 amendments already carries it. It changes the matcher's bound after the calibration, so it needs the equivalence test at every preset and a full re-measurement of the re-screen, which belong with the other re-screen levers there.
   - **The re-screen's fixed part** (about 23 to 25 s at 200,000 subjects: the subjects' read and decryption, specs, `prepareReverseIndex`, build; rescreen-6d item 9(c)) and **list-name analysis stored at sync time** (matcher-6d): to Phase 11, unchanged.
   - **The calibration's re-screen section citing `result.phases`** (rescreen-6d, "if the owner wants it"): the published section is left as measured; the re-measurement of item 3 cites each record's own `result.phases`, with no scratch timer. Rewriting the published section is the owner's choice.
   - **The owner's items of the calibration** (Stage 1 and 2 amendments items 11 to 13): the three missed budgets as open lines of the Phase 6 acceptance in PROGRESS, C78.7 over several seeds, and `result_truncated` above 1% at `tolerant` on this draw, to the owner and Phase 11.
   - **No change needed:** `packages/watchlist/tsconfig.json` (calibration-6d deviation 1), and `apps/api/src/jobs/reports/screening.ts` (matcher-6d: no lever there was needed for decision 3).
